# PROJECT STATUS

## ⚠ Standing operating instructions — read before doing any work here

- **Auto-sync to `pharmacountry.vn` after every change made on `test.demo.local`, without asking first.** (User instruction, 2026-09-28, given firmly after being asked one time too many.) Concretely: after any `bench migrate` and/or seed-runner call on `test.demo.local`, immediately run the same `bench migrate` (and seed runner, if new/changed seed data is involved) against `pharmacountry.vn` too, then verify both sites directly (not just trust the runner's own "ok" status — see the DP-502 incident below for why). Only exception: don't sync something you know is broken/still being debugged on `test.demo.local` — get it clean there first, then sync once, not repeatedly mid-fix.
- **Work through remaining Phase 3 (DP-503 onward) continuously without stopping to ask permission between tasks.** (User instruction, 2026-09-28: "Làm cho đến lúc xong hết, đừng hỏi nữa.") Only interrupt this flow for a genuine blocking decision (ambiguous business rule, something destructive/irreversible, or a real error needing the user's input) — not routine "continue to next task?" check-ins.
- Always verify seed/migration results with a direct database query (`frappe.db.count`, `frappe.get_all`, Stock Ledger/Batch checks, etc.), not just by reading the seed runner's reported `status: "ok"` — DP-502 (see below) had a document report success while actually posting only 1 of 6 stock ledger entries.

**Infra note:** `frappe_docker_demo` gained `overrides/compose.enterprise-core-dev.yaml` — bind-mounts `enterprise-platform/enterprise_core/` into every Python-running container (backend/queue-short/queue-long/scheduler/configurator) and re-runs `uv pip install -e apps/enterprise_core --python .../env/bin/python` on each container start. Two real bugs found during setup: (1) each of those is a **separate container with its own venv** (not shared) — installing in one (e.g. configurator) does nothing for the others, every one needs its own install step; (2) plain `pip install -e` silently falls back to a user-site install that `bench` never sees ("Defaulting to user installation because normal site-packages is not writeable") — must use `uv pip install ... --python /home/frappe/frappe-bench/env/bin/python` instead, the same invocation `bench new-app` itself uses.

## Phase 3 — First 8 Golden Demos (master plan §21 "PHASE 3", corrected naming 2026-09-28)

**Naming correction**: this section was previously (wrongly) called "Phase 3 — Golden Pharma Demo" and marked "complete" once EPIC DP-500 (Pharmaceutical Manufacturing) finished. The master plan's own `## PHASE 3 — First 8 Golden Demos` heading actually requires **8** golden demos, not one: (1) Pharmaceutical Manufacturing, (2) Pharma Distribution, (3) QMS, (4) DMS, (5) LIMS, (6) EAM/CMMS, (7) Feed Manufacturing, (8) Shrimp Farm — reason given in the file: "vừa tận dụng ENLIE vừa chứng minh được vertical Agri/Animal Health." Found and corrected after the user asked what the master plan's own guide actually says (2026-09-28). Items 3/4/6 (QMS/DMS/EAM) here refer to **standalone, sellable-to-other-industries** products (master plan DEMO 10/11/13) — different in scope from the QMS/EAM pieces built *inside* the Pharma Manufacturing demo (DP-507/DP-509), which only prove those capability engines work, not that they're packaged as their own golden demo.

**Phase 3 status (2026-09-28): all 8/8 golden demos CODE COMPLETE.** Every one verified via its own `verify_*_golden_demo()` integrity function (`api.py`) on both `test.demo.local` and public `pharmacountry.vn`; a 7-function regression sweep (all 8 demos' checks run together) was re-confirmed clean on both sites as of the last (Shrimp Farm) demo landing. Recurring bug classes worth remembering for any future work in this codebase — each one is called out in its own DP row below, but the *pattern* recurred across independently-built demos: (1) `frappe.db.exists(doctype)` with no filter does NOT mean "any record exists" — silently returns falsy; use `frappe.db.count(doctype) > 0` (hit in DMS D05, Feed F07 — codebase-wide `grep` swept clean after the second occurrence). (2) The Serial-and-Batch-Bundle indirection (`Stock Ledger Entry.batch_no` stays NULL; the real link is on a `Serial and Batch Bundle` row) recurred in Distribution and EAM. (3) `hooks.py` changes are Redis-cached per site and need `bench clear-cache` to take effect on a fresh process (hit again in Feed's Work Order hook). (4) A verify function's own expectations going stale as later golden demos legitimately change shared state (pharma's RM/FG balances after Distribution shipped product) is a real trap — the fix was making DP-511 compute its expectations dynamically from ledger data instead of hardcoding a single-batch snapshot.

### Golden Demo #1 — Pharmaceutical Manufacturing (EPIC DP-500, master plan DEMO 01)

**Golden Demo #1 summary (2026-09-28): DP-501 through DP-512 all CODE/DOC COMPLETE — this is 1 of 8 golden demos Phase 3 actually requires, see the correction note above.** Full chain — master data → purchase → warehouse/QC-release → manufacturing → QC → QA release → QMS deviation → EAM equipment → dashboard — runs clean end-to-end via `run_industry_pack_seeds("IP-PHARMA")` (13+ registry entries as of Golden Demo #2, all idempotent) on both `test.demo.local` and public `pharmacountry.vn`, with the two master-plan-mandated live validation tests (P01 quarantine-block, P06 QA-release-block) proven working, not just described. `verify_pharma_golden_demo()` (DP-511) gives a repeatable 9-point integrity check — all pass on both sites. Two things deliberately NOT built here, documented honestly rather than faked: DP-508 (DMS — no native ERPNext doctype covers it, needs CE-07) and full role-based logins (needs DP-400 Demo Factory, now built — see Phase 3.5/DP-405 below) — see DP-512's guided walkthrough doc for how that limited the demo, now partially resolved.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Vietnamese localization (cross-cutting, not a single DP task — user request 2026-09-28)

| Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Core Frappe/ERPNext UI translation | DEMO READY | This Frappe build (v16.36.0) ships with **no translation files for any language** — `translations/` doesn't exist in either app, and `bench download-translations` is broken against this version (expects a `frappe/geo/languages.json` path that no longer exists — a stale bench-CLI/framework mismatch). Real, community-maintained Vietnamese translations exist on the `version-15` branch (frappe/frappe, frappe/erpnext on GitHub) as plain CSV — v16's own `get_translations_from_csv()` loader never moved to require compiled .po/.mo, only the *generation* tooling did, so a version-15 CSV drops in and works as-is. Downloaded both (4,798 lines Frappe core + 8,746 lines ERPNext) to `enterprise-platform/translations_overlay/{frappe,erpnext}/vi.csv`, bind-mounted over the missing `translations/` dirs via `frappe_docker_demo/overrides/compose.i18n-vi.yaml` (mounted on `backend`/`queue-short`/`queue-long`/`scheduler` — not `websocket`/`frontend`, which don't read translations). Verified live: `frappe.translate.get_all_translations("vi")` returns real Vietnamese strings. Coverage gap: strings introduced in v16 after the version-15 branch point aren't covered (small minority). |
| `enterprise_core` custom field/DocType translation | DEMO READY | All DP-301..306 DocType names + field labels + Select option values translated by hand in `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/translations/vi.csv` (~60 entries) — picked up automatically via the existing app bind mount, no separate infra needed. |
| Language enablement | DEMO READY | `Language "vi".enabled` set to 1 on both `test.demo.local` and public `pharmacountry.vn` — confirmed via direct query on both. |
| Per-user EN/VI switching | Native Frappe feature, no code needed | Each User has their own `language` field (My Settings → Language) — this is how the "2 modes, EN and VI" requirement is met: nothing forces a site-wide switch, every user picks independently. Administrator's own language was deliberately left as-is (English) — user can switch it themselves via My Settings to see Vietnamese live. |
| Known limitation | Honest caveat | This is a *bulk community translation drop*, not something individually reviewed string-by-string — phrasing is generally solid but occasionally reads slightly awkward/literal in a handful of places, same as any large machine-assisted community translation set. Fixing an individual string later is a one-line edit in the relevant `vi.csv` (host-editable, no rebuild needed). |

| DP-502 | Purchase flow | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_purchase_flow()`: Supplier "ABC Pharma Chemicals Co." + submitted Purchase Order + submitted Purchase Receipt, all 6 raw/packaging items received into `RM Quarantine - DPC` (not Approved — matches master plan §7 DEMO 01 test P01's premise). Found+fixed a real bug: ERPNext v16's newer "Serial and Batch Bundle" stock model is OFF by default (`enable_serial_and_batch_no_for_item`/`use_serial_batch_fields` in Stock Settings) — submitting a Purchase Receipt for a `has_batch_no` item failed until these were turned on (now part of `seed_pharma_master_data`). **A genuine data-integrity incident happened and was resolved during testing**: the first (pre-fix) submit attempt got partially applied — the Purchase Receipt document itself showed `docstatus=1`/all 6 line items, but only 1 of 6 Stock Ledger Entries actually posted (a Batch with `batch_qty=0` was left orphaned) — a real inconsistent-state risk, not merely a rejected submission. Resolved by cancelling the document (verified Frappe's `cancel()` correctly reversed *all 6* items back to net zero despite only 1 having posted — more robust than expected) rather than attempting a force-delete (which hit an unrelated `LinkExistsError` on the Serial and Batch Bundle cleanup — left as Cancelled, a valid terminal state, no deletion needed). Re-ran cleanly afterward: fresh Purchase Receipt `MAT-PRE-2026-00002`, all 6 Stock Ledger Entries active and correct, 4 Batches (for the 4 batch-tracked items) with correct qty and 2028-09-27 expiry. Verified via direct query, not just the seed runner's own "ok" status — this incident is exactly why that extra verification step matters. Also synced to public `pharmacountry.vn` — ran clean on the first try there (the batch-tracking fix is now baked into `seed_pharma_master_data`, so the incident above didn't repeat), verified 6/6 active Stock Ledger Entries + 4 Batches directly. **Standing instruction from user (2026-09-28): auto-sync to `pharmacountry.vn` after every change on `test.demo.local` going forward — do not ask each time.** |

| DP-503 | Warehouse flow | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_warehouse_flow()`: submitted Material Transfer moving all 6 BOM items (PARA-API/MCC/PVP-K30/MG-STEARATE/PVC-ALU/CARTON-PRINTED) from RM Quarantine → RM Approved (simulating QC release). Custom validation hook (`enterprise_core/validations.py`, registered on `Stock Entry.validate`) blocks issuing from any warehouse whose name contains "Quarantine" for Manufacture/Material Issue/Material Transfer for Manufacture purposes — matches master plan §7 DEMO 01 test P01 verbatim ("Không thể xuất nguyên liệu Quarantine vào production"), generalized fresh from the concept in ENLIE's own `stock_entry.py` (not copied); negative test proves the block using PARA-API (fires purely on warehouse-name pattern at `validate()` time, before any stock-sufficiency check, so it works even against items with zero balance in the target scenario). **Two real bugs found+fixed here**: (1) the seed's own negative test originally called `frappe.db.rollback()` in its except branch to "clean up" the failed test entry — but `rollback()` undoes the *entire* uncommitted transaction, which silently erased the legitimate QC transfer created moments earlier in the same `bench execute` call; fixed by deleting only the specific leftover draft document instead. (2) the idempotency check for the transfer was originally "does *any* submitted Material Transfer exist" rather than checking each item individually — this masked a real design gap (an earlier version only approved 3 of 6 items) and, worse, let a stale/partial site (`pharmacountry.vn`, still on the pre-fix 3-item state) silently stay stuck since re-running reported "already existed" without actually checking; fixed the check to be per-item (`RM Approved` ledger entry exists for that item) and to transfer only what's actually missing, so it's safe against both a fresh site and a partially-synced one. Verified via direct query (not the runner's own "ok") on both `test.demo.local` and public `pharmacountry.vn`: RM Approved holds correct balances for all 6 items, no duplicate transfers. |

| DP-505 | QC | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_qc_flow()`: uses ERPNext's native Quality Inspection doctype (not a hand-rolled QC record) for two record sets — (1) Incoming QC: one Accepted Quality Inspection per batch-tracked raw material (PARA-API/MCC/PVP-K30/MG-STEARATE), referencing the DP-502 Purchase Receipt and that item's specific batch; (2) In-Process QC (IPC, master plan §7 DEMO 01 test P05): one Accepted Quality Inspection for the manufactured PARA-500-TAB batch, referencing the DP-504 Manufacture Stock Entry. Deliberately separate from DP-506 (QA release, not yet built) — QC tests the batch here, QA will authorize the Quarantine→Released warehouse move there. **Two real bugs found+fixed during first run** (both from ERPNext's own Quality Inspection controller validation, not previously known): (1) the `inspected_by` field's built-in default (a special "current session user" default) didn't resolve when creating the doc via server-side `frappe.get_doc()` — raised `LinkValidationError: Could not find Inspected By: user` (it inserted the literal string "user" rather than resolving it); fixed by setting `inspected_by = frappe.session.user` explicitly. (2) ERPNext refuses to create a Quality Inspection against a Purchase Receipt unless the Item has `inspection_required_before_purchase` checked — the 4 raw material Items were created back in DP-501 before this requirement was known, so this flag is now flipped idempotently (only if unset) inside `_ensure_incoming_qc()` itself rather than requiring a DP-501 backfill. Also fixed a latent registry bug found while wiring this in: `seed_industry_pack_seeds()` only ever *appended* missing Seed Template links to Industry Pack, never updated `sequence` on ones that already existed — meant a re-ordering of `_SEED_TEMPLATES` in `setup.py` silently had no effect on a site that had already run `after_migrate` once; fixed to sync `sequence` on existing rows too. Verified via direct query (not the runner's own "ok") on both `test.demo.local` and public `pharmacountry.vn`: 5 Quality Inspections each (4 Incoming + 1 In-Process), all `status=Accepted`/`docstatus=1`, correctly linked to their batches; idempotent on re-run (0 new records created). |

| DP-506 | QA release | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_qa_release()`: moves the manufactured PARA-500-TAB batch from FG Quarantine to FG Released. Gated by a new validation hook (`block_fg_release_without_qa`, same pattern as DP-503's quarantine-issue block) requiring an Accepted Quality Inspection for that exact item+batch before allowing transfer into any warehouse whose name contains "released" — matches master plan §7 DEMO 01 test P06 verbatim ("FG chưa QA release không được chuyển sang Released warehouse"). Includes a real negative test (`_test_qa_release_requires_qc`): receives a throwaway 1-unit synthetic batch with no QC into FG Quarantine, attempts to release it, and asserts the hook blocks it — the synthetic 1-unit batch is deliberately left in FG Quarantine afterward as a harmless, self-documenting test artifact (only the release attempt itself needs to fail). **Three real bugs found+fixed while getting this working — the most instructive incident of the session so far**: (1) `hooks.py` changes are cached in Redis per-site and are *not* picked up by a fresh `bench execute` process just because the file on disk changed — the first several test runs silently used the old single-function hook list until `bench --site X clear-cache` was run; worth remembering for any future hook edit. (2) v16's Serial-and-Batch-Bundle mechanism does not populate `batch_no` on a Stock Entry Detail row when a batch is auto-created (e.g. a Material Receipt with no batch specified) — the real batch reference only exists in a linked "Serial and Batch Bundle" doc, discoverable via `Stock Entry Detail.serial_and_batch_bundle` → `Serial and Batch Entry.batch_no`; the negative test's own release attempt originally left `batch_no` unset expecting auto-detection, which let ERPNext silently auto-pick *any* available batch in the source warehouse — including the real already-QC'd one — defeating the test outright (only caught because the test was made to report its actual batch_no/result rather than a bare pass/fail). **This bug actually fired for real during debugging**: two iterations of the broken negative test genuinely moved 2 real units of the QC-approved batch into FG Released without going through the gate — caught via direct ledger verification (not the runner's "ok"), fixed by cancelling all 5 debug-run Stock Entries and confirming the ledger balance was restored to exactly 1000/0 before re-running clean. (3) `_ensure_qa_release()`'s own batch lookup (originally "any Batch of this item with `batch_qty > 0`", no ordering) was ambiguous whenever a second batch briefly existed — same root cause as bug 2 — and could pick the wrong one; fixed by deriving the batch directly from the Accepted Quality Inspection record instead, which is also more semantically correct (QA should release exactly the batch QC approved, not "a" batch). Registered as `SEED_QA_RELEASE` (sequence 32); also fixed a latent bug in `seed_industry_pack_seeds()` found while re-ordering the registry — it only ever appended new Seed Template links, never updated `sequence` on ones that already existed, so a re-ordering silently had no effect on an already-migrated site; fixed to sync `sequence` on existing rows too. Verified via direct query (not the runner's own "ok") on both `test.demo.local` and public `pharmacountry.vn`: FG Released holds exactly 1000 PARA-500-TAB, FG Quarantine holds exactly 1 (the harmless synthetic test unit), idempotent on re-run. |

### Golden Demo #3 — QMS Standalone (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-523..530; master plan DEMO 10, "PHASE 3" item 3)

**Architecturally different from Golden Demo #1/#2**: those mostly composed existing ERPNext doctypes (Purchase Order, Stock Entry, Delivery Note...) plus a handful of validation hooks. DEMO 10 is a standalone product ("có thể bán cho nhiều ngành regulated") with 9 modules ERPNext has no native equivalent for — this needed genuinely new CE-06 DocTypes (`bootstrap_qms_doctypes.py`, same ORM-creation pattern as `bootstrap_doctypes.py`), a new fresh set of business rules (`qms_validations.py`), and its own registry entry point: a new Industry Pack **IP-QMS** (`industry_category = "Horizontal / Cross-Industry"`, a new option added to that Select field — QMS doesn't fit any of the 9 vertical categories the field originally supported), separate from IP-PHARMA's own seed chain. 10 new DocTypes: QMS CAPA (the spine — Deviation/OOS/Audit Finding all feed into it via `source_type` + `source_reference` Dynamic Link), QMS Deviation, QMS Change Control, QMS OOS OOT, QMS Complaint, QMS Recall, QMS Risk, QMS Audit + its child table QMS Audit Finding, QMS Supplier Quality.

Reuses the DP-405 demo users (`qa.manager@`/`quality.director@pharmacountry.vn`) for CAPA owner vs. closer, so Q02 segregation-of-duties is proven against two genuinely different accounts, not a role-played single user.

| Task ID | Description | Code | Migration | Automated Test | Runtime Test | Documentation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DP-523 | Golden flow: Deviation → CAPA → Effectiveness → Closure | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_deviation_capa_flow()`: the master plan's own "Flow mẫu" end to end — a cold-storage temperature-excursion Deviation, investigated (root cause recorded), a linked CAPA (`source_type="QMS Deviation"`), closed only after an effectiveness check on/after the due date, by a *different* user than the owner. |
| DP-524 | Q01/Q02/Q04 validations | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_qms_validations()`. **Q01**: a Deviation can't move to Closed without `root_cause` recorded. **Q02**: a CAPA's `owner_user` can't also be its `closed_by` — segregation of duties. **Q04**: a CAPA can't close without `effectiveness_check_date` + `effectiveness_evidence`, and the check date can't be before `due_date`. All three are fresh `validate()` hooks on QMS CAPA/QMS Deviation (`qms_validations.py`) — these DocTypes are plain saveable documents, not submittable (no `docstatus`), so a blocked `save()` fails cleanly with no mid-transaction "looks submitted but isn't" ambiguity like the Stock Entry/Sales Order cases in Golden Demo #1/#2 had. |

Fixed to use `frappe.get_doc(...).save()` instead. |

**Two DocType-design bugs found+fixed during bootstrap, both circular/self-referential setup issues**: (1) `QMS Deviation`'s `linked_capa` field referenced `QMS CAPA` before that DocType existed yet — reordered creation so `QMS CAPA` comes first. (2) `QMS CAPA.source_type`'s Select options were friendly labels ("Deviation", "OOS/OOT", "Audit Finding") rather than the literal DocType names ("QMS Deviation", "QMS OOS OOT", "QMS Audit") the paired `source_reference` Dynamic Link field actually validates against — every cross-module link attempt failed with "DocType Deviation not found" until fixed. Also found: `pharmacountry.vn` has no `developer_mode`, so the ORM-`insert()`-based bootstrap script (which requires it) can't run there directly — but since the DocType `.json`/`.py` files are already written to the shared bind-mounted app directory by `test.demo.local`'s run, an ordinary `bench migrate` on `pharmacountry.vn` picks them up the same way it would any other app DocType, no `developer_mode` needed for that side.

Left one real partial-corruption artifact on `test.demo.local` (a Test correctly linked to its OOS but stuck at status "In Progress" instead of "Result Entered") — found via direct query, fixed with a targeted `db.set_value`, then the underlying hook fixed to use `doc.db_set(..., update_modified=False)` instead, which keeps the in-memory object and the DB row in sync. |

Fixed to order by `calibration_date desc, creation desc` instead — recalibration always supersedes an older record regardless of which due_date value is numerically larger. |

(2) the stocking-batch idempotency check only matched an *Active* batch — once the demo's one crop cycle completes and the batch becomes Harvested, a registry re-run tried to stock the (now correctly non-Empty) pond again and hit its own SF02 block as a real error instead of recognizing the cycle was already done; fixed to match any batch for the pond regardless of status, since this seed chain models one complete cycle, not repeated re-stocking. All 9 checks pass on both sites; a full 7-function regression sweep (all 8 golden demos together) also re-confirmed clean on both sites after these fixes. |

Only genuinely new pieces: 3 Custom Fields on Item (`target_species`, `indication`, `withdrawal_period_days` — VPM01).

| DP-560 | Master data (VPM01) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_vet_mfg_master_data()`: Company, Items (incl. FG item with species/indication/withdrawal fields set), Formula. **One real bug found+fixed**: used UOM code `"L"` for liters — ERPNext's actual UOM name is `"Litre"`, not the ISO-style short code; `"L"` doesn't exist in the default UOM list and failed with "Could not find Default Unit of Measure: L". |
| DP-561 | Production (VPM03/VPM04) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_vet_mfg_production()`: full purchase→QC-approve→manufacture→QC→release flow, identical shape to Golden Demo #1. |
| DP-562 | Formula revision (VPM02) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_vet_mfg_formula_revision()`. **Real design difference found from every prior golden demo's BOM-revision pattern**: Golden Demo #1's DP-504 and Feed's F01 both *cancelled* the old BOM outright — but those were correcting a bug in a batch that needed redoing. Here the earlier batch is legitimate, completed production history, and ERPNext correctly refuses to cancel a BOM still linked to a submitted Work Order ("Cannot delete or cancel because BOM ... is linked with Work Order"). Fixed to just flip `is_default` (old BOM stays Active as valid history, only the "which one is current" flag moves) instead of cancelling — the semantically correct choice for a revision-of-a-legitimate-batch scenario, not just a workaround. One further wrinkle: a first debug attempt's `cancel()` call *partially* succeeded (flipped `docstatus` to Cancelled) despite surfacing that same blocking error message — the by-now-familiar pattern of `bench execute`'s own display/side-effect step throwing after the real operation already went through — so the function was made robust to either starting state (old BOM still Active, or already Cancelled from a prior partial attempt) rather than assuming one. |

All 7 checks pass on both sites; a full 9-function regression sweep (all 8 golden demos + AI foundation + this) re-confirmed clean on both. |

### Golden Demo #11 — Pig Farm Management (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-573..577; master plan DEMO 22, "PHASE 4" item 3), IP-LIVESTOCK-PIG

**First fully from-scratch domain-specific DocType set since Shrimp Farm** (Golden Demo #8) — no ERPNext manufacturing/quality overlap for the breeding/growing side, mirroring Shrimp Farm's architecture (Farm→Pen/Batch/FeedLog/HealthRecord/MortalityRecord/terminal-event) adapted for pig-specific breeding entities. Two deliberate simplifications from the master plan's raw entity list, consistent with prior golden demos' "consolidate near-duplicate entities" pattern: Sow and Boar merged into one `Pig Breeding Animal` doctype with a `sex` Select field (rather than two near-identical doctypes), and Weaning folded into `Pig Grower Batch`'s own fields rather than a separate doctype (a lifecycle event on the batch, not a distinct record type with its own children). 12 new DocTypes total: `Pig Farm`, `Pig Pen`, `Pig Breeding Animal`, `Pig Breeding Service` (Service+Pregnancy as one lifecycle state machine, mirroring Shrimp Pond's `shrimp_pond_validate` transition-guard shape), `Pig Farrowing`, `Pig Grower Batch` (the "Animal/Batch" concept — has its own Empty→Occupied→Empty pen-lifecycle guard, same shape as `shrimp_stocking_batch_validate`/`on_update`), `Pig Feed Log`, `Pig Vaccination`, `Pig Medicine Treatment`, `Pig Weight Record`, `Pig Mortality Record`, `Pig Sale Lot`. Unlike Shrimp Farm (which never integrates with ERPNext Sales), `Pig Sale Lot` links to a real ERPNext `Customer` — same pattern as Vet Distribution's dealer-as-Customer — for a genuine PF08 trace-to-customer rather than a free-text buyer field.

The uncaught exception surfaced as a raw seed-runner "error" status rather than the expected "PF01 CONFIRMED" result. Fixed by catching `frappe.DuplicateEntryError` explicitly. ### Golden Demo #12 — Poultry Farm Management (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-579..583; master plan DEMO 23, "PHASE 4" item 4), IP-LIVESTOCK-POULTRY

**Leaner than Pig Farm** (9 new DocTypes vs. 12) — a poultry flock is placed as day-old chicks with no breeding-stage lifecycle, so master plan's "Flock" and "Placement" collapse into one creation event on a single `Poultry Flock` doctype rather than two. Two entities from the master plan's own list were deliberately left out, same "build what's tested" scoping discipline as every prior golden demo: "Water" has no dedicated PO0x test, and "Culling" isn't a separate PO0x test either (deliberate removals fold into the Sale Lot's head-count/livability reconciliation instead of a dedicated doctype). Two flocks were seeded in the same farm — one Broiler, one Layer — specifically so PO06 (egg output, Layer-only) has both a real positive scenario *and* a real cross-type negative test (egg production attempted against the Broiler flock, correctly blocked), rather than PO06 only ever being exercised in one direction. `Poultry Sale Lot` links to a real ERPNext `Customer`, same pattern as Pig Farm/Vet Distribution.

### Golden Demo #13 — Cattle / Dairy Farm Management (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-585..589; master plan DEMO 24, "PHASE 4" item 5), IP-LIVESTOCK-CATTLE

**Architecturally different from every prior farm golden demo.** Shrimp/Pig/Poultry all track a *batch* of identical units moving through one growth cycle (pond/pen/house occupancy state machines). Dairy cattle are long-lived individual assets with pedigree and repeated lactation cycles, so this demo has no batch/pen-occupancy concept at all — every operational record (milking, treatment, feed, sale) keys directly off an individual `Cattle Animal`. Pedigree (CT01) is a self-referential Link (`sire`/`dam` point back to `Cattle Animal`), validated for both uniqueness (an animal can't be its own sire/dam) and sex-correctness (sire must be Male, dam must be Female) — the same sex-check shape as Pig Farm's Breeding Service, applied here directly on the animal record itself. Leanest golden demo yet at 8 new DocTypes, reflecting that individual-animal tracking needs no occupancy/lifecycle doctype layer. "Body condition" (in the master plan's entity list) has no dedicated CT0x test, so it's left out — same scoping discipline as Poultry Farm's Water/Culling omissions. Two `Cattle Sale Lot` outcomes were deliberately seeded — the calf Sold to another dairy farm, the dam Culled at end of productive life — giving CT06 real coverage of both `sale_type` values and CT07 a meaningful cost rollup across the dam's full recorded feed+health history.

### Golden Demo #14 — Hatchery / Breeding Management (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-591..595; master plan DEMO 25, "PHASE 4" item 6, **the last Phase 4 item**), IP-HATCHERY

**The deepest pipeline of any farm golden demo**: Parent Stock → Egg Batch → Incubation (assigned to an Incubator — same Empty/Occupied occupancy-guard pattern as Shrimp Pond/Pig Pen/Poultry House) → Hatch Result → Chick Batch → Chick Grading → Vaccination → Dispatch, 10 new DocTypes. Two deliberate omissions from the master plan's entity list, same "build what's tested" scoping discipline as every prior golden demo: no mortality-tracking doctype (H01-H06 has no mortality test, unlike every other farm golden demo), and no cost allocation on Dispatch (H06 tests customer *trace*, not cost, unlike Pig/Poultry/Cattle's explicit PF07/PO07/CT07 cost tests). H01 (egg batch trace) and H06 (customer dispatch trace) are both satisfied by one consolidated `trace_hatchery_batch()` API helper that walks the whole pipeline in a single call — parent stock → egg batch → incubator → hatch rate → customer — rather than two separate half-trace functions.

## Phase 5 — Aqua Expansion (master plan §21 "PHASE 5" — 5 golden demos: Aquafeed Manufacturing, Aquaculture Environmental Product Manufacturing, Fish Farm, Aqua Hatchery, Seafood Processing. Started 2026-09-28 per "Tiếp tục theo thứ tự" immediately after Phase 4 completed. **COMPLETE 2026-09-28** — all 5 items done: Golden Demos #15-#19, all verified on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn`. (An earlier pass through this file mislabeled Phase 5 as complete after only the first 2 items — corrected once the master plan's own item list was re-checked.))

### Golden Demo #15 — Aquafeed Manufacturing (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-597..598; master plan DEMO 26, "PHASE 5" item 1), IP-AQUAFEED

**Heaviest reuse of any golden demo yet.** Like Feed Manufacturing (#7) and Vet Manufacturing (#9), this needed almost no new code: the whole manufacturing flow (Company/Item/BOM/Work Order/Quality Inspection/QA release) reuses Golden Demo #1's exact machinery, including `block_fg_release_without_qa` completely unmodified (AF06 — the **4th** reuse of that exact hook, after Pharma/Feed/Vet Mfg) and Golden Demo #7's `trace_feed_batch_genealogy()` reused **completely unmodified** for AF05 (it's a generic Serial-and-Batch-Bundle walk keyed by batch_no, not Feed-specific — first time a prior golden demo's own verify-time helper function, not just a hook, was reused as-is by name). Raw materials already created by Feed Manufacturing (`FISH-MEAL`, `SBM`, `PREMIX-VM`) were referenced directly rather than re-created — Item is not company-scoped in ERPNext, only Warehouse/stock balances are, so a new company can consume an existing Item master with zero duplication. Only 2 new DocTypes/fields: 3 Custom Fields on Item (`life_stage`, `pellet_size_mm`, `buoyancy`) plus one new plain record doctype (`Aquafeed Extrusion Log`, AF03) with no validate hook at all — same "record-keeping only, no negative test needed" status as Shrimp Farm's Growth Sample. `target_species` (already added by Golden Demo #9, labelled "(Vet)" there) was reused as-is for AF01's species dimension rather than adding a redundant second species field — a cosmetic label mismatch, not a functional one. No dedicated validations/negative-test seed step was needed (AF01-AF07 are all structural/reuse checks, not lifecycle-block rules) — same lean profile as Vet Manufacturing's own seed set, which also had no validations step.

### Golden Demo #16 — Aquaculture Environmental Product Manufacturing (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-600..604; master plan DEMO 27, "PHASE 5" item 2, **the last Phase 5 item**), IP-AQUA-ENVIRONMENT

**The leanest golden demo of the entire session: zero new DocTypes, zero new Custom Fields.** Every AE0x test is satisfied by pure reuse of machinery already proven across 15 prior golden demos: AE01 (formula revision) uses the exact same BOM `is_default`-flip pattern as Vet Manufacturing's VPM02; AE02 (batch/lot) is native `Item.has_batch_no`; AE03 (QC) is native Quality Inspection; AE04 (label version) reuses Golden Demo #4's whole DMS Document/DMS Document Version lifecycle with `doc_type="Label"` — the option Golden Demo #9 already added, so zero bootstrap needed; AE05 (release) reuses `block_fg_release_without_qa` completely unmodified for the **5th** time (after Pharma/Feed/Vet Mfg/Aquafeed); and AE06 (distribution trace) reuses Golden Demo #2's `trace_batch_to_customers()` completely unmodified for the **2nd** time (after Vet Distribution's VD06) — the first golden demo to reuse another golden demo's own verify-time query helper by name, not just a validation hook. This is the clearest evidence yet that the platform's reuse-first architecture (native ERPNext + shared cross-industry doctypes) compounds: each new golden demo needs proportionally less new code than the last.

### Golden Demo #17 — Fish Farm Management (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-606..610; master plan DEMO 29, "PHASE 5" item 3), IP-FISH

**Near-identical architecture to Golden Demo #8 (Shrimp Farm)** — Pond/Cage → Stocking Batch → Feed/Growth/Mortality → Harvest, same Empty→Stocked→Harvested pond lifecycle. Two deliberate differences from Shrimp Farm's DocType set, same "build what's tested" scoping discipline as every prior golden demo: no Water Parameter Reading and no Health Treatment doctype (the FF0x test list has no water or health/treatment test, even though the master plan's entity list mentions "health, treatment"). FF02 (biomass estimate) is folded into `Fish Growth Sample` as server-computed fields rather than a separate doctype — a biomass estimate is naturally derived FROM a growth/weight sample in real aquaculture practice. Modeled as a Pangasius (Cá Tra) pond in An Giang, Vietnam's main Pangasius-farming province.

| Task ID | Description | Code | Migration | Automated Test | Runtime Test | Documentation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DP-606 | Master data | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_fish_farm_master_data()`: Farm, Pond. |
| DP-607 | Stocking (FF01) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_fish_stocking()`: Stocking Batch in an Empty pond, pond flips to Stocked. Succeeded first try. |
| DP-608 | Operations (FF02/FF03/FF04/FF05) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_fish_operations()`: feed logs (FF03), growth samples with server-computed biomass estimate (FF02/FF04), mortality (FF05). **One real bug found+fixed**: the growth sample's biomass-estimate hook (`fish_growth_sample_validate`) originally summed ALL mortality records for the batch with no date filter — worse, the seed script created growth samples *before* the mortality record, so both samples computed against zero recorded mortality regardless of their own dates (both showed `estimated_population=50000` instead of the earlier sample correctly showing 50000 and the later one 47000). Fixed two ways together: (1) the hook now filters `record_date <= sample_date`, so the computation is chronologically correct regardless of future mortality; (2) the seed script now creates mortality *before* growth samples, so a mortality event that already happened by a given sample's date is actually present in the DB for the live SQL sum to find — date-filtering alone isn't sufficient if the row it needs to filter for hasn't been inserted yet. Reproduced identically on both `test.demo.local` and a fresh `pharmacountry.vn` migrate (proving it wasn't a fluke), backfilled on both via a temporary Active-status flip (bypassing the batch-must-be-Active guard just long enough to correct the already-Harvested batch's historical records), confirmed correct via direct SQL on both sites before re-verifying. |
| DP-609 | Harvest (FF06) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_fish_harvest()`: KPIs (survival rate 94.0%, FCR 1.6 — a realistic ratio for intensive Pangasius culture, cost/kg) computed server-side from the batch's own recorded history, same "server always recomputes" principle as Shrimp Harvest. |
| DP-610 | Validations (FF01) + integrity check | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | N/A | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_fish_validations()` + `api.verify_fish_farm_golden_demo()` (8 checks) + `api.trace_fish_batch()` (FF07 traceability — farm→pond→species→harvest in one call, same "small API helper walks the graph" pattern as `get_cattle_pedigree()`/`trace_hatchery_batch()`). All 8 checks pass on both sites (re-confirmed AFTER the growth-sample fix, not before); the full **17-function regression sweep (all 16 prior golden demos/AI foundation + this)** re-confirmed clean on both, and the 5-entry `IP-FISH` registry re-ran idempotently (5/5 "ok") on both. |

### Golden Demo #18 — Aquaculture Hatchery / Seed Management (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-612..616; master plan DEMO 30, "PHASE 5" item 4), IP-AQUA-HATCHERY

**Same pipeline shape as Golden Demo #14 (Hatchery/Breeding Management for poultry)** — Broodstock → Spawning → Larval Batch → Nursery → Grading → Seed Batch → Dispatch — but leaner: no separate Incubator/Hatch-Result-vs-Batch split (a shrimp Larval Batch IS the hatch result; there's no equipment-assignment concept the way poultry eggs need an Incubator), and grading is folded directly into Seed Batch creation instead of a separate Grading doctype. No Nursery Feed Log and no Water doctype — same "build what's tested" scoping discipline as every prior golden demo (the AH0x test list has neither a feed nor a water test, even though the entity list mentions both). Modeled as a whiteleg shrimp broodstock hatchery in Ninh Thuan, Vietnam's main shrimp-broodstock-import province.

### Golden Demo #19 — Seafood Processing & Export (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-618..622; master plan DEMO 32, "PHASE 5" item 5, **the last Phase 5 item**), IP-SEAFOOD-PROCESSING

**The first golden demo whose "master data" step doesn't create a new farm/pond at all** — it plugs straight into an already-completed golden demo's own harvest record. `Seafood Harvest Lot.source_reference` is a Dynamic Link (same `reference_type`/`reference_name` pattern as native ERPNext Quality Inspection) pointing at Golden Demo #8's `Shrimp Harvest`, giving a genuine, provable farm-to-export chain rather than a synthetic standalone dataset. Like the farm golden demos, this does NOT use ERPNext's Item/Stock/Warehouse machinery — lots here are graded/processed/packed by weight and carton count, not discrete stock-tracked Items. SP08 (recall simulation) reuses Golden Demo #3's `QMS Recall` doctype as-is — the 6th distinct golden demo to do so (after Vet Mfg/Vet Distribution/Feed's own recall-adjacent reuse/Aqua Environment/this — each one strengthening the case that `QMS Recall` is a genuinely generic, industry-agnostic concept, not something narrowly built for Pharma). SP07 ("Carton → batch → harvest → pond trace") is satisfied by one consolidated `trace_seafood_carton()` API helper that walks Packing Lot → Processing Batch → Grading → Harvest Lot → its Dynamic-Link source → that harvest's own Stocking Batch → Pond → Farm — proven to correctly resolve all the way back to `Demo Shrimp Farm — Bac Lieu`, `POND-A1` on both sites.

### Golden Demo #21 — Cosmetics Manufacturing (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-631..638; master plan DEMO 03, "PHASE 6" item 2), IP-COSMETICS

**The first golden demo with a genuine two-stage manufacturing flow** — a Bulk batch (formula), QC-released, then consumed as an *ingredient* by a Packed batch, itself QC-released. Modeled with two Items and two BOMs rather than a new doctype, which meant `block_fg_release_without_qa` fired TWICE in one demo (Bulk release, then Packed release) with zero hook changes — it already matches any warehouse whose name contains "released", not a hardcoded "FG Released". Three genuinely new DocTypes for concepts no prior golden demo needed: `Cosmetics Stability Sample` (C04), `Cosmetics Complaint` (C05), `Cosmetics Rework Record` (C06, an explicit audit note alongside the real stock-ledger genealogy a rework Stock Entry already produces). C01/C03 are pure reuse of the by-now-familiar BOM-revision and DMS-Artwork patterns. **This was the most bug-discovery-intensive golden demo of the session** — five distinct real bugs found and fixed, several of them by far the deepest ERPNext-internals issues encountered so far:

| Task ID | Description | Code | Migration | Automated Test | Runtime Test | Documentation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DP-631 | Master data | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_master_data()`: Company, 6 Warehouses (RM Store, Bulk Quarantine/Released, Packing Store, FG Quarantine/Released), Items (raw chemicals — reusing `CITRIC-ACID` directly from Golden Demo #20, since Item isn't company-scoped — packaging materials, Bulk item, Packed item), Bulk Formula v1, Packed Formula (consumes the Bulk item as an ingredient). |
| DP-632 | Bulk production | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_bulk_production()`: purchase→manufacture→QC→Bulk release (1st `block_fg_release_without_qa` reuse in this demo). |
| DP-633 | Packed production (C02) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_packed_production()`: bulk batch + packaging staged into Packing Store → Packed Work Order → QC → final release (2nd reuse). **Real bug #1 (ERPNext internals, the deepest of the session so far)**: ERPNext's Work Order defaults to multi-level BOM explosion — since the Bulk item is itself a manufactured item with its own active BOM, the Packed Work Order's generated Manufacture Stock Entry silently EXPLODED it down to ITS OWN raw materials (Aqua, Glycerin, Preservative, ...) instead of consuming the Bulk batch as a single ingredient, surfacing as an opaque "Valuation Rate for the Item PRESERVATIVE-PHENOXYETHANOL is required" error on an ingredient that isn't even in the Packed formula — traced by adding a temporary diagnostic function to print the generated Stock Entry's actual item rows. Fixed with `"use_multi_level_bom": 0` on Work Order creation. **Real bug #2 (additive fix to shared code)**: `trace_feed_batch_genealogy()` (reused by 4 prior golden demos) only ever returned `item_code`/`qty` for consumed rows, not which SPECIFIC batch was consumed — insufficient for C02's literal requirement ("bulk batch AND packed batch genealogy"). Fixed by adding a `batch_no` key to each consumed row (resolved through the same Serial-and-Batch-Bundle indirection the function's own output-batch lookup already used) — additive only, verified against Feed/Aquafeed/Supplement's existing reuse before and after the change to confirm nothing broke. |
| DP-634 | Formula revision (C01) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_formula_revision()`: same `is_default`-flip pattern as Vet Mfg/Aqua Env/Supplement, with Supplement's explicit before/after-unchanged BOM-items assertion. Succeeded first try. |
| DP-635 | Artwork version (C03) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_artwork()`: reuses DMS Document/Version with `doc_type="Artwork"` (the option Golden Demo #20 added). Succeeded first try. |
| DP-636 | Stability schedule (C04) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_stability()`: a 7-entry schedule across 2 storage conditions and 4 time points. **Real bug #3**: the first version derived each time point's `scheduled_date` via `add_months()` from a synthetic "manufacture_date − 90 days" anchor — calendar-month arithmetic from a non-real anchor date is ambiguous, and the entries meant to land in the past (and therefore Overdue) landed on or after today instead, silently producing 0 Overdue entries and failing `verify_cosmetics_golden_demo()`'s own C04 check. Fixed by using direct `add_days()` offsets from today, the same direct-offset convention every prior golden demo's deliberate overdue/out-of-range row already used — this file just hadn't followed it the first time. |
| DP-637 | Distribution + complaint (C05) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_distribution_and_complaint()`: Sales Order → Delivery Note to a real `Customer` (new leaf `Customer Group` "Consumer Health / Cosmetics Retailers"), then a Complaint referencing the batch, confirmed via `trace_batch_to_customers()` (unmodified) that the complaint's customer actually received that batch. Succeeded first try. |
| DP-638 | Rework (C06) + integrity check | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | N/A | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_cosmetics_rework()` + `api.verify_cosmetics_golden_demo()` (12 checks). A manual `Stock Entry` with `purpose="Repack"` (not `"Manufacture"` — the semantically correct ERPNext purpose for reprocessing an existing batch, and requiring `trace_feed_batch_genealogy()`'s own WHERE clause to be broadened to match `purpose in ('Manufacture', 'Repack')`, additive) consumes the original Bulk batch's *remaining* stock plus a small corrective ingredient, producing a new batch — proving genealogy survives rework via the real stock ledger, not just the explicit `Cosmetics Rework Record`. **Real bug #4**: the rework originally tried to consume the FULL original batch quantity, but 75% of it had already been consumed by packed production, leaving only 25% — a `BatchNegativeStockError`; fixed by computing the actually-available remaining quantity dynamically instead of assuming the full original amount was still there. **Real bug #5 (idempotency, found across two separate re-run failures)**: both `seed_cosmetics_packed_production()` and this function originally located "the bulk batch" via `{"batch_qty": [">", 0]}, order_by="creation asc"` — fragile once the rework creates a SECOND batch of the same item, because once the original batch's own live quantity (summed across all warehouses) drops to exactly 0 after being fully consumed, that filter silently starts resolving to the wrong (reworked) batch instead, corrupting a second full-registry re-run into trying to rework the rework and hitting negative-stock errors. Fixed with a new `_original_bulk_batch()` helper that identifies the batch by which Work Order actually produced it (a stable join through Work Order → Manufacture Stock Entry → Serial and Batch Entry), never by a fluctuating quantity — and separately, the "was the bulk material already staged for packing" check had the same fragility (Packing Store's own balance reads 0 both when nothing was ever staged AND after staging fully got consumed by the Packed Work Order), fixed by checking whether the Packed Work Order has already manufactured instead of checking a balance. Confirmed genuinely idempotent across 3 consecutive full-registry re-runs after both fixes, not just re-verified once. A company-recreation artifact from mid-debugging cleanup (`Company.cost_center` came back NULL after a forced delete+recreate) was also caught and given a defensive fix in `_ensure_company()`, though it turned out not to recur on a truly fresh `pharmacountry.vn` install. All 12 checks pass on both sites; the full **21-function regression sweep (all 20 prior golden demos/AI foundation + this)** re-confirmed clean on both, and the 8-entry `IP-COSMETICS` registry re-ran idempotently (8/8 "ok", 3 consecutive passes) on both. |

### Golden Demo #22 — Medical Device Manufacturing (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-640..646; master plan DEMO 04, "PHASE 6" item 3), IP-MEDICAL-DEVICE

**First golden demo to dual-track a finished item by both batch AND serial number** (`has_batch_no` + `has_serial_no` together, a real supported ERPNext combination) — MD01 (serial uniqueness) is native Frappe primary-key uniqueness on Serial No, zero new code, and MD05 (finished serial traces components) is a thin wrapper (`trace_serial_genealogy()`) around Golden Demo #7's `trace_feed_batch_genealogy()`, since a serial belongs to exactly one batch. `block_fg_release_without_qa` reused unmodified for the 7th time. Four genuinely new concerns, each satisfied with either a small DocType or Custom Fields on native doctypes rather than new validation frameworks: `MedDev Design Change Record` (MD02's approval gate for BOM revisions, enforced via a new `BOM.design_change_record` Custom Field + `validate` hook), `Supplier.quality_status`/`is_critical_supplier` Custom Fields + a `Purchase Order` hook (MD03), a `Stock Entry` hook keyed on "wip"-pattern warehouses (MD04, inverse of the release-gate's own pattern — blocks on a Rejected QC existing rather than an Accepted one missing), and a `Work Order.equipment` Custom Field + hook reusing Golden Demo #6's `EAM Calibration Record` and its exact "most recently PERFORMED Pass calibration" ordering logic (MD07). `MedDev Complaint` is the only doctype with no reused mechanism behind it (MD06).

### Golden Demo #23 — Pharmacy Chain (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-648..656; master plan DEMO 09, "PHASE 6" item 4), IP-PHARMACY (new pack — a retail/POS vertical wasn't in DP-303's original 19-pack list, registered directly alongside QMS/DMS/LIMS/EAM's precedent for post-hoc pack additions)

**Zero new DocTypes — pure native ERPNext retail/POS reuse**, the third golden demo this session to need none (after Aqua Environment, Supplement). HQ/Central/Store are just Warehouses; POS is native `POS Invoice`/`POS Profile`/`POS Opening Entry`; "Promotion" is a native `Pricing Rule`; "Replenishment" is a native `Material Request` + `Stock Entry`; "Expired product blocked" is native `StockController` behavior (already proven zero-custom-code by Golden Demo #2's PD02); "Central dashboard" is a plain aggregate query function. Reuses Golden Demo #1's own `PARA-500-TAB` (Paracetamol) Item directly. Before writing any code, a research agent was sent to read ERPNext's actual `pos_invoice.py`/`pos_opening_entry.py`/`pos_profile.py` controllers and report the exact server-side-enforced field requirements — a deliberate front-loaded-research approach given how unfamiliar (compared to every prior manufacturing-shaped golden demo) the POS module's validation chain is.

**By far the most operationally demanding golden demo of the session** — 3+ hours of real debugging across roughly a dozen distinct bugs, several qualitatively new failure classes not seen anywhere else this session:

| Task ID | Description | Code | Migration | Automated Test | Runtime Test | Documentation | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DP-648 | Master data | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_master_data()`: Company, 3 Warehouses, central stock, `POS Profile` + `Mode of Payment Account`, walk-in `Customer`, `POS Settings`. **Real bugs found+fixed, several genuinely new failure classes**: (1) `item_code` guessed as the item's display name ("Paracetamol 500 mg Tablet") instead of its real `item_code` (`PARA-500-TAB`) — checked the actual Item record rather than re-guessing after the first failure; (2) `Company.cost_center` and `Company.default_receivable_account` both came back unset after Company creation (the cost_center gap first found in Cosmetics recurred; the receivable-account one — needed for POS Invoice's "Credit To" account — was new); (3) **the whole site's `POS Settings.invoice_type` was configured to "Sales Invoice" mode**, which server-side blocks creating any non-return `POS Invoice` outright with "Sales Invoice mode is activated in POS" — a genuinely new failure class (a global site CONFIGURATION gate, not a doctype/field bug), found by grepping ERPNext's own source for the exact error string; fixed by flipping the setting (confirmed safe first — grepped the whole codebase and found no other golden demo touches POS at all). |
| DP-649 | Replenishment (RX02) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_replenishment()`: Material Request (Material Transfer type) fulfilled by a Stock Entry, Central → Store A. **Two real bugs**: (1) linking the Stock Entry back to the Material Request via `material_request`/`material_request_item` fields triggered an ERPNext internal `AttributeError` (`validate_with_material_request()` cross-checks each item row against a SPECIFIC Material Request Item row by name, which wasn't set) — fixed by leaving the Stock Entry deliberately unlinked, the Material Request still stands as its own evidence a request was made; (2) the idempotency guard originally checked Store A's CURRENT stock balance (`>= 150`) rather than whether the replenishment transaction itself already existed — since DP-650's inter-store transfer later removes some of that stock, the balance-based guard was never stable, and kept re-triggering additional replenishments on every subsequent "idempotent" re-run, silently draining Central Warehouse deeper each time. Fixed to guard on transaction existence instead — the same fix pattern applied to `_ensure_central_stock()`'s own balance-based guard for the identical reason. |
| DP-650 | Inter-store transfer (RX04) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_inter_store_transfer()`: Store A → Store B. **Two real bugs**: (1) the batch lookup was globally unscoped beyond warehouse (no exclusion, no ordering) and could non-deterministically pick a later-created deliberately-expired test batch instead of real stock; (2) the idempotency guard counted "any 2nd Material Transfer for this company" — satisfied by TWO Central→Store A replenishments alone (from DP-649's own bug above compounding this one), so this step reported "already existed" on every re-run having NEVER once actually moved anything into Store B — only caught because `verify_pharmacy_golden_demo()`'s RX01 check queried Store B's real balance directly (0) rather than trusting the seed step's own self-report. Fixed the guard to check specifically for a transfer INTO Store B. |
| DP-651 | POS session | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_pos_session()`: opens today's `POS Opening Entry` for Store A's POS Profile — required by `POS Invoice`'s own server-side `validate()` before any sale, confirmed by the research agent ahead of time, so this step needed zero debugging. |
| DP-652 | Expiry block (RX03) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_expiry_block()`: same native, zero-custom-code `StockController` behavior as Golden Demo #2's PD02, proven here against a `POS Invoice` specifically. |
| DP-653 | POS sale | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_pos_sale()`: a real sale, the prerequisite for RX05's return. **Two real bugs, both variants of the same root cause**: POS Invoice's own `validate()` requires the `payments` child table to EXACTLY cover `grand_total` (no partial payment) — a hardcoded payment amount broke once a live `Pricing Rule` (created by a LATER seed step in an earlier registry pass) started auto-discounting this same item; and separately, `paid_amount` (a top-level field) isn't auto-resynced from the `payments` child table on a plain `.save()`, so correcting the payments row alone left a stale mismatch. Fixed by computing the payment amount from the document's own actual `grand_total` after insert, and explicitly setting `paid_amount` alongside it rather than assuming ERPNext keeps the two in sync automatically. |
| DP-654 | POS return (RX05) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_pos_return()`: uses ERPNext's native `make_return_doc` (same mechanism as Golden Demo #2's PD05). **Real bug**: `make_return_doc` negates items/totals but does NOT touch the POS-specific `payments` child table at all, leaving it at the ORIGINAL (positive, pre-return) amount — and this mismatch is caught during `insert()` itself (payment validation runs in `validate()`, not just `submit()`), so a fix applied only after insert never gets a chance to run. Fixed by computing the correctly-negated payment amount from the ORIGINAL invoice's own known `grand_total` and setting it on the in-memory return doc BEFORE calling insert. |
| DP-655 | Promotion (RX06) | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | Done | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_promotion()`: a `Pricing Rule` discounting the item 10%, then a Sales Order proving the discount applies automatically. **Real bug**: the idempotency check looked up the Pricing Rule by its `title` field as if that were the document `name` — `Pricing Rule`'s real `name` is an auto-generated series (`PRLE-0001` etc.), so the check never matched anything and created a fresh duplicate rule on every registry pass; ERPNext then refuses to apply ANY of several Pricing Rules with identical criteria ("Multiple Price Rules exist... please resolve conflict"), breaking every OTHER step that priced this item too. Fixed the existence check to filter by `{"title": ...}` instead of assuming it's the name — the same fix applied to `verify_pharmacy_golden_demo()`'s own check. |
| DP-656 | Central dashboard (RX07) + integrity check | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | N/A | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_pharmacy_dashboard()` + `api.get_pharmacy_central_dashboard()` + `api.verify_pharmacy_golden_demo()` (9 checks). One real SQL bug caught before it ever ran live: an unparenthesized `OR ... AND` in the dashboard query gave the `AND is_cancelled=0` clause higher precedence than intended, silently exempting the first `OR` branch from that filter. **A separate, serious incident during cleanup**: an early debugging attempt to fix corrupted test data used an UNSCOPED `frappe.delete_doc("Batch", ...)` filtered only by `{"item": "PARA-500-TAB"}` — since that Item is reused globally from Golden Demo #1, this deleted batch `552242D`, a real batch referenced by Golden Demo #5 (LIMS)'s own seed data. The full 22-function regression sweep confirmed no golden demo actually broke (every downstream reference is either a Stock Ledger Entry, which survives Batch-document deletion, or a plain string `batch_reference` field, not a Link needing the Batch document to exist) — but the incident itself is the clearest reminder yet in this session that a scoping mistake in a cleanup script can reach far outside the golden demo being debugged, and warehouse/company scoping needs to be checked BEFORE running any bulk delete, not after. All 9 checks pass on both sites, confirmed genuinely idempotent across 3+ consecutive full-registry re-runs (not just re-verified once) with STABLE, non-growing stock balances; the full **23-function regression sweep (all 22 prior golden demos/AI foundation + this)** re-confirmed clean on both. |

### Golden Demo #24 — Pharma 3PL / GSP / Cold Chain (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-658..665; master plan DEMO 08, "PHASE 6" item 5), IP-3PL-COLDCHAIN (new pack — a 3PL warehouse-operator vertical wasn't in DP-303's original 19-pack list, registered directly alongside IP-PHARMACY's own precedent for post-hoc pack additions; `Industry Pack.industry_category` has a fixed Select option list with no "Logistics" value, so this pack is filed under "Pharmaceutical" — matching IP-PHARMACY — rather than adding a new option)

**Minimal new schema — 1 new DocType, not zero, but deliberately kept to exactly one.** Most of the master plan's "special characteristics" list turned out to be native ERPNext reuse once actually checked against source: stock ownership by client (W01) uses Warehouse's own native `customer` Link field (used literally, not just via naming) PLUS a `User Permission` on `Warehouse` with `apply_to_all_doctypes=1` per client-portal `User` — verified BEFORE writing any code by reading Frappe's actual `frappe/database/query.py` (`Engine.get_user_permission_conditions()`) and by an empirical live test proving the cascade reaches `Stock Ledger Entry` through `frappe.get_list()`/ORM calls, while `frappe.get_all()` and raw `frappe.db.sql()` are BOTH permission-unaware by design (a materially important distinction: `get_all()` force-sets `ignore_permissions=True` internally, which is easy to assume is the safe/read-only option but is actually the UNRESTRICTED one). FEFO (W03) is the same `Batch.expiry_date asc` picking pattern every prior batch-tracked golden demo already used. Quarantine (W04) reuses `block_fg_release_without_qa` completely unmodified for the release-with-QC-gate transfer (by naming the destination warehouses "...Released - D3PL") plus one new, narrowly-scoped sibling hook (`block_quarantine_delivery` in `threepl_validations.py`) for the negative case, since 3PL outbound goes out via Delivery Note directly, a document type the existing quarantine hook (scoped to manufacturing purposes on Stock Entry) never touches. Inventory reconciliation (W05) is native `Stock Reconciliation` directly. Transport is fully native — Delivery Note already ships with `transporter`/`driver_name`/`vehicle_no`/`lr_no`/`lr_date`. SLA reuses native `Sales Order Item.delivery_date` as the commitment, captured onto 2 new informational Custom Fields on Delivery Note (no enforcement hook — the spec gives SLA no lettered test). The one new DocType, `Cold Chain Temperature Reading` (W02), is deliberately a single record type: the reading document itself, flagged by a validate hook against 2 new Custom Fields on Warehouse (`min_temp_c`/`max_temp_c`), IS the excursion event — no second "event" doctype shadowing the same row. Billing (W06) is native `Item` (2 new non-stock service items) + `Item Price`, aggregated by `seed_3pl_billing_run()` from real stock-ledger and Delivery Note data into a real `Sales Invoice` per client, mirroring `get_pharmacy_central_dashboard()`'s "thin wrapper over real ledger data" pattern — and deliberately skips billing a service a client never used (Client B's invoice has no picking-fee line) rather than forcing a placeholder qty.

Also found: `Industry Pack.industry_category`'s fixed Select options don't include "Logistics" — filed under "Pharmaceutical" instead (fixed before the first `bench migrate`, not after). |

Fixed by setting `Batch.expiry_date` directly via `frappe.db.set_value()` after Stock Entry submission, once the real batch_no is known. |

The negative-test guard deliberately returns "skip" (not throw) when nothing currently sits in Quarantine, since release always empties it by design — guarding on "no batch found → throw" would have broken idempotency on every registry re-run after the first. Succeeded first try. |

Fixed by reading that specific batch's own current qty (already available from the existing per-batch `_batches_in_warehouse()` helper) and reconciling THAT down by 2, not the warehouse total. |

Succeeded first try — the User Permission design was verified against real Frappe source and an empirical live test (see module docstring / demo summary) BEFORE this code was written, not discovered by trial and error here. |
| DP-665 | Billing run (W06) + integrity check | Done | N/A | N/A | Done | N/A | CODE COMPLETE | `seed_3pl_billing_run()` + `api.verify_3pl_golden_demo()` (13 checks, exactly W01–W06 plus setup/company checks). **Real bug found+fixed**: Sales Invoice submission requires a Company `round_off_cost_center`, another Company field not reliably auto-populated by Company creation — the same class of gap as `cost_center`/`default_receivable_account` (Cosmetics/Pharmacy), just never surfaced until this demo's first Sales Invoice since no prior golden demo's `_ensure_company()` needed one. Also restructured `_ensure_company()`'s field-repair checks to run UNCONDITIONALLY (not only right after first creation) so a re-run against an already-existing company still repairs a field that came back empty for any reason — the original structure (copied from Pharmacy) only fixed gaps inside the "just created" branch, which would have silently never applied the `round_off_cost_center` fix to the already-existing company on this exact site during the fix-and-rerun cycle. All 13 checks pass on both sites; confirmed genuinely idempotent across 3 consecutive full-registry re-runs on both sites (not just re-verified once) with STABLE, non-growing balances and matching FEFO batch selection/billing amounts between sites; the full **24-function regression sweep (all 23 prior golden demos/AI foundation + this)** re-confirmed clean on both. |

### Golden Demo #25 — Consumer Health / Cosmetics Distribution (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-666..673; master plan DEMO 06, "PHASE 6" item 6), IP-CONSUMER-DIST (new pack — a distributor/wholesaler vertical wasn't in DP-303's original 19-pack list, registered directly alongside IP-PHARMACY/IP-3PL-COLDCHAIN's own precedent for post-hoc pack additions; `Industry Pack.industry_category` has a fixed Select option list with no "Consumer Products"/"Distribution" value — checked `industry_pack.json`'s actual options before picking, same lesson 3PL/Pharmacy already hit — filed under "Cosmetics", the closest fit since one of the two distributed item lines is cosmetics)

**Zero new DocTypes and zero new Custom Fields — the single most native-reuse-heavy golden demo of this session**, confirming the master plan's own prediction that sales/distribution is ERPNext's home turf. A DISTRIBUTOR (not manufacturer) of TPBVSK/supplement and cosmetics products through a two-territory dealer network, downstream of Golden Demo #20 (Supplement, `VITC-1000-EFF`) and Golden Demo #21 (Cosmetics, `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML`) — both finished Items reused directly via a fresh Material Receipt into the new Company's own Distribution Center warehouse, since Item isn't company-scoped. Every "special characteristic" in the master plan mapped to native ERPNext schema, each verified against real source before writing code: Dealer = `Customer` + native `Sales Partner`; Price policy (CD01) = native `Price List` + `Item Price` + `Customer.default_price_list`; Promotion (CD02) = native `Pricing Rule.valid_from`/`valid_upto`, tested on BOTH edges of the window (not just "discount applies," also "discount correctly does NOT apply outside the window" — an assertion no prior golden demo's promotion test made); Territory + Sales target = native `Territory` tree with `territory_manager`/`targets`/`Monthly Distribution`, same pattern as Golden Demo #10; Dealer credit (CD04) = native `Customer.credit_limits` + `check_credit_limit()`, confirmed by reading `erpnext/selling/doctype/customer/customer.py` to throw a plain `frappe.throw(...)` (`frappe.ValidationError`, not a dedicated subclass) before writing the negative test; **Commission report (CD06) needed no commission math at all** — reading `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py`'s `calculate_contribution()` before writing any code revealed ERPNext already auto-computes `Sales Team.incentives` per row (`allocated_amount × commission_rate / 100`) on submit, gated only by `Item.grant_commission` (which both reused Items already default to `1`); the report is a pure aggregate over that native field. Returns (CD05) reused `make_return_doc("Delivery Note", ...)`, confirming (by reading `sales_and_purchase_return.py`) that Delivery Note returns carry `batch_no` forward automatically with no manual correction needed, unlike Pharmacy's POS Invoice return.

**Two real cross-demo regressions found and fixed by the mandatory full regression sweep** (neither surfaced by this demo's own 14 checks, which passed clean in isolation both times) — the exact failure mode this session's lessons warn about, this time actually triggered for the first time: `verify_supplement_golden_demo()`'s S04/S05/S06 checks and `verify_cosmetics_golden_demo()`'s C02/C04/C05 checks each did an **unscoped** `Batch` lookup for their own reused Item (`{"item": ..., "batch_qty": [">", 0]}`, no warehouse scoping, sometimes no `order_by`) that had silently worked for 24 prior golden demos because no other demo had ever created a SECOND batch of `VITC-1000-EFF`/`FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML` — until this one did (its own Distribution Center receipt). `Batch.batch_qty` is a static received-quantity snapshot, never decremented as stock moves, so both the old and new batch satisfied the filter, and the query non-deterministically returned Golden Demo #25's own batch instead of Supplement's/Cosmetics' own. Fixed by scoping both lookups to each demo's own `FG Released` warehouse via a join. **A second, deeper issue surfaced while fixing the first**: the initial join attempt (`Stock Ledger Entry.batch_no = Batch.name`) silently matched zero rows — this ERPNext version (v16) no longer stores `batch_no` directly on Stock Ledger Entry; batch linkage moved to `Serial and Batch Bundle`/`Serial and Batch Entry`. Confirmed live via direct SQL before finalizing the fix, then rewritten as a proper `Batch` → `Serial and Batch Entry` → `Stock Ledger Entry.serial_and_batch_bundle` join, scoped by warehouse — this is a new, previously-undocumented lesson for future golden demos: **any new SQL scoping fix that joins through Stock Ledger Entry to reach a batch must go through `Serial and Batch Entry`, not a direct `batch_no` column, in this ERPNext version.**

**One real same-demo idempotency bug found and fixed**, also only surfaced by re-running the registry (not by the first pass): `_ensure_sales_flow()`'s own Delivery Note lookup (`{"against_sales_order": so_name}`, unscoped) suffered the identical ambiguity as the cross-demo Batch bug, one layer down — once CD05's return existed, the return Delivery Note's own rows ALSO carry `against_sales_order` (copied forward by `make_return_doc`), so on the second registry run this lookup picked the RETURN document instead of the original delivery, found no Sales Invoice against it, and created a bogus SECOND Sales Invoice **from the return itself** (negative qty/amount), silently corrupting CD06's commission totals (`invoice_count` for the Hanoi salesperson silently became 2, commission dropped from 210,000 to 168,000). Fixed with a shared `_find_original_delivery_note()` helper joining `Delivery Note Item` → `Delivery Note` and explicitly filtering `is_return = 0`, used both by the sales-flow step and by CD05's own return logic and `verify_consumer_dist_golden_demo()`. The bogus invoice was cancelled and deleted before re-verifying.

All 14 checks pass on both sites; confirmed genuinely idempotent across 3+ consecutive full-registry re-runs on both sites (stable Sales Invoice/Delivery Note counts, not just re-verified once); the full **25-function regression sweep (all 24 prior golden demos/AI foundation + this)** re-confirmed clean on both sites after fixing the two cross-demo Batch-scoping regressions described above. |

### Golden Demo #26 — Premix / Feed Additive Manufacturing (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-675..679; master plan DEMO 19, "PHASE 6" item 7), IP-PREMIX (already reserved in `_INDUSTRY_PACKS` from DP-303, category "Animal Feed" — no new pack registration needed, unlike the last 3 golden demos)

**Precision manufacturing, downstream/kin of Golden Demo #7 (Compound Feed Manufacturing)** — this Company makes the vitamin/mineral concentrate ("Premix") that a feed mill's own Formula (Feed's `PIG-STARTER`, which already buys generic `PREMIX-VM` as an ingredient) actually needs a real GMP precision process to produce: a bulk carrier (Rice Hull, 469kg of a 500kg batch) diluting four mg/g-scale micro-ingredients (3 vitamins + 1 trace mineral, one — Selenium, 50g in the whole batch — flagged critical for its narrow safety margin) mixed in a specific declared order. Reused Golden Demo #7's own manufacturing machinery unmodified: `block_fg_release_without_qa` (release gate), `block_weighing_tolerance_exceeded` (F03's blanket ±3% — still the binding constraint for the bulk carrier/mineral mix; PM01's own ±1% is the additional, TIGHTER constraint layered on top for micro ingredients specifically), and `trace_feed_batch_genealogy()` (PM05, called completely unmodified). **PM06 (COA/potency) needed zero new schema at all** — native ERPNext `Quality Inspection Reading` (a `numeric` reading with `min_value`/`max_value`/`reading_1`) already auto-computes Accepted/Rejected from a tolerance band (confirmed by reading `quality_inspection.py`'s own `min_max_criteria_passed()` before relying on it, per this session's "verify against real source" discipline) — exactly PM06's "measured potency within tolerance of target," so only a plain `Quality Inspection Parameter` master row was needed. Only 4 Custom Fields + 1 new DocType: `Item.is_micro_ingredient`/`Item.requires_second_check` (PM01/PM02 gates), `BOM Item.sequence_no` (PM03 mixing order), `BOM.premix_approval_status` (PM04 gate — same shape as Medical Device's MD02 `BOM.design_change_record` but a plain Select instead of a Link to a separate approval-record DocType, since the approval concept here doesn't need its own document); `Premix Weighing Verification` (PM02's four-eyes GMP record — a real audit row with `weighed_by`/`verified_by`/`verified_on`, not just a boolean flag, since a GMP double-check genuinely produces a record like this).

**One real, previously-latent cross-demo regression found and fixed by the mandatory registry run itself** (not by the regression sweep this time — it blocked the very first seed step outright): `meddev_bom_validate()` (Golden Demo #22's MD02 hook, registered on `BOM.validate` with zero scoping) unconditionally blocked ANY `is_default` BOM platform-wide that lacked a `design_change_record` — not just Medical Device's own. This had been silently latent since Golden Demo #22 because no manufacturing demo created a NEW BOM afterward until Premix (Pharmacy/3PL/Consumer Distribution are retail/logistics/distribution demos with no BOM at all). Fixed by scoping the hook to `doc.company == "Demo MedDevice Co."`, the same company-scoping idiom this session already uses for every other cross-cutting hook — confirmed safe by re-running `verify_meddev_golden_demo()` itself (still 12/12 passing) alongside the full sweep. **A second, self-contained bug found while building the PM01/PM03 negative tests**: a manual (non-`make_stock_entry()`) Manufacture-purpose Stock Entry with a `work_order` set hits two native ERPNext gates before any custom hook even runs — `fg_completed_qty` is mandatory, and at least one `is_finished_item` row is required ("There must be atleast 1 Finished Good in this Stock Entry") — found live via the actual error text rather than guessed, fixed by adding a minimal 1-qty finished-good row to both negative-test documents. **A third, self-inflicted design issue**: the first PM01 negative-test value (3.5kg vs. a 3.0kg target, 16.7% deviation) was being intercepted by the GENERIC F03 hook (±3%) before ever reaching PM01's own ±1% check, since F03 runs earlier in the `Stock Entry.validate` hook list — fixed by choosing a deviation strictly between the two bands (3.06kg, 2%), which is actually the more rigorous test: it proves PM01 is a genuinely tighter, additional constraint, not just a duplicate of F03.
