### Golden Demo #27 — Feed / Ingredient Trading (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-680..685; master plan DEMO 21, "PHASE 6" item 8), IP-INGREDIENT-TRADING (new pack — a pure-trading, no-manufacturing vertical wasn't in DP-303's original 19-pack list, registered directly alongside IP-PHARMACY/IP-3PL-COLDCHAIN/IP-CONSUMER-DIST's own precedent for post-hoc pack additions; filed under the fixed Select option "Animal Feed" — same category as IP-FEED/IP-PREMIX — since the traded goods are feed ingredients/additives, no new option list value needed)

**Zero new DocTypes and zero new Custom Fields — matching Consumer Distribution (Golden Demo #25) as the most native-reuse-heavy golden demo shape this session, confirming the master plan's own "ERP trading core đủ" prediction for this vertical.** A pure TRADING company (import/wholesale of raw feed ingredients — Soybean Meal from Singapore, Fish Meal from Argentina — NOT a manufacturer, no BOM/Work Order anywhere in this demo) buying from overseas suppliers with real FX exposure, receiving with QC, reselling to domestic feed-mill customers under a committed-quantity contract, tracking margins. Every "special characteristic" in the master plan mapped directly onto native ERPNext Buying/Selling schema, each verified against real source before writing any code: shipment reconciliation (FT01) reads native `Purchase Order Item.qty`/`received_qty` directly, with billed qty DERIVED (Purchase Order Item has no native `billed_qty` field, only `billed_amt`, an amount — confirmed by reading `purchase_order_item.json` before writing this) by summing `Purchase Invoice Item.qty` for invoices referencing the Purchase Receipt; FX handling (FT02) is native `Purchase Order.currency`/`conversion_rate`/`base_grand_total`, proven genuine (not just "a currency field exists") with two POs identical in every respect except `conversion_rate`, yielding the same USD `grand_total` but two different, arithmetically-correct VND `base_grand_total` amounts; supplier lot (FT03) is a LIGHTER lookup than `trace_feed_batch_genealogy()` (which is keyed on finding a Manufacture/Repack Stock Entry — this demo has no manufacturing step at all, so that function would find nothing here) — a new `get_ingredient_supplier_lot()` joining `Batch` → `Serial and Batch Entry` → `Serial and Batch Bundle` (using the bundle's own `voucher_type`/`voucher_no`, one hop shorter than routing through Stock Ledger Entry) → `Purchase Receipt.supplier`; incoming QC (FT04) is native `Quality Inspection` (`reference_type="Purchase Receipt"`, `inspection_type="Incoming"`), release gated by `block_fg_release_without_qa` reused COMPLETELY UNMODIFIED (9th reuse this session); contract quantity (FT05) uses native `Blanket Order` (Manufacturing module), NOT the generic CRM `Contract` doctype — both doctypes' real source were read before choosing: `Contract` is a legal-document tracker with no quantity/drawdown concept at all, while `Blanket Order Item.ordered_qty` is a NATIVE field auto-recomputed by `BlanketOrder.update_ordered_qty()` (summed from `Sales Order Item.stock_qty` where `blanket_order == self.name` and `docstatus == 1`, triggered from `Sales Order.update_blanket_order()` on submit) plus native over-commit enforcement (`validate_against_blanket_order()`) — confirmed live that `Selling Settings.blanket_order_allowance` is 0/unset on this site before writing the negative test; price history (FT06) is a pure aggregation over native `Item Price.valid_from`/`price_list_rate` (multiple dated rows per item/price_list are allowed by ERPNext's own `check_duplicates()`, confirmed by reading `item_price.py` before relying on this); margin report (FT07) is a real aggregation over actual Purchase Invoice (`base_amount`, already company-currency-converted) vs. Sales Invoice data per item, the same "thin wrapper over real ledger data" pattern as `get_pharmacy_central_dashboard()`/3PL's billing run/Consumer Distribution's commission report. Two traded ingredients: `SOYBEAN-MEAL-48` (carries the shrinkage/FX/QC/contract story, FT01-FT05) and `FISH-MEAL-65` (a second, lighter chain whose only job is proving FT06/FT07's per-item aggregation genuinely GROUPS instead of being hardcoded to one item).

**Four real bugs found and fixed live, none guessed in advance:** (1) `Purchase Order` has NO `po_no` field at all — that's a Sales Order-only field (the customer's own PO reference number on an order the company RECEIVES); an early draft of this seed mistakenly copied that idiom onto the buying side and hit `"Unknown column 'po_no' in 'WHERE'"` — fixed by using Purchase Order's own plain `title` field as the idempotency marker instead. (2) A Purchase Invoice in USD against the Company's auto-created (VND-only) default payable account throws `"Party Account Creditors - DIT currency (VND) and document currency (USD) should be same"` — ERPNext's real fix for genuine multi-currency AP is a DEDICATED foreign-currency payable account wired onto the Supplier's own `Party Account` child table (`accounts`), not a property flip on the default account; fixed by creating a sibling `Creditors USD - DIT` account (`account_currency="USD"`) and appending it to each Supplier's `accounts` table — with an UNCONDITIONAL repair guard (not just on first creation) so a Supplier created before this fix existed self-heals on a re-run, the same idempotency-guard class this session's lessons warn about. (3) `Item.inspection_required_before_purchase=1` (FT04's own prerequisite) blocks Purchase Receipt SUBMISSION itself unless that exact row already carries a linked `quality_inspection` — circular for this demo's intended flow (receive into Quarantine → THEN inspect → THEN release, the same "QC gates the NEXT step" shape as every other golden demo's incoming-QC pattern this session). Fixed via the correct native lever, confirmed by reading `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`'s `validate_inspection()` before writing this: `Stock Settings.allow_to_make_quality_inspection_after_purchase_or_delivery` — a GLOBAL, ONLY-RELAXING toggle (skips the inline-QI-required check for Purchase Receipt/Purchase Invoice/Sales Invoice/Delivery Note; never makes any existing flow MORE strict), safe to enable site-wide with zero risk to any other golden demo, confirmed by the full regression sweep. This also required reordering the import-shipment task boundary: DP-681 now only receives the shipment (FT01's ordered-vs-received leg + FT02); DP-682 creates the Quality Inspection FIRST, then the Purchase Invoice (FT01's invoiced-qty leg), matching the real ERPNext gate order. (4) `Quality Inspection Reading.reading_1` is a Data field validated against the SITE's own `System Settings.number_format`, not a fixed decimal point — `pharmacountry.vn` (production) uses `"#.###,##"` (comma decimal separator) while `test.demo.local` (dev) uses the default `"#,###.##"` (period); a hardcoded `"47.2"` string passed on dev but was rejected on production (`"Reading 1 47.2 is not a valid number in the #.###,## number format"`) — the SAME code, unmodified, behaved differently per site purely due to locale configuration, a genuinely new bug class for this session. Fixed with a small `_locale_reading()` helper deriving the site's actual decimal separator from its `number_format` string (the character 3 positions before the end, which covers every stock Frappe number_format option) instead of assuming `"."`.

### Golden Demo #28 — Meat / Animal Product Processing (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-686..690; master plan DEMO 31, "PHASE 6" item 9, **the last Phase 6 item**), IP-MEAT-PROCESSING (already reserved in `_INDUSTRY_PACKS` from DP-303, category "Processing" — no new pack registration needed)

**Structurally the closest kin in the entire codebase to Golden Demo #19 (Seafood Processing & Export)** — read and adapted from it directly rather than designed from scratch. Same architectural choice: no new Company, no ERPNext Item/Stock/Warehouse machinery at all, a self-contained parallel pipeline of 7 new DocTypes (`Meat Plant`, `Meat Incoming Lot`, `Meat Processing Batch` + its `Meat Processing Batch Source` child table, `Meat Packing Lot`, `Meat Cold Storage Record`, `Meat Distribution`), because lots here are received/processed/packed by live weight and carton count, not discrete stock-tracked Items. Same Dynamic-Link-to-an-existing-record reuse story as Seafood's own Shrimp/Fish Harvest precedent — `Meat Incoming Lot.source_reference` (source_type/source_reference, same pattern) points at the REAL `Pig Sale Lot` Golden Demo #11 (Pig Farm) already sold to "Dong Nai Meat Processing Co." — a Customer record that demo planted in anticipation of exactly this one, discovered by querying the live database rather than assumed. Deliberately went one step beyond Seafood's 1:1 chain: a second, self-contained "Direct Farm Intake" lot (no upstream golden demo behind it) was added so the one real Processing Batch consumes TWO incoming lots via a genuine child-table genealogy, making MP03 a non-trivial multi-source proof rather than a 1:1 shortcut, and making MP07's recall-impact query prove something real (that querying either of two different upstream lots resolves to the SAME downstream finished/distributed lot). Slaughter was deliberately NOT given its own DocType (no lettered test calls for one) — folded into `Meat Processing Batch` as a live-weight -> carcass-weight -> cut-weight two-stage yield (MP02), each stage with its own independent over-yield gate. MP04 (QC hold/release) is a genuinely NEW explicit enforcement gate Seafood's own kin never needed a lettered test for — modeled directly after Premix's Weighing Verification Pending/Verified gate and 3PL's quarantine-release gate: `Meat Processing Batch.qc_status` starts "On Hold", and `Meat Packing Lot.validate` BLOCKS insert unless the batch is "Released" — proven live (not just asserted) by attempting the real pack-while-On-Hold insert before the real release, leaving a distinctively-marked (`carton_count=9999`) artifact-free negative test a later verify pass can positively confirm never persisted (the same "no submitted doc left behind" idiom Ingredient Trading's FT05 used). MP07 (recall) goes further than Seafood's SP08 (which only proved a `QMS Recall` record exists): a real `get_meat_recall_impact()` forward-traversal function directly answers the master plan's literal wording — "given a specific incoming lot ..., identify every finished/distributed lot that must be recalled" — reusing Golden Demo #3's `QMS Recall` doctype as-is for the drill record itself, the 7th distinct golden demo to do so this session.

**One real bug found and fixed, caught by reading the seed step's own reported result rather than trusting "status: ok" alone** (exactly the discipline this session's lessons exist to enforce): `Meat Packing Lot.total_packed_weight_kg` was declared `read_only` but nothing ever computed it server-side, so the first `test.demo.local` seed run silently saved it as `None` — visible only because the seed step's own returned message happened to print the computed total inline ("62 cartons x 20.0kg = Nonekg"). Fixed by adding the computation (`carton_count * net_weight_per_carton_kg`) to `meat_packing_lot_validate()` alongside its existing MP04 gate check, then repairing the one already-persisted record directly (a derived read-only field, safe to backfill via `frappe.db.set_value`). Confirmed fixed on the very first `pharmacountry.vn` seed run afterward (correctly showed "1240.0kg" with no manual repair needed there), and reconfirmed via 3+ consecutive idempotent re-runs on both sites.

## Phase 6A — AI Demo Expansion (master plan §19E "AI & AUTOMATION DEMO STRATEGY" — 9 items: AI-DEMO-01 Executive Assistant, AI-DEMO-02 QMS Copilot, AI-DEMO-03 DMS revision comparison, AI-DEMO-05 Manufacturing Insight, AI-DEMO-06 Procurement comparison, AI-DEMO-09/10 Farm/Aquaculture Insight, permission-aware RAG, Tool Registry, evaluation datasets. Started 2026-09-29 per "tiếp tục theo thứ tự" immediately after Phase 6 completed — master plan's own framing: "Sau khi các Golden Demo có business data thật" (now that the golden demos have real business data). **COMPLETE 2026-09-29** — all 9 items done, all verified on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn`.)

### AI-DEMO-01 — Executive Assistant + Tool Registry (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-691..695; master plan §19E lines ~3154-3181, the first Phase 6A item, built directly on top of the existing Phase 2A / CE-13 AI foundation — Golden Demo #8's AI provider/model/policy/logging architecture (`ai_core.py`, `ai_seeds.py`) — reused completely UNMODIFIED in its core mock adapter, extended (not redesigned) only where the existing schema already anticipated tool-calling)

**Mandatory architecture shape proven end-to-end**: `User -> Ask Enterprise -> AI Action -> approved tools/reports -> structured business data -> AI synthesis`, with the master plan's explicit "Không cho AI arbitrary SQL" constraint enforced structurally, not just by convention — `ask_enterprise()` (new `ai_executive_assistant.py`) resolves a small FIXED `question_code -> AI Tool` mapping (5 known demo questions, deliberately NOT free-text NLP intent classification — this demo proves the architecture, not an NLP layer), looks up each tool from the new `AI Tool` registry DocType, and calls ONLY that tool's registered Python function — there is no code path from a user's question to a raw SQL string anywhere in this module. **Tool Registry (`AI Tool` DocType)**: a deliberately minimal registry — `tool_code` (unique), `label`, `description`, `python_function_path` (dotted path), `required_permission_doctype` (documents which DocType's permissions gate the tool), `enabled` — a list of what's ALLOWED, not the implementation itself, designed to be extended (not just used once) by the next 8 AI-DEMO items in this phase. **5 tool implementations** (`ai_tools.py`), each real, read-only, and reusing existing golden-demo data verified by reading actual DocType JSON/seed files before writing any query (never guessed): `get_overdue_capas()` (Golden Demo #2 QMS `QMS CAPA`, mirrors the exact overdue predicate already proven in `api.escalate_overdue_capas()`), `get_batches_on_hold()` (the warehouse-name-substring "Quarantine" convention shared by Golden Demo #24 3PL / #9 Feed / #26 Premix / #22 Med Device), `get_near_expiry_inventory()` (generalizes Golden Demo #1 Pharma's PD07 near-expiry pattern beyond one hardcoded item), `get_worst_fcr_pond()` (real FCR data from Golden Demo #17 Fish Farm's `Fish Harvest` AND Golden Demo #8 Shrimp Farm's `Shrimp Harvest`, both queried and merged), and `get_revenue_trend_explanation_data()` (Golden Demo #25 Consumer Distribution's Sales Invoice data, broken down by Territory — the same axis as the existing `get_consumer_dist_territory_sales()`). **Every single query in every tool goes through `frappe.get_list()` with an explicit `user=` kwarg — never `frappe.get_all()`/raw SQL** (this session's own established lesson, applied here as the central design constraint, not an afterthought): confirmed empirically by reading `frappe/model/db_query.py` that `DatabaseQuery.execute(user=...)` performs real permission-checking AS that user without needing to mutate `frappe.session.user`, and that a child-table doctype (`Serial and Batch Entry`, needed because Stock Ledger Entry has no direct `batch_no` column in this ERPNext version) requires an explicit `parent_doctype="Serial and Batch Bundle"` kwarg or every query throws `Insufficient Permission` (found live, not guessed). **Permission-aware proof, concrete and empirical, not asserted**: reuses Golden Demo #24 (3PL)'s already-seeded Client A/Client B portal users (each restricted via a `Warehouse` User Permission with `apply_to_all_doctypes=1`) — asking the identical question ("Batch nào đang bị hold?") as each user through `ask_enterprise()` returns genuinely DIFFERENT, non-overlapping warehouse sets (Client A -> `Client A Quarantine - D3PL` only, Client B -> `Client B Quarantine - D3PL` only), checked directly against the returned `data_facts` AND the `sources` citation list (not trusting a status flag), on both sites. **Fact vs. interpretation, structurally separated, not blended**: the response shape carries `data_facts` (real tool output, a structured dict) and `ai_interpretation` (the — deliberately still MOCK, per the hard constraint below — model's own narrative string) as two distinct top-level fields, plus `sources` (citations) and `tools_called`/`provider`/`model`/`fallback_used`/`job_log`. **`AI Job Log` extended, not redesigned**: the doctype already had unused `tool_calls`/`retrieved_sources` Small Text fields since Phase 2A (built in anticipation of exactly this) — `ai_core.run_ai_action()` gained two new OPTIONAL kwargs (`tool_calls`, `retrieved_sources`) that populate them when passed, with every existing call site (including all 3 of Phase 2A's own `_test_*` validations) behaving identically when omitted; `_call_provider_adapter()` itself — the deliberate MOCK — was left completely untouched, per the explicit hard constraint against wiring any real LLM API/credentials into this demo.

**One real bug found and fixed, caught only by testing against `pharmacountry.vn`'s own accumulated history rather than trusting the dev-site pass**: the synthetic multi-month revenue-history Sales Invoices this demo seeds (no other golden demo seeds genuinely multi-month-dated Sales Invoices — every prior seed lands on `nowdate()`, confirmed by reading every seed module before writing this one) tripped ERPNext's own native `check_credit_limit()` on `pharmacountry.vn` — that site's Hanoi dealer customer already had enough OTHER accumulated invoice history (from repeated demo runs) that this seed's own additional invoice pushed it over its 15,000,000 VND credit limit, a threshold `test.demo.local` hadn't reached yet. `bench migrate` failed cleanly mid-`after_migrate` (Frappe's request-scoped transaction rolled back completely — confirmed zero partial `AI Tool`/Sales Invoice records were left behind on `pharmacountry.vn`, nothing to manually clean up). Fixed with a new `_ensure_credit_headroom()` helper that raises (never lowers) the company-scoped `Customer Credit Limit` child-table row to 100,000,000 VND before creating the seed invoices — this demo's own data no longer depends on how much unrelated invoice history happens to already exist on a given site. A second, smaller issue (not a bug, a design gap): `get_revenue_trend_explanation_data()` without a `company` filter aggregates Sales Invoices across EVERY golden demo's company, diluting the deliberate month-over-month decline story — fixed by adding a `_QUESTION_DEFAULT_PARAMS` mapping in `ask_enterprise()` (question_code -> default kwargs, always overridable by an explicit caller-supplied param) scoping `revenue_trend` to Golden Demo #25's company by default.

All checks pass on both sites with matching figures (this-month VND 12,422,500 vs. prior-month VND 14,100,000, identical on both); confirmed idempotent across 3+ consecutive full-registry re-runs on both sites; the full **29-function regression sweep (all 28 prior golden demos/AI foundation + this)** re-confirmed clean on both sites. |

**Deliberately NOT done here, by explicit instruction** — kept `_call_provider_adapter()` exactly as the Phase 2A mock left it: no real LLM API call, no real API keys/credentials, no live external network call. This demo proves the ARCHITECTURE (routing, permission enforcement, tool-call auditing, fact/interpretation separation), not a live model — if real API wiring would meaningfully improve this specific demo, that is a cost/credential decision left to the user, not made unilaterally here.

### AI-DEMO-02 — QMS Copilot (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-696..700; master plan §19E lines ~3183-3192, the second Phase 6A item, built directly on top of AI-DEMO-01's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call, no parallel plumbing)

**Same mandatory architecture shape as AI-DEMO-01**, extended with the ONE new thing this demo exists to prove: **human approval bắt buộc (mandatory)**, per master plan principle #24 (line ~3690, "AI output ở regulated workflow mặc định là suggestion/draft, không auto-final approval"). `run_qms_copilot(use_case, source_reference, user, **params)` (new `ai_qms_copilot.py`) mirrors `ask_enterprise()`'s orchestration almost exactly — a fixed `use_case -> AI Tool` mapping, real tool calls AS the user, real tool output fed into the unmodified `run_ai_action()` — but EVERY call additionally persists a new, generic `AI Draft` DocType row that starts life as `Draft` and can only leave that status via an explicit, attributable human review. **3 new tool implementations** (`ai_tools.py`, appended to the SAME registry, not a parallel one): `get_deviation_detail()` (full `QMS Deviation` + any linked `QMS CAPA`), `find_similar_deviations()` (real, explainable, NON-vector similarity: keyword overlap across subject/description/root_cause + a same-severity bonus — deliberately NOT the later "Permission-aware RAG" Phase 6A item, nothing embedding-based to fake), `get_audit_finding_summary_data()` (a `QMS Audit` plus all its `QMS Audit Finding` child rows, requiring the same `parent_doctype="QMS Audit"` child-table kwarg lesson AI-DEMO-01 established). All 3 confirmed against REAL `QMS Deviation`/`QMS CAPA`/`QMS Audit` schemas read from the actual DocType JSON first (this codebase's `QMS Deviation` has no `deviation_type`/`department`/`item` fields, so similarity is built on what genuinely exists: `severity` + free-text fields — a real architectural fork resolved by reading source, not guessing per the master-plan spec's own suggested field names). **5 use cases, all 5 requiring approval** (a real design decision, documented in the code, not made silently): summarize deviation, find similar deviation, suggest investigation questions, draft CAPA, summarize audit finding. The master plan's "Human approval bắt buộc" wording is stated at the COPILOT level, not scoped only to CAPA-drafting — an unreviewed AI summary sitting on a Deviation record reads exactly like human-authored QA text to anyone who opens it later, so treating summaries as "just display it, no review needed" was judged the more convenient but less defensible reading. The one real structural distinction that DOES matter and IS enforced in code: only `draft_capa`'s approval (`approve_capa_draft()`) mints a brand-new business record (a real `QMS CAPA`); the other 4 use cases' approval (`approve_ai_suggestion()`) endorses the draft text with NO new record created — a deliberate refusal in each function prevents calling the wrong one for the wrong use case.

**New shared, generic `AI Draft` DocType** (`doctype/ai_draft/`) — deliberately copilot-agnostic (`copilot_code`, `use_case`, `source_doctype`/`source_reference` Dynamic Link, `content`, `suggested_fields` JSON, `status` Draft/Approved/Rejected, `generated_by_job_log` Link to AI Job Log, `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_on`/`review_notes`, `resulting_doctype`/`resulting_reference` Dynamic Link) so a future DMS Copilot (AI-DEMO-03) or any other Phase 6A item needing "AI produced a draft, a human must review it" reuses this SAME table and the SAME generic helpers (`ai_drafts.py`'s `create_ai_draft()`/`decide_ai_draft()`) instead of building its own parallel schema — this was the explicit design goal, not an afterthought. **Two structural guarantees enforced at the DocType level** (`ai_drafts.ai_draft_validate()`, wired via `hooks.py` doc_events on "AI Draft", the exact style already used for QMS's own Q01/Q02/Q04), not just left to caller discipline: (1) a draft cannot leave 'Draft' status without `reviewed_by` on record — structurally impossible for AI output to self-approve; (2) a decided draft (Approved/Rejected) cannot be silently re-decided by a later save. **The mandatory-approval proof, concrete and empirical**: (a) a fresh `draft_capa` AI Draft produces NO QMS CAPA until `approve_capa_draft()` is explicitly called (checked via a before/after CAPA-count delta, not just a status flag); (b) approving mints exactly one new real CAPA, rejecting mints none — checked directly against `QMS CAPA` row counts on both sites; (c) approval is attributed to `quality.director@pharmacountry.vn`, a specific real human user reused from Golden Demo #3's own CAPA-owner/closer segregation precedent — never the AI, never left blank; (d) both DocType-level guard rails (no self-approval, no re-deciding a finalized draft) empirically fire as `frappe.ValidationError`, confirmed by direct negative tests, not just documented as intended behavior.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during this build, both caught by validation before reaching a "done" state**: (1) the seed data for the "unrelated deviation" negative control originally included the literal word "cold" in a sentence meant to say it was UNRELATED to cold-chain equipment — an ironic self-inflicted keyword collision that made the similarity search flag it as similar to the cold-storage deviation; fixed by rewording the seed text to remove all accidental vocabulary overlap, re-verified with a clean pass. (2) a more serious cross-contamination bug: `draft_capa`'s repeated approvals were targeting the SAME "Warehouse B" `QMS Deviation` that Golden Demo #2/#3's OWN pre-existing `qms_seeds._ensure_capa_for_deviation()` uses for an un-ordered `frappe.db.get_value("QMS CAPA", {"source_type": ..., "source_reference": ...})` lookup that assumes AT MOST ONE CAPA exists per deviation — this demo's own append-only `AI Draft`/`QMS CAPA` accumulation (the same precedent as AI Job Log, expected and by design) violated that unrelated assumption, causing repeated `bench migrate`/validation runs to create several extra same-keyed CAPAs and making the golden demo's own idempotency check nondeterministically pick up and re-close an ARBITRARY freshly-AI-approved CAPA instead of its real flagship one (caught live on `test.demo.local` — 7 duplicate CAPAs had already accumulated, several incorrectly auto-closed, before this was noticed; `verify_qms_golden_demo()` happened to still report all-passed throughout, meaning this would NOT have been caught by the regression sweep alone). Fixed by retargeting `draft_capa` (and every approval test/verify check touching it) to the AI-DEMO-02-owned "Warehouse C" similarity-control Deviation instead — a record nothing else in the codebase assumes CAPA-uniqueness over — and by manually cleaning up the 7 polluted duplicate CAPA rows plus their associated AI Draft rows on `test.demo.local` before re-verifying. Confirmed clean afterward: the golden demo's own CAPA count for Warehouse B stayed at exactly 1 across 5+ additional re-runs on both sites, and `verify_qms_golden_demo()` continued to pass throughout.

All checks pass on both sites; confirmed idempotent across 5+ consecutive full-registry re-runs on both sites (including the Warehouse B CAPA-count-stays-1 regression proof); the full **30-function regression sweep (all 29 prior golden demos/AI foundation/AI-DEMO-01 + this)** re-confirmed clean on both sites, zero cross-demo regressions. |

**Deliberately NOT done here, by explicit instruction** — same hard constraint as AI-DEMO-01: `_call_provider_adapter()` left completely untouched, no real LLM API call, no real credentials, no live external network call. The deterministic CAPA-field pre-fill (`_suggest_capa_fields()` — due-date offset by severity, action-plan templating from root_cause) is plain business-rule logic, NOT a substitute LLM call — it runs entirely separately from, and in addition to, the one (still mocked) `run_ai_action()` synthesis call, mirroring how a real copilot would combine rule-based structured pre-fill with an LLM narrative.

### AI-DEMO-03 — DMS Copilot (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-701..704; master plan §19E lines ~3194-3201, the third Phase 6A item, built directly on top of AI-DEMO-01/02's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call, the SAME generic `AI Draft` DocType from AI-DEMO-02, no parallel plumbing)

**Same mandatory architecture shape as AI-DEMO-01/02**: `run_dms_copilot(use_case, source_reference, user, **params)` (new `ai_dms_copilot.py`) mirrors `run_qms_copilot()`'s orchestration almost exactly — a fixed `use_case -> AI Tool` mapping, real tool calls AS the user, real tool output fed into the unmodified `run_ai_action()`, every call persisting a new `AI Draft` row (the SAME shared DocType AI-DEMO-02 built specifically so a future copilot like this one could reuse it, not a parallel table). **4 new tool implementations** (`ai_tools.py`, appended to the SAME registry — 12 tools total now): `compare_document_revisions()` (real field-level diff between two `DMS Document Version` rows — status/content_summary/reviewed_by/approved_by/effective_date/obsolete_date — deliberately a structured comparison, not a text-diff algorithm), `suggest_impacted_documents()` (real relationship-based lookup — this schema's `DMS Document` has no explicit cross-reference field, confirmed by reading `dms_document.json` before designing this, so impact is proxied via same `department`/`doc_type`, each candidate stating its basis explicitly, never a black-box score), `suggest_training_impact()` (real query over `DMS Training Assignment` for a document's current Effective version — reads the real rows `dms_validations.dms_document_version_on_update()` already auto-creates, never fabricates a training list), and `search_effective_documents()` (real, non-vector keyword search — reuses AI-DEMO-02's already-generic `_tokenize()` as-is — restricted to `DMS Document Version.status == 'Effective'` AND parent `DMS Document.status == 'Effective'`, with any Obsolete/Draft match that ALSO shares keywords reported separately in `obsolete_or_draft_matches_excluded`, never inside `results` — the mechanism this demo's own empirical exclusion proof is built on). **5 use cases, all 5 requiring approval — a design decision documented in `ai_dms_copilot.py`'s own module docstring, not made silently**: the master plan gives no explicit "Human approval bắt buộc" sentence for THIS demo the way it did for QMS Copilot, so this build applied master plan principle #24's general wording (line ~3690, regulated-workflow AI output defaults to suggestion/draft) directly, consistent with AI-DEMO-02's own precedent. The most tempting exception — exempting `suggest_impacted_documents` as "just a data-fact listing" — was deliberately rejected for the same reason AI-DEMO-02 rejected exempting `summarize_deviation`: an unreviewed AI-generated impact list sitting next to a real change-control record reads exactly like a QA-authored assessment to anyone who opens it later, and a relationship PROXY (not a verified cross-reference) can be wrong in ways that matter in a regulated document-control process. **The one genuine structural distinction from QMS Copilot, enforced in code**: NONE of DMS Copilot's 5 use cases mint a new business record on approval (a revision comparison, a change summary, an impacted-documents list, a training-impact assessment, and a Q&A answer are all "interpret real data, a human decides what to do next" outputs, unlike QMS's `draft_capa`) — so there is no DMS-Copilot equivalent of `approve_capa_draft()`; every use case goes through the single generic `approve_dms_draft()`/`reject_dms_draft()` pair, which adds its own `copilot_code` guard (refuses to approve/reject a draft belonging to a different copilot, e.g. `qms_copilot`) on top of `ai_drafts.py`'s existing DocType-level guards. **Q&A over effective documents is explicitly NOT the later "Permission-aware RAG" Phase 6A item** (no embeddings — the master plan's own distinction, preserved deliberately) **and is not anchored to one authored record** — its `source_reference` is a free-text search query, not a `DMS Document` code, so its `AI Draft` row is persisted with `source_doctype`/`source_reference` left empty (both fields are optional on `AI Draft`, not `reqd`) rather than pointing at an arbitrary single document.

**Extra demo data, all confirmed novel by reading `dms_seeds.py` in full before designing anything** (Golden Demo #4's own seed data has exactly ONE document, `SOP-WH-02`, with 2 versions — nothing to relate it to or search across without this): 4 new `DMS Document` rows spanning all 4 relationship-basis combinations relative to SOP-WH-02 (`SOP-WH-05` same department+type, `FORM-WH-01` same department only, `SOP-QA-01` same type only, `POLICY-HR-01` a genuine negative control sharing neither), and 2 of SOP-WH-02's own already-auto-created `DMS Training Assignment` rows flipped `completed=1` (a direct field update on EXISTING rows, never a new one) so `suggest_training_impact()` has a genuinely non-empty "needs retraining" answer instead of a trivially-empty one. **The Q&A effective-vs-obsolete exclusion proof needed NO new seed data at all** — SOP-WH-02's own real v1 ("Review cold storage logs weekly.", now Obsolete) and v2 ("...cold storage logs daily...", Effective) already share the keywords "cold"/"storage"/"logs", so searching for "cold storage logs" empirically proves the exclusion using data Golden Demo #4 already seeds: v1 is still found by the tokenizer (proving the search itself works, not just silently skipping it) but is reported exclusively in `obsolete_or_draft_matches_excluded`, never in `results` — directly mirroring the master plan's own RAG "Obsolete doc not used as current" requirement even though this isn't the RAG demo itself.

**No new bugs found during this build** — the first of the 3 Phase 6A copilots so far to reach a clean first-pass verify on both sites, attributed directly to proactively applying this session's own prior lessons instead of re-discovering them: `dms_seeds.py`/`dms_validations.py` were read in FULL before writing a single query (confirmed `DMS Training Assignment` and `DMS Controlled Print Log` are ordinary standalone DocTypes, not child tables, so no `parent_doctype` kwarg is needed, unlike `QMS Audit Finding`); every tool query used `frappe.get_list(..., user=user)`, never `get_all()`; and — the specific lesson AI-DEMO-02's own cross-contamination bug taught — before designing any approval logic, `dms_seeds.py`'s own idempotency assumptions were checked first, which is exactly what led to the design decision that NONE of DMS Copilot's use cases mint a new record: there was no dedicated "safe target record" needed (the AI-DEMO-02 fix pattern of retargeting to a demo-owned record like "Warehouse C") because there is no new-record-creation code path to collide with anything in the first place. Confirmed empirically, not just by design intent: `verify_dms_golden_demo()` re-run 3+ times on both sites throughout this build never moved off `all_passed: true`, and direct row-count checks on `DMS Document`/`DMS Document Version`/`DMS Training Assignment`/`DMS Controlled Print Log`/`QMS CAPA` stayed byte-for-byte identical across 3+ seed re-runs, 2+ validation re-runs, repeated verify re-runs, and a full `bench migrate` cycle on both sites (only `AI Draft`/`AI Job Log` grew, exactly as expected for their own append-only design).

### AI-DEMO-05 — Manufacturing Insight (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-705..708; master plan §19E lines ~3213-3219, the fourth Phase 6A item, built directly on top of AI-DEMO-01/02/03's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call; note AI-DEMO-04 "LIMS Insight" is intentionally skipped — it is not one of the curated Phase 6A items, only AI-DEMO-01/02/03/05/06/09/10 plus RAG/Tool-Registry/eval-dataset items are)

**Design decision, documented in `ai_manufacturing_insight.py`'s own module docstring, not made silently: this demo follows AI-DEMO-01 (Executive Assistant)'s NO-DRAFT shape, not AI-DEMO-02/03 (QMS/DMS Copilot)'s `AI Draft`/human-approval shape.** The master plan's own text for this demo carries no "Human approval bắt buộc" sentence the way it does for QMS Copilot; unlike DMS Copilot (which chose to apply the draft workflow anyway, reading principle #24 broadly, because EVERY one of its use cases authors interpretive text attached to a real controlled-document record), Manufacturing Insight's own name is the tell — "Insight," not "Copilot." All 4 use cases (explain production delay, yield anomaly analysis, batch-record completeness review, downtime summary) are READ-ONLY analytical questions answered from real structured data and handed back to the asking user, exactly AI-DEMO-01's `ask_enterprise()` shape — none of them author content that gets attached to, or could be mistaken for, human-authored documentation on another record; even the two most compliance-adjacent use cases (yield anomaly, batch completeness) only flag/compute against a stated real threshold or a stated real required-record list, they never decide or authorize anything. `ask_manufacturing_insight()` (new `ai_manufacturing_insight.py`) mirrors `ask_enterprise()`'s orchestration exactly — a fixed `question_code -> AI Tool` mapping, real tool calls AS the calling user, real tool output fed into the unmodified `run_ai_action()` — with a response shape structurally IDENTICAL to `ask_enterprise()`'s (`data_facts`/`ai_interpretation`/`sources`/`tools_called`/…), proven empirically by asserting no `ai_suggestion` key exists anywhere in the response, not just documented as intended.

**4 new tool implementations** (`ai_tools.py`, appended to the SAME registry — 16 tools total now), all reusing Golden Demo #26 (Premix)'s real `Work Order`/`Batch`/`Quality Inspection`/`Premix Weighing Verification` machinery, schemas confirmed by reading `premix_seeds.py`/`premix_validations.py` and by querying a real Work Order doc on a live site first (confirming genuine `planned_start_date`/`planned_end_date`/`actual_start_date`/`actual_end_date` fields exist, and that `planned_end_date` is never auto-populated by the native flow): `explain_production_delay()` (real `planned_end_date` vs `actual_end_date` comparison, correlated against that SAME Work Order's own real `Premix Weighing Verification.verified_on` timing when it lands after `planned_end_date` — a REAL, directly-recorded contributing factor, never a guess, with an honest "no correlated cause found" fallback when none exists); `analyze_yield_anomalies()` (real `Work Order.qty` vs `produced_qty` across multiple batches of the same item, flagged against a stated `threshold_percent`, default 5%); `check_batch_record_completeness()` (a real per-batch audit: submitted Accepted Quality Inspection, traceable Manufacture Stock Entry/Work Order, and — for each `Item.requires_second_check=1` critical ingredient in that Work Order's BOM — a Verified four-eyes `Premix Weighing Verification`); `summarize_downtime()` (a real Work Order timing-gap AGGREGATE, explicitly labeled — in the tool's own returned `methodology_note` — as a DERIVED PROXY, `max(0, actual_end_date - planned_end_date)`, never presented as a native recorded "downtime" fact, since no manufacturing golden demo in this codebase records one — confirmed by grep before writing this). Every query is `frappe.get_list(..., user=user)`-only, including two new permission-checked child-table helpers (`_batch_for_manufacture_stock_entry()`, reused inline for BOM Item/Serial and Batch Entry lookups) following this session's own established `parent_doctype=` idiom.

**A real architectural finding that shaped the whole seed design, caught by reading the code first, not by trial and error**: `premix_seeds.py`'s own `_ensure_work_order()` does an un-ordered `frappe.db.exists("Work Order", {"production_item": "PREMIX-BROILER-2PCT", "docstatus": 1})`, and `api.verify_premix_golden_demo()`/`premix_seeds._finished_batch()` do `frappe.db.get_value("Batch", {"item": "PREMIX-BROILER-2PCT"}, "name", order_by="creation desc")` — both are "at most one" assumptions over the SAME flagship item/Work Order this new demo would otherwise need to add MORE Work Orders/batches to (there is no genuine planned-vs-actual timing variance or yield variance across the single real flagship batch to compare against). Adding more Work Orders/batches for the REAL `PREMIX-BROILER-2PCT` item would have deterministically corrupted `verify_premix_golden_demo()`'s own `order_by="creation desc"` batch lookup — exactly AI-DEMO-02's own "Warehouse C isolation" lesson, re-derived independently here rather than assumed. **Fix, applied from the start rather than discovered by a failure**: all of this demo's new Work Order/batch data lives under a brand-new, AI-DEMO-05-owned FG item, `PREMIX-BROILER-2PCT-AI5` (same company, same warehouses, same 6-ingredient recipe/ratios/sequence, same already-flagged critical/micro ingredients — still real Premix GMP business logic, just on an isolated parallel production line), which every one of Premix's own existing lookups is structurally incapable of matching (they filter on the exact literal `"PREMIX-BROILER-2PCT"` string). Confirmed empirically, not just by design: `verify_premix_golden_demo()` re-run 3+ times on both sites after this demo's data was added never moved off `all_passed: true`, and `verify_ai_manufacturing_insight_golden_demo()` itself asserts this as one of its own checks (re-running `verify_premix_golden_demo()` live and requiring it to still pass).

**4 real, isolated Work Order scenarios seeded** (`ai_manufacturing_insight_seeds.py`), each deliberately isolating ONE tool's positive test case from the others (mirroring `premix_seeds.py`'s own PM01/PM02/PM03 "isolate the cause" test discipline): `WO-A-ONTIME` (on-time, 100% yield — the negative-control baseline), `WO-B-DELAYED` (planned to finish 5 days before the seed ran, actually finishes today — a genuine ~5-day/120-hour delay — with its critical ingredient's four-eyes `Premix Weighing Verification.verified_on` deliberately set only 1 day before completion, a real fact `explain_production_delay()` correlates against), `WO-C-YIELD-ANOMALY` (on-time; FG output declared at 430kg against a 500kg Work Order — 86% yield, full BOM-scale raw-material consumption still occurs, a real mass-balance yield-loss scenario, not a fabricated number — beyond the 5% threshold), `WO-D-YIELD-NORMAL-VARIANCE` (on-time; 480kg declared, 96% yield, 4% deviation — WITHIN the threshold, proving genuine discrimination rather than a blanket "under 100%" rule). All 4 pass through the SAME real PM01 (±1% micro-weigh tolerance)/PM02 (four-eyes double-check)/PM03 (mixing sequence) gates the flagship batch does — nothing was bypassed to make them succeed. Each is fully documented (Accepted Quality Inspection, traceable Manufacture Stock Entry) so `check_batch_record_completeness()` has 4 real COMPLETE batches to contrast against its ONE positive test case: a deliberately-incomplete standalone `Batch` record (no Work Order, no Quality Inspection, no Premix Weighing Verification at all — never routed through a real manufacture flow, which would legitimately trigger PM01-04 and could never actually produce an incomplete real batch of this item, since Golden Demo #26's own validations are airtight by design).

**One real ERPNext gotcha found and fixed during this build**: a MANUALLY-constructed Manufacture-purpose Stock Entry (needed for `WO-C`/`WO-D`'s yield-loss scenarios, where FG output must be decoupled from BOM-scaled RM consumption — something the native `make_stock_entry()` mapper doesn't allow) succeeded at `insert()` but threw "For Quantity (Manufactured Qty) is mandatory" at `submit()`. Root cause, found by reading `stock_entry.py` source directly rather than guessing: `Stock Entry.validate()` contains `if not self.from_bom: self.fg_completed_qty = 0.0` — a manually-built entry's `from_bom` Check field defaults falsy, so ERPNext silently zeroes out the just-set `fg_completed_qty` during the entry's OWN first `validate()` call (which runs at `insert()`, AFTER the mandatory-qty check already passed using the original value) — the zeroed value is what a subsequent `submit()`'s re-validation then correctly rejects. Fixed by explicitly setting `from_bom=1` (and `bom_no`) on the manual entry, exactly what the native mapper always sets for free. Caught immediately by the FIRST seed run (transaction rolled back cleanly, zero partial records left on `test.demo.local` — confirmed via direct row-count query before retrying), not discovered later during verification.

### AI-DEMO-06 — Procurement Assistant (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-709..712; master plan §19E lines ~3221-3229, the fifth Phase 6A item, built directly on top of AI-DEMO-01/02/03/05's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call, the SAME generic `AI Draft` DocType from AI-DEMO-02)

**A deliberate HYBRID design, documented in `ai_procurement_assistant.py`'s own module docstring — the first Phase 6A item that is NEITHER all-draft (QMS/DMS Copilot) NOR all-no-draft (Manufacturing Insight).** The master plan's own text for this demo gives 4 purely analytical use cases (extract supplier quotation, normalize terms, compare quotations, summarize price/delivery/payment/quality history) plus ONE narrower, explicit hard constraint: **"AI không tự approve supplier"** (the AI must NEVER itself approve/qualify a supplier) — structurally different from QMS Copilot's blanket "Human approval bắt buộc" (every output needs review): this names ONE specific consequential DECISION (a supplier's qualification status), not every piece of output. So: `extract_quotation`/`normalize_terms`/`compare_quotations`/`summarize_supplier_history` follow AI-DEMO-05 (Manufacturing Insight)'s NO-DRAFT shape exactly (`ask_procurement_assistant()` is structurally identical to `ask_manufacturing_insight()`) — none of the 4 mint or authorize anything, they only answer real analytical questions handed back to the asking user. `recommend_supplier_qualification` (the 5th use case) is the ONE case that reuses AI-DEMO-02's `AI Draft` mechanism, modeled directly on `run_qms_copilot()`'s `draft_capa`/`approve_capa_draft()` pair — the closest precedent, an AI Draft that, once approved by a real human, changes a real consequential record (here `Supplier.quality_status` instead of minting a new `QMS CAPA`).

**5 new tool implementations** (`ai_tools.py`, appended to the SAME registry — 21 tools total now), reusing Golden Demo #27 (Feed / Ingredient Trading)'s real multi-supplier, multi-currency Purchase Order/Purchase Receipt/Purchase Invoice/Quality Inspection data, schemas confirmed by reading the actual DocType meta on a live site before writing any query (`Supplier Quotation` has NO native `payment_terms_template` field — confirmed empirically, unlike Purchase Order/Purchase Invoice which do — so a quotation's payment terms stay in its own native `terms` Text Editor free-text field, never restructured into a fabricated schema that doesn't exist): `extract_supplier_quotation()` (honestly documented as SIMULATING extraction from an already-structured `Supplier Quotation` record — this platform has no live document/PDF/email OCR/parsing capability, and the tool's own docstring/notes say so explicitly rather than fabricating that capability); `normalize_supplier_quotations()` (converts each quotation's unit price to Company base currency via its OWN `conversion_rate` — the exact FX pattern Golden Demo #27's own FT02 already established and verified, reused rather than reinvented — plus incoterm/lead_time_days/`Item.min_order_qty` side by side); `compare_supplier_quotations()` (a real, structured side-by-side comparison built on the normalized data, stating which supplier wins on each real dimension, never a black-box score); `summarize_supplier_history()` (a real aggregation over a supplier's actual Purchase Order/Purchase Receipt/Purchase Invoice/Quality Inspection history — price trend, on-time delivery rate via real `Purchase Receipt.posting_date` vs. that same shipment's own `Purchase Order Item.schedule_date`, payment terms actually used via the real `Purchase Order.payment_terms_template`, QC pass/fail rate with the LATEST inspection reported separately); and `recommend_supplier_qualification_change()` (the ONE consequential tool — a pure RECOMMENDATION computed from real history facts plus real `Supplier.quality_status`/`is_critical_supplier` fields against 2 stated, explainable thresholds, mirroring `analyze_yield_anomalies()`'s own "coarse, easily explained threshold" discipline — this function has NO write call anywhere in its body, proven both by its own docstring and by a static source-level check in both the seed validations and `verify_ai_procurement_assistant_golden_demo()`).

**The "AI cannot self-approve a supplier" structural proof, demonstrated negatively, not just asserted** — this demo's single most important acceptance criterion: (1) a live before/after read shows `recommend_supplier_qualification()` itself NEVER changes `Supplier.quality_status`, even though it invokes the (mock) AI synthesis step; (2) the fresh recommendation is always persisted as a pending `AI Draft` (`requires_human_approval=True`, `approval_status='Draft'`), never auto-applied; (3) `approve_supplier_status_draft()` — modeled on `approve_capa_draft()` — is the ONLY function anywhere in this codebase that ever writes `Supplier.quality_status` as a consequence of an AI recommendation, and it requires an explicit `reviewed_by` that must resolve to a REAL `User` record (both this function's own check AND, a second time, `ai_drafts.ai_draft_validate()`'s existing DocType-level no-self-approval guard, reused unmodified); (4) every guard fires empirically: no `reviewed_by` blocked, a bogus/non-existent `reviewed_by` string blocked, a raw self-approval save (flip to Approved without `reviewed_by`) blocked, re-deciding an already-Approved draft blocked; (5) a STATIC, source-level proof (via `inspect.getsource()`, with each function's own docstring prose stripped first so the check can't false-positive on itself) confirms the read-only tool and the orchestration entry point contain ZERO Supplier-write calls, and `approve_supplier_status_draft()` contains EXACTLY ONE, gated behind the `reviewed_by` check. A REAL positive control proves the tool distinguishes good and bad suppliers rather than just flagging everything: Golden Demo #27's own untouched, real Cargill Asia Trading data (`quality_status="Pending"`, one clean on-time Accepted-QC shipment) recommends an UPGRADE to `Approved` — deliberately never applied by this demo's own seed validations or verify function, so Cargill's real Supplier record stays untouched throughout (isolation discipline, checked directly, not assumed).

**Isolation, following this session's own AI-DEMO-02 "Warehouse C"/AI-DEMO-05 "PREMIX-BROILER-2PCT-AI5" lesson a third time**: `verify_ingredient_trading_golden_demo()` was read in full before writing anything — its 8 checks are all keyed to specific, differently-named suppliers/items/PO titles (Cargill/Nutreco, SOYBEAN-MEAL-48/FISH-MEAL-65, `IT-PO-SOY-01` etc.), and `get_ingredient_trading_margin_report()`'s own per-item aggregation is a super-set-safe `{"SOYBEAN-MEAL-48", "FISH-MEAL-65"} <= items_with_positive_margin` check, not an exact match — so this demo safely REUSES Golden Demo #27's Company/warehouses/USD-payable-account plumbing (and, read-only, Cargill's own real data) while creating a completely distinct, isolated Supplier (`"Mekong AgriSource Trading Co. (AI-DEMO-06)"`) and Item (`CORN-GLUTEN-MEAL-60`) nothing else in the codebase assumes uniqueness over, for the one flow that actually writes anything (the qualification-recommendation approval). 3 real Purchase Order -> Purchase Receipt -> Quality Inspection (-> Purchase Invoice, for the first 2) chains were seeded ~120/~75/~30 days apart with a genuinely WORSENING real trend on both delivery (on-time, then 11 days late, then 14 days late) and quality (Accepted comfortably in-spec, Accepted borderline, then genuinely Rejected via Quality Inspection's own NATIVE reading-vs-min/max auto-status computation — confirmed by reading `erpnext/stock/doctype/quality_inspection/quality_inspection.py`'s `validate()` before relying on this, not guessed) — real data the disqualification recommendation is honestly built on, not a fabricated narrative. 2 new `Supplier Quotation` records (the platform's first — confirmed by grep, no golden demo had ever used this doctype) give `normalize_supplier_quotations()`/`compare_supplier_quotations()` a genuine, non-contrived trade-off: Nutreco quotes a lower per-kg price even after FX conversion (10,437 vs. Cargill's 10,621 VND/kg) but a longer 35-day lead time vs. Cargill's faster 21 days.

**One real ERPNext gotcha found and fixed during this build, caught on the FIRST validation run rather than trusting the seed step's "created" self-report**: assigning `Purchase Receipt.posting_date`/`Purchase Invoice.posting_date` directly (exactly the same pattern already used successfully for `Purchase Order.transaction_date` in this same seed module) silently got RESET back to today at insert/submit — all 3 of the isolated supplier's Purchase Receipts landed on the same date, which then made `summarize_supplier_history()`'s "latest QC status" resolution nondeterministically pick an Accepted inspection instead of the real, intended Rejected one (caught immediately by `_test_supplier_history_real_decline()` asserting `latest_qc_status == "Rejected"` and getting `"Accepted"` back). Root cause, confirmed by direct experimentation on a live throwaway document rather than guessed: `Purchase Receipt`/`Purchase Invoice` also carry their own `set_posting_time` Check field (the same lever a real user flips via the "Edit Posting Date/Time" UI checkbox) — without it explicitly set to `1`, an assigned `posting_date` is silently discarded in favor of "today," while `Purchase Order.transaction_date` has no such gate and behaves as a plain editable field. Fixed by setting `set_posting_time = 1` alongside `posting_date` in both `_ensure_receipt_ai6()` and `_ensure_purchase_invoice_ai6()`; the 3 mis-dated records were cancelled/deleted and regenerated correctly (`2026-06-15` / `2026-08-10` / `2026-09-27`, genuinely ascending) before any further validation ran. A second, smaller issue (a test-harness bug, not a product bug): the static source-level "no write call" proof's naive substring scan on raw `inspect.getsource()` output initially false-positived on the target functions' OWN docstrings (which quote `.save()`/`.insert()` etc. IN PROSE, describing what must NOT be present) — fixed by stripping each function's leading triple-quoted docstring before scanning (`_strip_docstring()`), applied identically in both `ai_procurement_assistant_seeds.py` and `api.verify_ai_procurement_assistant_golden_demo()`.

### AI-DEMO-09/10 — Farm/Aquaculture Insight (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-713..716; master plan §19E lines ~3250-3268, the sixth Phase 6A item, built directly on top of AI-DEMO-01/02/03/05/06's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call. The master plan's own curated Phase 6A summary list treats "Farm/Aquaculture Insight" as ONE combined item even though its detailed spec numbers it as two sub-items — **AI-DEMO-09 (Livestock Farm Assistant)** and **AI-DEMO-10 (Shrimp/Aquaculture Assistant)** — built together here, as instructed, under one new module pair (`ai_farm_aquaculture_insight.py`/`ai_farm_aquaculture_insight_seeds.py`) with two entry points rather than one combined function)

**Design decision, documented in `ai_farm_aquaculture_insight.py`'s own module docstring: both verticals follow AI-DEMO-05 (Manufacturing Insight)'s NO-DRAFT shape**, per the task's own explicit instruction — all 8 use cases across both verticals (FCR deterioration explanation, mortality anomaly, barn/flock requiring attention, feed-cost analysis; pond instability analysis, feed/FCR trend, water trend analysis, anomaly explanation) are READ-ONLY analytical questions answered from real structured data and handed back to the asking user, never minted into or displayed alongside another record. Two entry points (`ask_livestock_insight()`/`ask_aquaculture_insight()`), not one combined function with a `vertical` parameter, because the two verticals have genuinely different question sets and default data scopes.

**"Automation handles hard thresholds. AI handles interpretation." — applied differently per vertical, because the underlying golden demos genuinely differ in what automation already exists (confirmed by reading each one's own validation module in full before writing a single tool, not assumed from the master plan's own suggested wording):** Golden Demo #8 (Shrimp Farm)'s `shrimp_validations.shrimp_water_reading_validate()` (SF10) ALREADY computes a real, deterministic `is_alert`/`alert_message` flag against fixed water-parameter thresholds on every `Shrimp Water Parameter Reading` — `analyze_pond_instability()`/`analyze_water_trend()` READ that existing flag and never recompute or duplicate the threshold check itself (a static, source-level check in `verify_ai_farm_aquaculture_insight_golden_demo()` confirms neither function's own source ever references `_WATER_THRESHOLDS`, docstrings stripped first so the check can't false-positive on itself); their own genuinely new contribution is a variance/trend analysis across readings that never individually crossed a threshold. Golden Demo #11 (Pig Farm)'s `pig_validations.py`, by contrast, was read in full and has NO FCR/mortality threshold-alert hook of any kind — there is no existing deterministic automation for `explain_fcr_deterioration()`/`analyze_mortality_anomaly()` to defer to, so (per the task's own instruction not to invent a new hard-threshold validation inside an "AI insight" tool function) neither tool was given one; instead both compare each cohort ONLY against the farm's own relative peer/trailing average ("this cohort's mortality is 3.33x the peer average," never a fixed "> X%" cutoff), computed transiently inside the read-only tool and never persisted as a blocking rule anywhere (a second static check confirms neither tool ever calls `frappe.throw`).

**Real data sources, chosen for richness over spreading thin, per the task's own guidance**: Golden Demo #11 (Pig Farm) for livestock — its `Pig Grower Batch` cohort structure (Feed Log/Weight Record/Mortality Record/Sale Lot, all keyed by `batch`) is directly comparable across cohorts, unlike Golden Demo #12 (Poultry)'s 2 existing flocks (Broiler vs. Layer — different feed products/scale/purpose, not comparable) or Golden Demo #13 (Cattle)'s individual-animal-only tracking (no cohort/mortality doctype at all). Golden Demo #8 (Shrimp Farm) for aquaculture — the ONLY aquaculture golden demo with real water-quality reading data (`Shrimp Water Parameter Reading`; confirmed Fish Farm/Aqua Hatchery/Aqua Environment all have none — "Aqua Environment" is a probiotic-manufacturing demo with no pond concept at all, not a water-monitoring one, despite its name) AND real FCR harvest data, uniquely serving 3 of the 4 aquaculture use cases from one source.

**Both flagship golden demos' own single existing cohort/cycle could not show a trend** (a single Pig Grower Batch or a single Shrimp Stocking Batch/Harvest has nothing to compare itself against), and Golden Demo #11's own flagship feed-log data isn't FCR-realistic (sized only to prove PF03/PF07 exist, not to be FCR-realistic — the same issue this session's own Golden Demo #8 DP-557 incident already found and fixed once for shrimp) — so `ai_farm_aquaculture_insight_seeds.py` adds 3 genuinely varied real cohorts/cycles per vertical under BRAND-NEW, isolated Pen codes (`PEN-AI9-C1/C2/C3`, never `PEN-G1`) and Pond codes (`POND-AI10-1/2/3`, never `POND-A1`) — the same "isolate under new pens/ponds, don't touch the flagship" precedent AI-DEMO-05/06 established, re-derived here by actually reading `pig_seeds.py`/`pig_validations.py`/`shrimp_seeds.py`/`shrimp_validations.py` in full first, not assumed: `verify_pig_farm_golden_demo()` and `pig_seeds.py`'s own `_batch_name()` both resolve the Grower Batch by filtering on the literal pen code `"PEN-G1"`, and `verify_shrimp_golden_demo()`'s final Harvest check has **no pond filter at all** (`order_by="creation desc"` across the WHOLE table) and asserts the most-recently-created Harvest's FCR falls in a realistic 0.8-2.0 range — every one of this module's 3 new isolated Shrimp Harvest FCRs (1.20/1.50/1.90) was deliberately kept inside that SAME range so that unrelated, already-passing check can never be corrupted regardless of which Harvest ends up "most recently created" from here on.

**3 real, deliberately deteriorating cohorts/cycles per vertical, chronologically ordered** (same "isolate the cause, prove genuine discrimination" discipline as AI-DEMO-05's WO-A/B/C/D scenarios): Livestock (`PEN-AI9-C1/C2/C3`, oldest->newest, real Sale Lot dates ~6/~3 months apart) — FCR 2.60 -> 3.003 -> 4.201 (only the last flagged, a 50.0% deviation vs. the 15.5% of the middle cohort — genuine threshold discrimination, not a blanket flag), mortality count 1 -> 2 -> 5 (the newest is 3.33x the farm's own peer average), cost/kg-gained rising monotonically (39,007 -> 46,547 -> 67,209 VND/kg, decomposed into a real feed-price component (15,000 -> 15,500 -> 16,000 VND/kg) and a real efficiency component) — the newest cohort ALSO carries a real, linked `Pig Medicine Treatment` (a respiratory/enteric illness outbreak) that `explain_fcr_deterioration()` correctly cites as the contributing factor. Aquaculture (`POND-AI10-1/2/3`, oldest->newest) — FCR 1.20 -> 1.50 -> 1.90 (all inside the 0.8-2.0 sanity range), survival 96% -> 92.5% -> 80%; water readings: Pond 1 is a stable 6-reading baseline (0 SF10 alerts anywhere), Pond 2 drifts moderately (still 0 alerts — proving the variance signal surfaces a real trend BEFORE any hard threshold is crossed), Pond 3 is an 8-reading series that visibly worsens and crosses SF10's real thresholds 5 times near the end (dissolved oxygen, then ammonia, then nitrite too), with a real, linked `Shrimp Health Treatment` (vibriosis suspected amid the same deteriorating water quality) grounding `explain_pond_anomaly()`'s synthesis.

### Permission-aware RAG (no dedicated EPIC number — used DP-717..720; master plan §13.9 "RAG / Enterprise Knowledge" lines ~1231-1264, RAG test list §19F lines ~3304-3310, the seventh Phase 6A item per the master plan's own curated order, built directly on top of AI-DEMO-01/02/03/05/06/09/10's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call — and the FIRST Phase 6A item to build genuinely new INDEXING infrastructure (a vector index + re-indexing pipeline) rather than just query tools over existing data)

**The mandatory pipeline shape (`Approved content -> extract -> normalize -> chunk -> embedding -> vector index -> retrieval -> permission filter -> LLM answer`) is built REAL end-to-end except the ONE step this platform's AI layer has been deliberately mock-only for since Phase 2A, extended here to cover embeddings too, by explicit instruction**: a new `rag_pipeline.py` module provides a real paragraph-then-fixed-word-count `chunk_text()`, a real, deterministic, honestly-documented MOCK `mock_embed()` (SHA-256 token-hash-bucket term-frequency vector, L2-normalized — no OpenAI/Cohere/sentence-transformers, no network call, no ML library — mirroring `ai_core._call_provider_adapter()`'s own mock-LLM disclosure), a plain-Python `cosine_similarity()` (no numpy dependency needed), and a new `RAG Chunk` DocType (hand-authored doctype folder, matching the AI Draft/AI Tool precedent rather than the older bootstrap-script Phase 2A doctypes) storing each chunk's embedding as a JSON list of floats plus real version metadata (`document_code`/`version_no`/`source_status`/`is_current`/`indexed_on`) — a real Frappe table, not an in-memory structure, queried via plain-Python cosine similarity at query time, exactly the "new DocType + plain-Python similarity, no real vector DB service" shape this build was scoped to.

**Source data: DMS Document/DMS Document Version alone (depth over breadth), a documented choice, not an oversight**: Golden Demo #4/AI-DEMO-03's real DMS Document/Version lifecycle already has, natively, exactly the version-lifecycle + Effective/Obsolete status machinery master plan §13.9 demands — no other doctype in this codebase has an equivalent real multi-version lifecycle to index against, and the task's own instruction explicitly allowed depth-over-breadth "if DMS alone lets you prove all 5 RAG tests convincingly" (it does — see below). A brand-NEW, isolated `DMS Document` (`SOP-CAL-01`, a Pressure Gauge Calibration SOP, department "Metrology") was created rather than reusing AI-DEMO-03's own `SOP-WH-02` — deliberately, since this demo needed to trigger a LIVE v2->v3 status transition mid-validation (for the re-index-on-change test) and SOP-WH-02's exact v1/v2 content pair is already load-bearing for AI-DEMO-03's own Q&A exclusion proof; reusing it would risk exactly the kind of cross-demo collision this session's own lessons warn against. `SOP-CAL-01` has 3 real chronological revisions (90-day -> 60-day -> 30-day calibration interval), each promoted through the SAME real Draft -> Under Review -> Approved -> Effective lifecycle `dms_seeds.py`'s own SOP-WH-02 uses, all 3 deliberately sharing the same core vocabulary (pressure gauge, calibration, certified reference standard, preventive maintenance, critical instrumentation, GMP-critical process step) so a current-revision query genuinely, demonstrably ALSO matches the 2 Obsolete revisions via the mock embedding — the empirical basis for the obsolete-exclusion proof, mirroring AI-DEMO-03's own cold-storage-logs proof but via the real embedding/cosine-similarity path instead of literal keyword overlap. The bulk indexer also backfills the WHOLE pre-existing DMS Document Version corpus (confirmed to include real Artwork/Label approval documents from other, unrelated golden demos — Supplement/Cosmetics/Veterinary — that also use this same shared DocType), proving the pipeline is genuinely general, not hand-fitted to one fixture.

**Re-indexing is wired as an ADDITIVE `hooks.py` doc_events entry, never an edit to `dms_validations.py`**: `DMS Document Version`'s `on_update` event now runs a LIST of 2 handlers — `dms_validations.dms_document_version_on_update()` (Golden Demo #4's own D02/D04 logic, untouched) FIRST, then `rag_pipeline.reindex_document_version_on_update()` SECOND (order matters: DMS Document's own current_version/status must already be updated before RAG re-derives `is_current`). The new handler runs unconditionally on every version save (not gated on `status=="Effective"`), so ANY revision/status transition re-indexes immediately — and because `dms_validations`' own handler flips a superseded sibling version to Obsolete via a real nested `.save()`, THAT save independently re-triggers the SAME hook list for the sibling too, so promoting one new version automatically re-indexes BOTH sides of an Effective/Obsolete swap from one real user action, with zero direct code dependency on `dms_validations`' internals. This is what made RAG test #4 provable as a genuinely live event rather than a manual/batch step.

**2 new AI Tools, appended to the SAME registry (31 total now)**: `search_knowledge_base()` (the real permission-aware semantic-search tool — embeds the query with the SAME mock function every chunk was embedded with, scores every `is_current=1` `RAG Chunk` by cosine similarity, and for every match re-checks the calling user's REAL permission on that chunk's own source record before returning it) and `get_document()` (the master plan §13.10 canonical worked-example tool name — a direct, permission-checked fetch of one named document's current Effective content, reassembled from its own indexed chunks in order). New orchestration module `ai_permission_aware_rag.py` (`ask_knowledge_base()`/`get_document_content()`) mirrors `ask_manufacturing_insight()`'s NO-DRAFT shape — **a documented design decision**: both use cases are read-only retrieval-plus-synthesis, never authoring content attached to another record (the same distinction Manufacturing Insight's own docstring drew), and master plan §13.9's own "Source citation trong UI" requirement is this demo's substitute safeguard for a missing approval step — every answer carries real, structured citations (document/title/version/chunk position) the asking human can independently verify, rather than requiring a separate reviewer before anyone sees the text.

**One real, empirically-found bug, fixed during this build**: `frappe.get_list(doctype, ..., user=user)` — this session's own established permission-scoping idiom, reused faithfully here — was assumed to simply return an empty list for a user with zero visibility, matching every prior demo's own row-level-restriction experience (e.g. a User Permission scoping one warehouse). It does NOT: when the calling user has NO read permission on the DocType AT ALL (as opposed to a row-level restriction that merely filters results to nothing), `frappe.get_list()` instead raises `frappe.PermissionError` — first discovered when the real negative-control user (`client.a.3pl@pharmacountry.vn`, real Stock-User-only role, zero DMS permission) crashed `search_knowledge_base()`'s per-chunk permission check outright instead of yielding zero results. Fixed with a new, reusable `rag_pipeline.is_source_visible()` helper that normalizes BOTH cases (doctype-level denial and row-level denial) to a single boolean, used by both `search_knowledge_base()` and `get_document()` — a genuinely new, load-bearing permission-model fact about this codebase's `get_list()` idiom, not previously exercised by any prior demo's own permission proofs (which all used row-level-restricted users, never a completely-unauthorized one).

**All 5 named RAG tests (master plan §19F) proven empirically, never asserted** — see `verify_ai_permission_aware_rag_golden_demo()`'s own checks: (1) **Effective doc retrieved** — `get_document_content()` returns the REAL current v3 content (30-day interval), reassembled from 8 real indexed chunks, never a stale version. (2) **Obsolete doc not used as current** — both Obsolete v1 and v2 genuinely score non-zero cosine similarity against the query's real stored embeddings (proving a real match), yet NEITHER is ever returned by `search_knowledge_base()` — only current v3 is, purely via the `is_current=1` filter. (3) **User without permission cannot retrieve** — `qa.manager@pharmacountry.vn` (real Quality Manager role) gets 10 real results; `client.a.3pl@pharmacountry.vn` (real Stock-User-only 3PL portal user, Golden Demo #24's own established negative-control identity, zero DMS permission) gets exactly 0, explained by a real `permission_denied_source_count=6` (proving the sources WERE matched then correctly excluded), never a coincidental non-match. (4) **Version update triggers re-index** — a LIVE v3-promotion event (a genuine `.save()`, inside the validation itself) is shown to automatically flip v2's 7 chunks to Obsolete/`is_current=0` and index v3's 8 chunks as Effective/`is_current=1`, with NO manual reindex call anywhere in the test. (5) **Source citations correct** — every citation `search_knowledge_base()`/`get_document_content()` returns is cross-checked against the real, live `DMS Document`/`DMS Document Version` record it claims to cite. Idempotency also confirmed directly: re-running the bulk indexer twice never duplicates a `RAG Chunk` row (26 chunks stable across repeated re-runs on both sites).
