**Deliberately NOT done here, by explicit instruction — extended one step further than every prior Phase 6A item**: `_call_provider_adapter()` left completely untouched (no real LLM API call, no real credentials, no live external network call), AND no real embedding-provider API/model/credentials/network call, and no real vector database service — `mock_embed()` is an honestly-documented simulation and `RAG Chunk` + plain-Python cosine similarity stands in for a vector DB, exactly per this build's own hard architectural constraint.

### Evaluation Datasets (no dedicated EPIC number — no DP task IDs assigned to this build; master plan §13.14 "AI Evaluation" lines ~1365-1382, the NINTH and FINAL curated Phase 6A item, built directly on top of every prior Phase 6A item's shared infrastructure — the SAME `AI Tool` registry, the SAME `ai_core.run_ai_action()` mock synthesis call, no parallel plumbing)

**The central honesty constraint this build is organized around**: `ai_core._call_provider_adapter()` is a fixed MOCK that returns a canned string regardless of input content — it never actually reasons about the real data handed to it, so "does the AI's output match the expected label" scoring would be completely vacuous if scored against the mock's own raw text (the mock can never be caught getting a classification right OR wrong). New `ai_evaluation.py` splits what an evaluation dataset can honestly check into two parts: (1) **pipeline health** — does `ai_core.run_ai_action()` (real, unmodified router/fallback/audit-logging) actually succeed for this action, using the platform's real AI Model/Provider configuration at that moment; (2) **classification correctness** — a REAL, deterministic, explainable (keyword-based, non-LLM, non-vector) classifier (`ai_tools._classify_qms_text()`) produces the actual category/severity/secondary-flag prediction checked against each case's labeled ground truth, mirroring the exact "real deterministic business-rule logic running alongside the untouched mock" precedent `ai_qms_copilot._suggest_capa_fields()` already established. `AI Evaluation Run`'s `groundedness_score`/`hallucination_flag_rate`/`actionability_score` fields are real (Data/text, deliberately NOT numeric — a Float field cannot honestly represent "not measurable": Frappe reads an unset Float back as 0.0, which would misleadingly look like a real, bad score of zero, caught empirically during this build) but always read literally `"N/A - mock adapter does not vary output"`, with a full disclosure in `rubric_scoring_notes` — never a fabricated number for a dimension the mock cannot produce. `source_support_score` (percentage of cases whose real `sources` citation list correctly includes the case's own input record) and `category_match`/`severity_match`/false-positive/false-negative tracking ARE real and deterministic.

**Two real actions evaluated, per an explicit architectural-fork decision documented here**: (1) `deviation_analysis` — Phase 2A's own original AI Action (`ai_seeds.py`'s `_ensure_action()`, tied to Golden Demo #2/#3's real `QMS Deviation` records) — the master plan's own `deviation_analysis` worked example is literally describing this platform's pre-existing action. (2) `classify_complaint` — a NEW, deliberately MINIMAL AI Action + Tool built specifically for this demo (`ai_tools.classify_complaint()`, registered in the SAME shared `AI Tool` registry, 32 tools total now) — chosen over "apply the eval-dataset shape to a different existing action" because the master plan names `classify_complaint` as its own canonical worked example (100 labeled cases, expected category, expected severity range, false-positive/negative tracking) and this codebase already has a real `QMS Complaint` DocType (Golden Demo #2/#3 QMS) to classify, giving the closest fidelity to spec. Deliberately does NOT get its own AI Draft/approval workflow or dedicated orchestration module like QMS/DMS Copilot — building a ninth full copilot demo would be scope creep beyond "Evaluation Datasets," so `classify_complaint` stays a minimal, real, registered tool+action whose sole purpose is giving this demo a second, structurally different real action to evaluate.

**A real, honestly-sized dataset, not an artificially-inflated 100 fabricated cases**: `QMS Complaint` had ZERO pre-existing records anywhere on this platform before this build (confirmed by reading every seed module first — it was only ever referenced by `bootstrap_qms_doctypes.py`'s own DocType-creation code), so all 12 `classify_complaint` cases are newly-seeded, isolated `QMS Complaint` records (`AI-EVAL-COMPLAINT-01`..`12`), honestly marked `is_synthetic=1` — still REAL, persisted records once created, just authored for this purpose rather than discovered. `deviation_analysis`'s 7 cases are mostly REAL organic data: the 5 real, pre-existing `QMS Deviation` records already on this platform (Warehouse B, Warehouse C, the packaging-label-misprint control, the Q01 validation-test deviation, and the OOS-raised deviation — spanning Golden Demo #2/#3 and AI-DEMO-02's own seed data) labeled with their own real, already-recorded severity/category, PLUS 2 new isolated Deviations (`AI-EVAL-DEVIATION-01/02`) added only because the real data lacked Critical severity and Process/Documentation category coverage — exactly the "add a FEW new labeled ones only if you need specific coverage the existing real data doesn't have" instruction, not a wholesale fabrication.

**False-positive/negative tracking genuinely fires, not just trivially reports zero — 2 deliberate, honestly-documented classifier limitations were designed into the dataset on purpose**: `AI-EVAL-COMPLAINT-10` ("felt unwell after use," described too vaguely to trigger any keyword) is a real FALSE NEGATIVE — a cautious human reviewer would flag it Adverse Event/Safety/Critical-adjacent, but the keyword classifier finds nothing and predicts Other/not-safety-critical. `AI-EVAL-COMPLAINT-11` ("pump mechanism produces a burning smell," a purely mechanical complaint) is a real FALSE POSITIVE — the word "burning" collides with the classifier's "burn" safety keyword, incorrectly flagging it Adverse Event/Safety. Both are real, explainable keyword-classifier limitations (not contrived to be unsolvable), and `classify_complaint`'s evaluation run empirically shows `false_positive_count=1, false_negative_count=1, passed=10/12` — proving the tracking mechanism actually catches a real mismatch rather than always reporting zero. `deviation_analysis` scores 7/7 (100%) — all 5 real organic records AND both new isolated ones classify correctly against their own real/labeled fields, evidence the classifier is genuinely accurate on real data, not gamed to pass.

**`check_model_swap_safe()` — master plan §13.14's governance principle made concretely demonstrable, not a docstring comment**: runs the real evaluation harness TWICE — once against the current production AI Model configuration (baseline) and once with a candidate model temporarily made the ONLY enabled `AI Model` site-wide (a real `ai_core._eligible_models()` routing change, restored in a `finally` block, same precedent as `ai_seeds.py`'s own `_test_fallback()`/`_test_policy_block()`) — and returns an ALLOW/BLOCK verdict from a REAL pass-rate comparison between two REAL, persisted `AI Evaluation Run` records. Two real scenarios proven: (1) swapping to `llama-3.3-70b-fast` (already real, already has the required "reasoning" capability) leaves the pass rate UNCHANGED (83.3% -> 83.3%, delta 0.0) since the deterministic classifiers don't depend on provider/model — correctly **ALLOWED**. (2) swapping to a new, deliberately capability-incomplete `AI Model` fixture (`legacy-chat-only`, capabilities="chat" only, kept disabled except transiently during this exact test) makes `run_ai_action()` genuinely unable to route AT ALL for every case (no eligible model) — a REAL 0% pass rate, an 83.3-point regression, correctly **BLOCKED**. This is exactly the class of regression a generic public benchmark score would never reveal, which is the whole point of the "never swap production model without regression-testing against the internal eval set" principle. Both scenarios confirmed to correctly restore every `AI Model`'s enabled state afterward (no lingering site-wide side effect).

**One real bug found and fixed during this build**: `AI Model` autonames on `hash` (its `name`/docname is never the same as its own `model_code` field) — `check_model_swap_safe()`'s first draft looked candidate models up and compared them by `name` instead of `model_code`, so `frappe.db.exists("AI Model", "llama-3.3-70b-fast")` always returned False and the function threw immediately. Fixed by looking up/comparing by the real `model_code` field throughout, caught by direct testing on `test.demo.local` before this ever reached "done."

## Phase 7 — Specialized UX / Portal / Web (master plan §8 "7 WEBSITE / PORTAL EXPERIENCE" lines ~2552-2577, §21 "PHASE 7" summary lines ~3493-3507. Started 2026-09-29 per "tiếp tục theo thứ tự" immediately after Phase 6A completed. **COMPLETE 2026-09-29** — all 7 master-plan-numbered WEB-0X items (WEB-01 through WEB-07) done, plus the user-requested Platform Hub, all verified on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn`. WEB-07 (Farm Customer / Technical Service Portal) was the last master-plan-numbered item in this phase; Phase 7 is now fully complete.)

**A genuinely different KIND of deliverable from every prior golden demo/AI demo — the platform's FIRST real frontend build.** Every Phase 1-6A item was a Python/Frappe backend seed module inside the existing `enterprise-platform` app, exercised through Frappe Desk or `bench execute`. WEB-01 is a real, standalone Next.js website in a brand-new top-level directory, calling the Frappe backend only through a new, narrow, guest-accessible HTTP API — matching master plan §8's own instruction ("khong mac dinh tat ca phai dung Next.js... voi public website, portal... thi Next.js la lua chon uu tien") and §9.1's deployment-topology rule (Next.js/NestJS get their OWN lifecycle, never folded into `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` "just to complete the stack").

### WEB-01 — Corporate + Product Catalog (master plan §8 lines ~2557-2558, the first Phase 7 item in the master plan's own order)

**Spec was deliberately terse ("Cho nha may/nha phan phoi" — for a factory/distributor) — company/page/data choices below are a documented judgment call, not a guess.** Checked 3 real candidate companies before picking one (grepped every `*_seeds.py` for `description`/`image`/`Item Price` fields, then confirmed live field values via `bench execute` against `test.demo.local`):
- Golden Demo #1 (Demo Pharma Co, the flagship manufacturer) — real raw-material/tablet Items, but NO `Item Price` anywhere (it never sells direct to an end customer) — poor fit for a "catalog with pricing."
- Golden Demo #21 (Cosmetics manufacturer) — consumer-facing finished goods, but likewise no selling price data — it manufactures FOR a distributor, doesn't sell direct.
- **Golden Demo #25 (Demo Consumer Distribution Co., IP-CONSUMER-DIST) — the one picked.** A genuine DISTRIBUTOR (matches WEB-01's own spec text exactly) that receives and resells 2 real consumer-facing finished goods reused from Golden Demo #20 (Supplement, `VITC-1000-EFF`) and Golden Demo #21 (Cosmetics, `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML`), and — uniquely among every golden demo checked — has REAL `Item Price` rows on the public "Standard Selling" price list (260,000 VND / 95,000 VND, live-confirmed) because Golden Demo #25's own CD01 test depends on that exact price data existing.

**Security design — a new, narrow, guest-whitelisted API module, never Frappe's normal authenticated surface.** New file `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/public_api.py`: 3 functions, each `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True, methods=["GET"])`, each an EXPLICIT hand-picked field allow-list (never `"*"`, never `get_all()`/a whole-doctype dump):
- `get_company_profile()` → `company_name`, `country`, `default_currency` (real) + a static about-blurb. No financial/internal Company field ever read.
- `get_catalog_items()` → every item in `_safe_item_codes()`'s real, data-driven safe set (see next point), each serialized to `item_code`/`item_name`/`stock_uom`/`price`/`currency` (real) + `category`/`description` (presentation copy, see below).
- `get_item_detail(item_code)` → same serialization for one item, but re-validates `item_code` against the SAME safe set rather than trusting "does this Item exist" — a guest probing an out-of-catalog code (tested live with `PARA-API`, a real pharma raw material from Golden Demo #1) gets a plain 404, never a peek at unrelated golden-demo data.
- `_safe_item_codes()` is a real SQL join, not a hardcoded item-code list: `Item` INNER JOIN `Stock Ledger Entry` (real movement for `Demo Consumer Distribution Co.` specifically) INNER JOIN `Item Price` (real, selling, "Standard Selling" list only — the internal dealer-tier price list is never touched). The showcased company name and price-list name are hardcoded module constants, NOT accepted as request parameters, so a guest can never redirect this API to describe a different company or the internal dealer price list.
- Never read or returned anywhere: cost/valuation rate, stock qty, warehouse, supplier, batch/lot/serial, customer/dealer/territory/commission data, any financial account, or any other internal Company/Item field.
- `Item.description`/`Item.image` are empty on every Item in every golden demo (grep-confirmed across the whole app, then confirmed live) — rather than mutate shared Item records reused across 3 golden demos, `public_api.py` supplies short, clearly-labeled PRESENTATION copy (`_CATALOG_COPY`/`_COMPANY_COPY` module dicts) keyed by real item_code, explicitly documented in the module docstring as marketing text authored for this demo, not a claim of real ERP data — it only ever renders for an item_code that independently passes the real SQL filter above.

**Verified unauthenticated, directly, BEFORE building the frontend** — `curl -H "Host: test.demo.local" http://localhost:8082/api/method/enterprise_core.enterprise_core.public_api.get_catalog_items` (no session cookie, no API key, no `Authorization` header at all) returned HTTP 200 with exactly the fields listed above; `get_item_detail?item_code=PARA-API` (a real but out-of-scope pharma item) returned a plain HTTP 404. Same 2 checks repeated identically against `pharmacountry.vn` after confirming Golden Demo #25's data already exists there (single shared bench serving both sites — confirmed via `bench --site all list-apps` — so no code "sync" step was needed beyond `bench --site pharmacountry.vn clear-cache`).

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-01-corporate-catalog\`** — the first file ever created under a new top-level `nextjs-demo/` directory, sibling to `enterprise-platform/` and `frappe_docker_demo/` per master plan §9.1's own topology diagram. Pages: `/` (home — real company name/country/currency + featured products), `/catalog` (full product grid), `/catalog/[itemCode]` (product detail). All data fetching is server-side (Server Components) — no Frappe URL/response is ever visible in client-side network calls, and no API key/session token exists anywhere in the frontend code (matches the backend's own guest-only design). `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched; no dedicated compose file was added for this app either — a plain `next dev`/`next start` process is the deliberately-right-sized deployment for this demo stage, per §9.1's own warning against over-building infrastructure "just to complete the stack."

**One real technical obstacle found and fixed**: Frappe is multi-tenant and routes purely on the HTTP `Host` header, and this machine has no DNS/hosts-file entry for `test.demo.local`/`pharmacountry.vn` — so the site has to be selected via an explicit `Host` header per request. Plain `fetch()` (Node's built-in, backed by undici) enforces the WHATWG Fetch spec's forbidden-header list, which includes `Host`, and silently produced a "site not found" 404 the first time this was tried. Fixed in `lib/api.ts` by using Node's lower-level `http` module instead (no such restriction), connecting to `FRAPPE_BASE_URL` (`http://localhost:8082`, the demo stack's own published frontend port) while sending `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST` (`test.demo.local`, swappable to `pharmacountry.vn`) as the `Host` header — both configured via `.env.local`, never hardcoded.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. `npm run dev` was run and every page verified via direct `curl` against the rendered server-side output: home page shows real `Demo Consumer Distribution Co.` / Vietnam / VND and both real featured products; `/catalog` lists both real items (`VITC-1000-EFF` "Vitamin C 1000mg Effervescent Tablet", `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML` "Facial Cleanser 150ml Bottle") with their real prices (260,000 VND / 95,000 VND); `/catalog/VITC-1000-EFF` renders the full real detail; `/catalog/PARA-API` (an out-of-catalog real pharma item code) correctly renders Next.js's own 404 rather than leaking pharma data. The full rendered HTML of all 3 pages was grepped for every internal/sensitive keyword this build's constraints forbid (`valuation_rate`, `warehouse`, `supplier`, `batch_no`, `commission`, `credit_limit`, `cost_center`, `tax_id`, `bank_account`, `territory`, `sales_person`, the pharma warehouse names, other golden demos' raw material codes) — the only hit was the word "dealer" inside this build's own generic marketing copy ("distributed through our dealer network"), never a real dealer name/credit/commission record. **No real browser/visual screenshot was taken or is claimed** — this environment has no browser available; verification was HTML/JSON-content-based only (confirmed real data renders + confirmed no sensitive field leaks), not visual/layout QA. This limitation, and the design choices above, are flagged to the user as the one open item genuinely appropriate for their own review (real domain/hosting decisions and visual design preferences were also explicitly left to them, per the task's own instructions).

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition.** All 36 `verify_*` functions in `api.py` re-run individually on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` after adding `public_api.py` — 36/36 pass on both sites, zero Tracebacks, matching every prior golden/AI demo's own results (`public_api.py` is purely additive: no existing file was modified, no existing DocType/data was touched).

No real-browser/visual QA performed or claimed (no browser available in this environment) — flagged to the user. |

**Deliberately NOT done here, flagged for the user rather than decided unilaterally**: no real domain/DNS/SSL/hosting decision (this remains a local `next dev`/`next start` demo, matching master plan §9.2's own "naming convention is informational only" framing); no real visual/browser screenshot QA; no containerized deployment (`nextjs-demo/compose.yaml` or similar) — the master plan explicitly warns against over-building deployment infrastructure a demo doesn't yet need, so this was deliberately left as a plain dev-server-capable app rather than adding infrastructure speculatively.

### WEB-02 — Brand / Consumer Product Website (master plan §8 line ~2560-2561, the second Phase 7 item in the master plan's own order)

**Spec was deliberately terse ("Cho supplement/cosmetics/veterinary brand" — for a supplement/cosmetics/veterinary BRAND) — vertical/company/page choices below are a documented judgment call, not a guess.** Deliberately a DIFFERENT kind of site from WEB-01: WEB-01 is a distributor's B2B-flavored corporate site + flat price-list catalog; WEB-02 must read as a genuine consumer brand's own marketing site (brand story, narrative product presentation, "where to buy") — not a WEB-01 re-skin. Checked all 3 candidate verticals the master plan names before picking one (grepped `supplement_seeds.py`/`cosmetics_seeds.py`/`vet_mfg_seeds.py`/`vet_dist_seeds.py` for `description`/`image`/vertical custom fields/`Item Price`, then confirmed live field values via `bench execute` against `test.demo.local`):
- Demo Cosmetics Co. (Golden Demo #21, `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML`) — a real consumer finished good, but no vertical-specific descriptive field beyond generic `has_batch_no`/`shelf_life_in_days` — no real data to build a distinctive brand story on besides invented copy.
- Demo Vet Pharma Co. (Golden Demo #9, `OXYTET-200-INJ`) — the richest CUSTOM fields of the three (`target_species`, `indication`, `withdrawal_period_days`), but those are clinical/regulatory label fields for a prescription veterinary injectable sold to dealers/vets — a lifestyle "brand site" for it would ring false.
- **Demo Supplement Co. (Golden Demo #19/#20, IP-SUPPLEMENT, `VITC-1000-EFF` "Vitamin C 1000mg Effervescent Tablet") — the one picked.** A real, everyday consumer wellness product, AND — once the full manufacturing chain is considered, not just the Item record — the richest genuinely REAL, verifiable data of the three: a real active Formula (BOM) with real ingredient proportions, a real `contains_allergen` flag (Soy Lecithin), and a real LIMS Specification + Approved LIMS COA test result (Vitamin C Content, published spec 950-1050 mg/tablet, latest verified batch tested at 1002 mg/tablet, live-confirmed). Also deliberately the ORIGINAL MANUFACTURER of one of WEB-01's own 2 showcased items, told from the brand's own point of view (formula/quality/story) rather than WEB-01's distributor point of view (price list) — so this genuinely reads as a different site.

**Security design — extends WEB-01's SAME guest-whitelisted module, not a new file.** Added a new section to the SAME `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/public_api.py` (not a sibling file — it's the identical guest-only security pattern WEB-01 already established, just a different company/item): 2 new functions, each `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True, methods=["GET"])`, each an EXPLICIT hand-picked field allow-list (never `"*"`, never `get_all()`/a whole-doctype dump):
- `get_brand_profile()` → `company_name`, `country`, `default_currency` (real, same 3 Company fields WEB-01 reads) + static `tagline`/`mission`/`philosophy` brand copy.
- `get_brand_product()` → `item_code`, `item_name`, `shelf_life_in_days`, `contains_allergen` (real `Item` fields — `stock_uom`/`item_group` exist but are deliberately NOT exposed, not consumer-meaningful) + `category`/`tagline`/`story`/`benefits` (presentation copy) + `ingredients` (real: current active+default BOM's item_code/name plus a COMPUTED formula-weight percentage — never a BOM cost/rate/amount field) + `quality` (real: the Effective `LIMS Specification`'s parameters plus the latest Approved `LIMS COA`'s test result, scoped to this item via `LIMS Sample.item_name` since the platform's shared LIMS chain serves multiple golden demos on the same doctypes — never batch_no, analyst, reviewer, or instrument identity).
- Never read or returned anywhere: cost/valuation rate, stock qty, warehouse, BOM cost/rate, supplier, batch/lot/serial, customer/dealer/territory/commission data, or any other internal Company/Item/Manufacturing field. `_BRAND_COMPANY`/`_BRAND_FG_ITEM` are hardcoded module constants, NOT accepted as request parameters — this demo has only one hero product, so unlike WEB-01's catalog, `get_brand_product()` isn't parameterized at all, leaving nothing guest-controllable.
- `Item.description`/`Item.image` are empty here too (same platform-wide gap WEB-01 found) — `_BRAND_COPY`/`_PRODUCT_COPY`/`_INGREDIENT_NOTES` module dicts supply short, clearly-labeled PRESENTATION copy, explicitly documented in the module docstring as marketing text authored for this demo, not a claim of real ERP data — it only ever decorates the real item/ingredient codes the real, data-driven queries return.

**Verified unauthenticated, directly, BEFORE building the frontend** — `curl -H "Host: test.demo.local" http://localhost:8082/api/method/enterprise_core.enterprise_core.public_api.get_brand_product` (no session cookie, no API key, no `Authorization` header) returned HTTP 200 with exactly the fields listed above, including the real live values (`shelf_life_days: 730`, `contains_allergen: true`, formula percentages `40/25/20/11/3/1`, quality `min 950/max 1050 mg/tablet`, `latest_tested_values.Vitamin C Content: 1002.0`, `verified_on: 2026-09-28`). The combined JSON response was grepped for every forbidden keyword (`valuation_rate`, `warehouse`, `supplier`, `batch_no`, `commission`, `credit_limit`, `cost_center`, `tax_id`, `bank_account`, `territory`, `sales_person`, `customer`, `dealer`, real customer/dealer names, `analyst`, `reviewer`, `instrument`, `basic_rate`, `stock_qty`, `serial_no`) — zero matches. Same 2 checks repeated identically against `pharmacountry.vn` (same shared bench code confirmed serving both sites — a `bench --site <site> clear-cache` plus one backend container restart, needed once so the running Python worker actually re-imported the edited module, was the only step needed on either site; no other "sync" step existed).

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-02-brand-website\`**, sibling to `web-01-corporate-catalog/` under the same top-level `nextjs-demo/` directory (master plan §9.1 topology), scaffolded with the exact same dependency versions (Next 16.3.6, React 19.2.8) and directly reusing WEB-01's own `lib/api.ts` Host-header-via-`http`-module pattern verbatim (same forbidden-`Host`-header-on-`fetch()` issue WEB-01 already found and fixed — not rediscovered here). Pages: `/` (home — brand tagline/mission + hero-product teaser + a real lab-verified-quality trust banner), `/product` (full narrative product page: benefits, allergen-transparency notice, real published formula with ingredient-percentage bars, real quality/lab-verification section), `/our-story` (brand mission + formulation philosophy), `/where-to-buy` (a "where to buy" CTA — deliberately narrative-only, explicitly disclosed in its own page copy and the README as not integrating with a real store locator, but naming WEB-01's real distributor site by name for context). All data fetching is server-side (Server Components) — no Frappe URL/response is ever visible in client-side network calls, and no API key/session token exists anywhere in the frontend code. `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched; no dedicated compose file was added for this app either, matching WEB-01's own precedent.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck, static-prerendering all 4 routes with real fetched data) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. `npm run dev` was run and every one of the 4 pages verified via direct `curl` against the rendered server-side HTML on `test.demo.local`: home page shows the real tagline/mission and the real 1002 mg/tablet verified result; `/product` shows the real ingredient list with real 40/25/20/11/3/1 percentages and the real quality specification/result; `/our-story` and `/where-to-buy` render the real company name/country. The dev server was then re-pointed at `pharmacountry.vn` (via `.env.local`'s `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST`) and the same real data (`Demo Supplement Co.`, `Vitamin C 1000mg Effervescent Tablet`, `1002`) was confirmed rendering there too, then reverted to `test.demo.local` as the checked-in dev default. The full rendered HTML of all 4 pages (both site configurations) was grepped for every internal/sensitive keyword this build's constraints forbid — zero matches on any page. **No real browser/visual screenshot was taken or is claimed** — same environment limitation as WEB-01; verification was HTML/JSON-content-based only. This limitation, and the vertical/design choices above, are flagged to the user as the open items genuinely appropriate for their own review.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition.** All 36 `verify_*` functions in `api.py` re-run individually on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` after extending `public_api.py` — 36/36 pass on both sites (`all_passed: true`, zero Tracebacks on every function, including `verify_supplement_golden_demo()` itself, confirming the new read-only WEB-02 queries against Demo Supplement Co.'s own Item/BOM/LIMS records didn't disturb that golden demo's own state). `public_api.py`'s WEB-02 section is purely additive: no existing function/DocType/data was modified.

### Platform Hub — Master Landing Site (USER-REQUESTED, NOT in the master plan's WEB-01..07 list — a direct product-direction instruction given after WEB-02 shipped)

**Why this exists, in the user's own words**: "cả website demo này cũng phải là 1 website dạng cty... mấy cục demo chỉ là khi người dùng chọn vào thôi" — the whole demo platform should itself present as one company/product website, with the individual industry demos being things a visitor selects into. This is NOT a master-plan line item (WEB-01 is "Corporate + Product Catalog," WEB-02 is "Brand / Consumer Product Website" — neither master-plan spec describes a platform-level hub), and it directly picks up WEB-02's own closing "flagged for the user" note that "a real cross-app integration between the two Next.js demos was judged out of scope for this demo stage — flagged in case the user wants that linked up later." The user did.

**Design choice: reuse the platform's own real `Industry Pack` registry, not a hardcoded frontend list.** `enterprise_core/setup.py`'s `_INDUSTRY_PACKS` (DP-303) already registers 27 real Industry Packs — the platform's own canonical mapping of vertical → Edition/roles/workspaces/seed data. Rather than write a second, parallel list of "the platform's industries" inside the new frontend (which would drift from reality the moment a pack is added/renamed), the hub's Industries/Solutions page calls a new guest-whitelisted endpoint that queries this SAME registry live. Checked the real `Industry Pack` DocType's fields first (`pack_code`, `pack_name`, `industry_category`, `description`, `default_edition`, `enabled`, plus 3 child tables) before designing the API — confirmed `description` is a real field but is NEVER populated by `seed_industry_packs()` (grep-confirmed), the exact same platform-wide gap WEB-01/WEB-02 already found for `Item.description`/`Item.image`.

**Which 27 packs, and which 2 get a "Live Demo" badge — a real, data-driven distinction, not a guess.** Cross-referenced `setup.py`'s own inline Golden-Demo-number comments (26 of the 27 registered packs have a `seed_industry_pack_*_seeds()` call wiring real `Seed Template` rows to them in `after_install()`/`after_migrate()`; only `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL` has none — registered but genuinely not yet built out with a golden demo). Rather than hardcode this "has real demo data" fact as a second list that could drift, the new `_pack_has_golden_demo()` helper computes it live: does this pack have at least one real `Industry Pack Seed` child row? Of those 26, exactly 2 (`IP-CONSUMER-DIST` → WEB-01, `IP-SUPPLEMENT` → WEB-02) also have a dedicated public Next.js site already built — this fact IS a hardcoded small allow-list (`_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS`), since "which packs get a public website" is a genuine project decision, not something derivable from any DB field, matching master plan §8's own instruction that a public frontend gets built "chỉ khi thật sự cần" (only when genuinely needed).

**Security design — extends the SAME guest-whitelisted module, not a new file.** Added a new "Platform Hub" section to the SAME `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/public_api.py` WEB-01/WEB-02 already established (not a sibling file). 2 new functions, each `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True, methods=["GET"])`:
- `get_industry_solutions()` → for every enabled Industry Pack: `pack_code`/`pack_name`/`industry_category` (real, explicit field allow-list — never `default_edition`/`terminology_overrides`, and never the `roles`/`workspaces`/`seeds` child rows' own content), `summary` (static presentation copy, see below), `has_golden_demo` (a real COMPUTED boolean, never the underlying seed-template rows), `has_live_demo`/`live_demo_label` (from the hardcoded 2-pack allow-list above).
- `get_platform_stats()` → 4 integers only (`industry_pack_count`, `capability_engine_count`, `golden_demo_pack_count`, `live_demo_count`) — never a list of records. `capability_engine_count` is the ONLY `Capability Engine` data this module ever reads or returns (not even `engine_code`/`engine_name`), the least real data that can still state a true platform-scale fact on the home page instead of a hardcoded/guessed number.
- **Deliberate departure from the literal "always use `get_list()`, never `get_all()`" guidance, documented rather than silently followed or silently ignored**: `Industry Pack` and `Capability Engine`'s own DocType permissions grant read/write only to `System Manager` — there is no Guest/All read permission on either doctype. `get_list()` ENFORCES that permission and would return nothing (or raise) for the guest user this endpoint runs as; `get_all()` deliberately skips permission enforcement, which is exactly why WEB-01/WEB-02's own `public_api.py` functions already use `get_all()`/`get_value()`/raw `frappe.db.sql()` throughout and never `get_list()` — a guest-whitelisted function has no ambient user permission scope to preserve in the first place; the function body's own explicit field allow-list and hardcoded filters ARE the entire security boundary, established precedent, not a new decision.
- Never read or returned anywhere: `Company`/`Item`/`Customer`/`Sales`/financial/transactional data of any kind — `Industry Pack`/`Capability Engine` are pure platform CONFIGURATION/registry doctypes, lower-stakes than WEB-01/WEB-02's own item-price/LIMS data even before the field allow-list is applied. `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS`/`_PACK_SUMMARIES` are hardcoded module constants, never accepted as request parameters.
- `_PACK_SUMMARIES` (one factual one-line summary per pack_code) is presentation copy authored from this project's own real build records (`setup.py`'s Golden-Demo-number comments, this document's own Phase 1–6 entries) — never invented capability claims, and it only ever decorates a pack_code that independently, really exists and is enabled in the DB. Same documented-marketing-text discipline as WEB-01's `_CATALOG_COPY`/WEB-02's `_BRAND_COPY`/`_PRODUCT_COPY`.

**Verified unauthenticated, directly, BEFORE building the frontend.** `curl -H "Host: test.demo.local" http://localhost:8082/api/method/enterprise_core.enterprise_core.public_api.get_industry_solutions` (no session cookie, no API key) returned HTTP 200 with exactly 27 packs, only the fields listed above, `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL` correctly the sole `has_golden_demo: false`, and exactly `IP-CONSUMER-DIST`/`IP-SUPPLEMENT` correctly the only `has_live_demo: true`. `get_platform_stats()` returned `{"industry_pack_count": 27, "capability_engine_count": 15, "golden_demo_pack_count": 26, "live_demo_count": 2}` — matching the manual cross-reference against `setup.py` exactly. Both checks repeated identically, unauthenticated, against `pharmacountry.vn` — identical results on both sites (one `docker compose -p demo restart backend frontend` was the only sync step needed for the running Python workers to pick up the edited module, same precedent as WEB-01/WEB-02; both sites share the same bind-mounted bench code).

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\hub-landing\`**, a THIRD sibling under the same top-level `nextjs-demo/` directory (master plan §9.1 topology), same dependency versions as WEB-01/WEB-02 (Next 16.3.6, React 19.2.8), directly reusing WEB-01/WEB-02's own `lib/api.ts` Host-header-via-`http`-module pattern verbatim. Pages: `/` (home — what ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM is, real platform-scale stat tiles, the 2 live demo sites), `/solutions` (all 27 real Industry Packs, sorted Live Demo → ERP System Demo → Not Yet Built, each card honestly badged), `/about` (what the platform is, why this hub exists, and an explicit disclosure that this is a demo/portfolio project with fictional company data — not a numbered master-plan deliverable pretending otherwise). `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched; no dedicated compose file was added, matching WEB-01/WEB-02's own precedent.

**Live-demo linkage is a frontend deployment fact, not backend data — and verified live before being hardcoded.** Rather than have the Frappe backend describe WEB-01/WEB-02's own dev-server URLs (a layering mismatch — that's frontend deployment config, not ERP business data), `hub-landing`'s own `.env.local` carries `HUB_WEB01_URL`/`HUB_WEB02_URL`, keyed by the same `pack_code` the backend API returns. Neither WEB-01 nor WEB-02 had a dev server actually running at the start of this work (confirmed via `netstat` — port 3000 was occupied by an unrelated project on this shared machine); both were started fresh and their real bound ports confirmed live (WEB-01 → **3001**, WEB-02 → **3002**, this hub itself → **3003**, all auto-assigned by `next dev` since 3000 was already taken) rather than trusting WEB-02's own prior report of "port 3000" at face value, per this task's own explicit instruction to re-verify rather than assume.

**Why the 24 non-live-demo cards are honest, non-clickable informational cards, not links to "the real Frappe login" — a documented judgment call.** Checked first: Frappe routes purely on the HTTP `Host` header, and this machine has no DNS/hosts-file entry for `test.demo.local`/`pharmacountry.vn` (confirmed: `curl http://localhost:8082/login` with no Host override returns a real HTTP `404`, not the login page — the same routing fact WEB-01/WEB-02 already found, but here it blocks a BROWSER link rather than a server-side API call, since a real visitor's browser cannot be told to send a custom `Host` header the way this app's own Node `http`-module server-side calls can). Presenting a link that would silently 404 for a real visitor would be dishonest in exactly the way this project's own "no fabricated data" discipline forbids, so these 24 packs (Golden Demos with real, complete, verified seed data but no dedicated public website — by the master plan's own design, since a frontend gets built "only when genuinely needed") are shown as clearly-labeled "ERP System Demo" cards with no link at all, plus a note that they're explored via Frappe Desk directly.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, TypeScript typecheck, static-prerendering all 4 routes with real fetched data) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. `npm run dev` was run and both dynamic pages verified via direct `curl` against the rendered server-side HTML: the home page's 4 stat tiles show the real `27`/`26`/`15`/`2`, and both live-demo cards link to the real `localhost:3001`/`localhost:3002`; `/solutions` renders all 27 real `pack_code`s with exactly 2 "Live Demo," 24 "ERP System Demo," and 1 "Not Yet Built" badge (counts confirmed via a `node`-based occurrence count, not just a visual skim, after an initial `grep -c` mis-read line-counts as occurrence-counts on this app's single-line minified static HTML). The full rendered HTML of all 3 pages was grepped for sensitive-data keywords (`valuation_rate`, `warehouse`, `batch_no`, `commission`, `customer`, `supplier`, `territory`, `password`, `api_key`, `secret`, etc.) — the only hits (`supplier`, `territory`) were traced to this app's OWN authored `_PACK_SUMMARIES` prose describing what a pack covers (e.g., "supplier lot QC," "territory... credit control"), not leaked record data — confirmed by reading the surrounding context, not assumed. **No real browser/visual screenshot was taken or is claimed** — same environment limitation as WEB-01/WEB-02; verification was HTML/JSON-content-based only.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition.** All 36 `verify_*` functions in `api.py` re-run individually (each its own isolated `bench execute` process, after an initial shared-`bench console`-session run falsely reported 5 AI-copilot failures — traced to session-level state pollution from running many functions back-to-back in one shared Python process, NOT a real regression from this change, confirmed by re-running each of those 5 individually and getting a clean pass every time) on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` — 36/36 pass on both sites after switching to isolated per-function invocation. `public_api.py`'s new "Platform Hub" section is purely additive: no existing function/DocType/data was modified, and WEB-01/WEB-02's own endpoints were re-curled directly and confirmed still returning identical real data after this file was extended.

### WEB-03 — B2B Customer / Dealer Portal (master plan §8 lines ~2563-2564, the third Phase 7 item in the master plan's own order — the fourth Phase 7 build overall once the user-requested Hub is counted)

**A fundamentally different KIND of build from WEB-01/WEB-02/Hub — this project's first authenticated frontend.** Every prior Phase 7 site is guest-only (`allow_guest=True`, zero login, hand-picked hardcoded scope). WEB-03's own spec text is unambiguous that it must NOT be: *"Catalog, price, stock, order, invoice, debt, return"* for a real logged-in dealer means real per-dealer negotiated pricing (not the public price WEB-01 shows), a real order history that is provably invisible to any other dealer, and a real credit/debt position — getting dealer isolation right was treated as more important than every other requirement combined, per this task's own explicit framing.

**Authentication design, decided and documented up front (mirroring how prior demos wrote their design reasoning before coding), in the new backend module's own docstring** (`enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/dealer_portal_api.py`): Frappe's own native session-cookie login (`POST /api/method/login`), NOT a hand-rolled password check and NOT a per-dealer API key/secret (rejected — no realistic UX for a customer typing an API secret into a login form, and the master plan explicitly asks for a customer-facing *portal*). The critical decision is where the real Frappe `sid` lives afterward: it is **never** sent to the browser. The Next.js app's own `/api/auth/login` Route Handler calls Frappe's login endpoint server-side, keeps the real `sid` in a server-only session store, and issues the browser a separate, opaque, Next.js-owned session token as an `httpOnly` cookie — client-side JS can never read it, and even a full network trace of the browser's own traffic never shows a real Frappe credential, only this app's own random token (empirically confirmed: the cookie jar captured during testing held a 64-hex-character opaque token, not a Frappe `sid`).

**Real bug found + fixed during this build**: Next.js compiles Route Handlers and Server Components/layouts into separate bundles. A plain `const sessions = new Map()` at module scope in `lib/session.ts` got duplicated across those bundles — a session created by the login Route Handler's bundle was invisible to the protected-page layout's Server Component bundle, which always saw an empty map and redirected back to `/login` even immediately after a successful login with a valid cookie. Confirmed empirically with `curl` in **both** `next dev` and a production `next build`+`next start` — not a dev-only artifact. Fixed by pinning the session `Map` onto `globalThis`, a true process-wide singleton unaffected by bundle duplication (the same well-known workaround used to avoid multiple `PrismaClient` instances in Next.js apps).

**Per-dealer scoping mechanism — reused, not reinvented: the same `User Permission` pattern Golden Demo #24 (3PL/Cold Chain) already proved.** `dealer_portal_api._get_my_customer()` is the ONLY function that resolves "which dealer is this" — it reads `frappe.session.user` (the framework-authenticated identity of the current request) and looks up the real `Customer` a `User Permission` (`allow="Customer"`, `apply_to_all_doctypes=1`, created by the new `dealer_portal_seeds.py`) links to that user. **No function in the module accepts a `customer`/`dealer_id` parameter from the caller** — grepped to confirm no such parameter exists anywhere. Every list/read query is filtered explicitly by the resolved customer, backed by the native User Permission cascade (which restricts `frappe.get_list()`-based reads on Sales Order/Sales Invoice/Delivery Note automatically, the exact mechanism 3PL's own `verify_3pl_golden_demo()` already proved cascades from a Warehouse-scoped permission), AND re-asserted in Python against every returned row's own `customer` field before it is ever returned — three independent layers, documented as deliberate defense-in-depth rather than trusting any single one.

**New, isolated dealer fixtures — NOT Golden Demo #25's own flagship dealers.** New file `dealer_portal_seeds.py` creates 2 real dealer `Customer` records on top of Golden Demo #25's existing company/price-list/warehouse master data, but under globally new names/po_no prefixes (`WEB03-...`) that cannot collide with Golden Demo #25's own `CD0x-.../FLOW-...` transactions or its documented idempotency assumptions:
- `WEB03 Portal Dealer Alpha` — credit limit 20,000,000 VND, happy-path dealer with a real pre-seeded Sales Order → Delivery Note → Sales Invoice chain.
- `WEB03 Portal Dealer Beta` — credit limit 3,000,000 VND deliberately tight, proving the real native `Customer.check_credit_limit()` block (the same mechanism Golden Demo #25's own CD04 test uses) fires through this portal's own `place_order()` endpoint, not a hand-rolled imitation of it.

Each gets exactly one real Frappe User (`dealer.alpha.portal@pharmacountry.vn` / `dealer.beta.portal@pharmacountry.vn`, `Demo@1234` placeholder password — same demo-credential convention as 3PL), roles `Sales User` + `Stock User` + `Accounts User` (the last needed because only `Accounts Manager`/`Accounts User` carry base permlevel-0 **read** on `Sales Invoice` in ERPNext's own stock role permissions — confirmed by reading `sales_invoice.json` directly, a disclosed, deliberate simplification: broader than a production build's ideal minimal custom Role, but the `Customer` User Permission still enforces the hard cross-dealer-isolation requirement regardless), and one `User Permission` scoping them to exactly their own `Customer`.

**Empirical two-dealer permission proof — the single most important test in this build, run for real, not asserted.** `dealer_portal_api.verify_dealer_portal_access_control()` (called from the new `enterprise_core.enterprise_core.api.verify_dealer_portal_demo()`) logs in as Alpha via `frappe.set_user()` (same technique 3PL's own verify function uses), confirms every order/invoice/debt row belongs to Alpha; logs in as Beta, same; then, still authenticated as Beta, requests one of Alpha's real Sales Order names directly and confirms it is genuinely rejected (`DoesNotExistError`, a plain 404 — never a distinguishable "found but not yours" response); repeats in reverse; confirms a Guest request is rejected outright. **Also independently re-verified against the actual running Next.js app via `curl`** (not just the isolated backend function): logging in as each dealer, browsing every page, and directly requesting the other dealer's real order URL —
```
Beta requesting /orders/<Alpha's real order id>  -> HTTP 404
Alpha requesting /orders/<Beta's real order id>  -> HTTP 404
Unauthenticated request to /dashboard             -> HTTP 307 -> /login
```
A full sensitive-keyword grep sweep across every one of Alpha's own rendered, authenticated pages confirmed zero occurrences of Beta's customer name, order/invoice numbers, or any raw internal field name.

**Real order placement through the portal, including the real credit-limit block, verified live via `curl` against the running app (not just the backend function):**
```
Alpha places a real, in-limit order (2 x VITC-1000-EFF) via POST /api/orders
  -> real Sales Order SAL-ORD-2026-00032 created, grand_total 420,000 VND, dealer-tier rate applied
Alpha attempts a deliberately oversized order (500 x VITC-1000-EFF, ~105M VND vs a 20M limit)
  -> rejected with ERPNext's own real, unmodified message: "Please contact your administrator
     to extend the credit limits for WEB03 Portal Dealer Alpha."
```
The order runs with **no** `ignore_permissions` — it is genuinely permission-checked as the real logged-in dealer user, so this is real native ERPNext validation firing, not an imitation of it. A real return was also filed live (`POST /api/returns`, re-checking the target Delivery Note's own `customer` against the session before doing anything, then ERPNext's native `make_return_doc()` — the same mechanism Golden Demo #25's own CD05 return uses) and confirmed to appear only on Alpha's own "My Returns" page.

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-03-dealer-portal\`**, a fourth sibling under `nextjs-demo/`, same dependency versions as WEB-01/02/Hub. Pages: `/login`, `/dashboard`, `/catalog` (dealer-priced + live stock), `/orders` (history + real place-order form), `/orders/[name]` (404s if it exists but belongs to another dealer), `/invoices`, `/debt`, `/returns` (history + real file-a-return form). All protected pages sit under an `app/(portal)/layout.tsx` route group whose `requireSession()` call is the single gate every one of them passes through before fetching any dealer data. `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched; no dedicated compose file was added, matching every prior Phase 7 app's own precedent.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. The full authenticated flow (login, every page, real order placement, real return, cross-dealer 404 proof both directions, unauthenticated-redirect proof, bad-password rejection) was tested via `curl` against a production `next start` build, then re-tested identically after swapping `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST` to `pharmacountry.vn` and confirming that site's own real seeded data (`SAL-ORD-2026-00030`, the real 3,150,000/20,000,000/16,850,000 VND debt figures) rendered correctly, then reverted to `test.demo.local` as the checked-in dev default. **No real browser/visual screenshot QA was performed or is claimed** — same honest disclosure as WEB-01/WEB-02/Hub.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition, including the new hub live-demo-list change (see below).** A new `verify_dealer_portal_demo()` was added to `api.py` (37th `verify_*` function), itself re-running `seed_dealer_portal_*` idempotently and the empirical access-control proof. All 37 `verify_*` functions re-run individually on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` — 37/37 pass on both sites, with `verify_dealer_portal_demo()` itself and `verify_consumer_dist_golden_demo()` specifically re-run multiple times to confirm idempotency (no duplicate customers/users/orders on repeated runs, and Golden Demo #25's own CD01-CD06 assertions still pass unchanged after WEB-03's isolated dealers/orders were layered on top of the same company).

**Platform Hub updated to show WEB-03 as a 3rd Live Demo — judged clearly in scope of the Hub's own stated purpose, not a unilateral scope-creep decision.** The user's own words creating the Hub were that visitors should "chọn vào" (select into) each real demo; a second, real, working dedicated site on top of Golden Demo #25 obviously qualifies. This required a real schema change, not just a data update: `public_api.py`'s `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS` changed from `pack_code -> single label` to `pack_code -> list of demos` (each carrying a stable `key`, `label`, and a new `requires_login` flag), since `IP-CONSUMER-DIST` now genuinely has two dedicated sites (WEB-01 guest-only, WEB-03 login-required) — `get_industry_solutions()`'s response field changed from singular `live_demo_label` to plural `live_demos`, and `get_platform_stats()`'s `live_demo_count` now counts individual demos (3) rather than packs (2). `hub-landing`'s own `lib/api.ts`/`app/page.tsx`/`app/solutions/page.tsx` were updated to flatten/render a list of demos per pack rather than assuming exactly one, with a distinct amber "Requires dealer login" badge on WEB-03's own card/link so the Hub never implies it's another guest-only site. Verified live: the Hub's home page stat tile now reads real `3` for "Live Public Sites," and both `/` and `/solutions` render all 3 real demo links with the correct badge on WEB-03 only. `hub-landing`'s own `npm run build`/`npm run lint` both re-verified clean after this change.

**Deliberately NOT done here, flagged for the user rather than decided unilaterally**: no real domain/DNS/SSL/hosting decision; no real visual/browser screenshot QA; no containerized deployment; the session store is in-memory only (documented limitation — a restart logs every dealer out; a production build would want Redis or an encrypted-cookie session instead); the `Accounts User` role granted to dealer users is broader than a production build's ideal minimal custom Role (disclosed above); returns are filed as an immediately-submitted stock-affecting Delivery Note directly by the dealer, with no staff-approval step in between — a real deployment might want an approval workflow rather than letting a portal login submit a real inventory-affecting document unsupervised, which is explicitly flagged here as a design question for the user rather than decided unilaterally.

### WEB-04 — Supplier / RFQ Portal (master plan §8 lines ~2566-2567, the fourth Phase 7 item in the master plan's own order — the fifth Phase 7 build overall once the user-requested Hub is counted)

**A close structural twin of WEB-03, deliberately — the hard architectural problems were already solved there and are reused here, not re-derived.** This task was explicitly framed as "structurally the SAME KIND of build as WEB-03, just for SUPPLIERS instead of DEALERS," and that framing held up in practice: the same real-session-cookie-never-reaches-the-browser design, the same `globalThis` fix, the same Node `http`-module Host-header workaround, and the same 3-layer scoping defense all transferred directly. The genuinely new design work was (1) which real suppliers to use, (2) how to model the RFQ→Quotation relationship (nothing in this codebase had ever created a `Request for Quotation` before this build), and (3) a new wrinkle WEB-03 never had to face — a doctype (`Request for Quotation`) whose supplier link lives only in a child table, not a top-level field.

**Supplier choice: reused Golden Demo #27's real Cargill Asia Trading Pte Ltd / Nutreco South America S.A. — NOT new isolated suppliers, the opposite call from WEB-03.** Both `ingredient_trading_seeds.py` and `ai_procurement_assistant_seeds.py` were read in full before deciding: every idempotency guard in both files is a marker/title existence check (`frappe.db.exists(..., {"title": marker})`), never a "does this supplier have exactly N documents" count — confirmed safe to layer new, distinctly-titled documents on top without risking either golden demo's own re-run assumptions. Both suppliers already carried a genuine multi-currency PO/PR/PI/QI history and 2 real pre-existing `Supplier Quotation` records from AI-DEMO-06 (Cargill CIF/21-day-lead, Nutreco FOB/35-day-lead — a real, non-contrived trade-off), so reusing them gave this portal an immediately-plausible demo with zero fabricated backstory. The isolated, demo-only suppliers those two seed modules use for their OWN validation (`Mekong AgriSource Trading Co. (AI-DEMO-06)`, and Golden Demo #22/MedDev's `MedTech Precision Components Ltd.`) were deliberately left untouched.

**RFQ design decision — one supplier per `Request for Quotation`, never a shared multi-supplier document, a deliberate simplification traded for an airtight isolation guarantee.** Native ERPNext's RFQ doctype supports inviting multiple suppliers in one document's `suppliers` child table — a real, common pattern. This build does not use that shape: every RFQ created here names exactly one supplier. Reasoning, documented in `supplier_portal_seeds.py`'s own module docstring: a shared multi-supplier RFQ would hold another supplier's quote_status/contact rows inside the very document a given supplier is otherwise entitled to read — every read would need careful per-row scrubbing, and one missed field would be a real cross-supplier leak. One-supplier-per-RFQ removes that entire risk category by construction, at the honest cost of not being able to demonstrate ERPNext's native side-by-side RFQ comparison workflow through this portal.

**A new wrinkle WEB-03 never had: a doctype with no top-level scoping field.** `Request for Quotation` has no top-level `supplier` Link field (suppliers only live in its `suppliers` child table), so — unlike `Supplier Quotation`/`Purchase Order`/`Purchase Receipt`, where the native `User Permission` cascade automatically restricts `frappe.get_list()` reads — the native cascade cannot restrict which RFQ *documents* a `Purchase User` can list (confirmed by reading `Request for Quotation`'s own permission rows: the native `All` role already grants `read=1` company-wide, no owner/link restriction). `supplier_portal_api.get_my_rfqs()`/`get_my_rfq_detail()` compensate with an explicit child-table-join filter (`frappe.get_list("Request for Quotation", filters=[["Request for Quotation Supplier", "supplier", "=", supplier]])`) PLUS a stronger-than-usual re-assertion that the RFQ's entire `suppliers` child table names only the resolved supplier — silently excluding (list) or 404-ing (detail) any RFQ that doesn't, rather than partially exposing it. Because every RFQ this platform seeds is single-supplier by construction, this check should always pass in practice; it exists purely as defense-in-depth against a future mistake.

**A real bug found and fixed during this build (a permission gap, not a Next.js gotcha this time — those were already solved by WEB-03 and reused without incident):** `Quality Inspection`'s own permission rows (`frappe.get_meta("Quality Inspection").permissions`) grant base read to `Quality Manager` only — `Purchase User` has none. `frappe.get_list("Quality Inspection", ...)` as a supplier-portal user silently permission-stripped the query down to a bare `{"name": ...}` row with the requested `status` field missing entirely (confirmed empirically: `get_my_qualification_status()` first returned `qc_pass_rate: 0.0` for Cargill despite a real, `Accepted` Quality Inspection existing). Fixed by switching those two supplementary QI lookups (in `get_my_deliveries()` and `get_my_qualification_status()`) to `frappe.get_all()`, with an explicit code comment explaining why this does NOT reopen any cross-supplier scoping gap: the filter in both cases is a `reference_name` already resolved from this supplier's own permission-checked, post-fetch-asserted Purchase Receipt list — never client-supplied.

**Per-supplier scoping mechanism — the same `User Permission` pattern WEB-03/3PL proved, applied via `_get_my_supplier()`.** New file `supplier_portal_api.py`'s `_get_my_supplier()` is the ONLY function that resolves "which supplier is this," reading `frappe.session.user` and a `Supplier`-scoped `User Permission` (`apply_to_all_doctypes=1`, created by the new `supplier_portal_seeds.py`) — no function anywhere in the module accepts a `supplier` parameter from the caller and trusts it as the scoping identity. Role granted: `Purchase User` only (narrower than WEB-03's `Sales User`+`Stock User`+`Accounts User` bundle — confirmed by reading every relevant doctype's own permission rows first that `Purchase User` alone covers base read on `Supplier Quotation`/`Purchase Order`/`Purchase Receipt`/`Supplier` and carries the `submit=1` a supplier needs to actually submit their own quotation).

**Empirical two-supplier permission proof — the single most important test in this build, run for real.** `supplier_portal_api.verify_supplier_portal_access_control()` (called from the new `enterprise_core.enterprise_core.api.verify_supplier_portal_demo()`, the 38th `verify_*` function) logs in as Cargill via `frappe.set_user()`, confirms every RFQ/quotation/PO/delivery/qualification row belongs to Cargill; logs in as Nutreco, same; then, still authenticated as Nutreco, requests Cargill's real RFQ/PO/quotation ids directly and confirms each is genuinely rejected (`DoesNotExistError`, a plain 404 — never a distinguishable "found but not yours" response); repeats in reverse; additionally proves Cargill attempting to **submit a quotation against Nutreco's RFQ** is rejected (not merely a read-side check); confirms a Guest request is rejected outright. **Independently re-verified against the actual running Next.js production build via `curl`:**
```
Cargill requesting Nutreco's RFQ detail            -> HTTP 404
Nutreco requesting Cargill's RFQ detail            -> HTTP 404
Cargill requesting her own RFQ detail              -> HTTP 200
Cargill requesting Nutreco's Supplier Quotation    -> HTTP 404
Nutreco requesting Cargill's Supplier Quotation    -> HTTP 404
Cargill requesting Nutreco's Purchase Order        -> HTTP 404
Nutreco requesting Cargill's Purchase Order        -> HTTP 404
Unauthenticated request to /dashboard              -> HTTP 307 -> /login
Nutreco submitting a quotation against Cargill's RFQ (not invited) -> {"error":"RFQ not found."}
```

**Real quotation submission through the portal, verified live via `curl` against the running app (not just the backend function):** logged in as Nutreco, `POST /api/quotations` against Nutreco's own RFQ created a real, submitted `Supplier Quotation` (`PUR-SQTN-2026-00004`, grand_total 8,200 USD); an immediate second submission against the same RFQ returned the SAME record (`"already_submitted": true`) rather than a duplicate — proving the idempotent-submission guard works live, not just in the seed script. This test record was cancelled and deleted afterward to restore the seeded demo's intended "Nutreco hasn't responded yet" narrative state before final verification.

**New fixtures (`supplier_portal_seeds.py`)**: 2 portal Users on the 2 real, pre-existing Ingredient Trading suppliers (`supplier.cargill.portal@pharmacountry.vn` / `supplier.nutreco.portal@pharmacountry.vn`, `Demo@1234` placeholder password), each with a `Supplier`-scoped `User Permission`; 2 new, single-supplier RFQs (`WEB04-RFQ-CARGILL-01` for 30,000kg Soybean Meal, `WEB04-RFQ-NUTRECO-01` for 20,000kg) — Cargill's is answered live through the real `submit_quotation()` API call (run as Cargill's real user, no `ignore_permissions`), Nutreco's is deliberately left unanswered, so the portal shows a genuinely mixed "one responded, one still pending" story rather than two symmetrical empty lists.

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-04-supplier-portal\`**, a fifth sibling under `nextjs-demo/`, same dependency versions as WEB-01/02/03/Hub. Pages: `/login`, `/dashboard`, `/rfqs` + `/rfqs/[name]` (embeds a submit-quotation form while still Pending), `/quotations` + `/quotations/[name]`, `/purchase-orders` + `/purchase-orders/[name]`, `/deliveries` (with each shipment's own Quality Inspection result), `/qualification` (own `quality_status`/`is_critical_supplier` + a real QC pass-rate aggregate), `/documents` (a real, modest list of document references assembled from the real records above — explicitly NOT a parallel document-management build, DMS already exists as its own Phase 3 capability). `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. The full authenticated flow (login as both suppliers, every page, real quotation submission, cross-supplier 404 proof both directions for RFQs/quotations/POs, cross-supplier submit rejection, unauthenticated-redirect proof) was tested via `curl` against a production `next start` build on `test.demo.local`, then the backend re-verified independently on `pharmacountry.vn` (its own real, independently-seeded RFQ/quotation data, `PUR-RFQ-2026-00001`/`00002`). **No real browser/visual screenshot QA was performed or is claimed** — same honest disclosure as WEB-01/WEB-02/Hub/WEB-03.

**A pre-existing data artifact noticed, not caused by this build, and deliberately left alone.** While curl-verifying Cargill's Purchase Orders page, 2 extra untitled Purchase Orders were found on Cargill (same item/qty/rate as `IT-PO-SOY-01`, created ~2 minutes before the correctly-titled `IT-PO-SOY-01`/`IT-PO-SOY-02-FX`) — this predates this session (the dev site's containers had already been running for hours before this build started) and looks like an orphan from an earlier, since-fixed run of `ingredient_trading_seeds.py`'s `_ensure_po()`. `verify_ingredient_trading_golden_demo()` already tolerates it (its checks are keyed to specific titled documents, not counts), so it was left alone rather than deleted — deleting another demo's real submitted documents was judged outside this build's scope. It does mean Cargill's "My Purchase Orders" page in this portal shows 4 rows instead of 2; disclosed here and in the app's own README rather than silently noticed and ignored.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions, including the new hub live-demo-list change (see below).** The new `verify_supplier_portal_demo()` (38th `verify_*` function) itself re-runs `seed_supplier_portal_demo_data()` idempotently, the empirical access-control proof, AND (since this build reuses real Golden Demo #27/AI-DEMO-06 data rather than isolated fixtures) re-runs `verify_ingredient_trading_golden_demo()` and `verify_ai_procurement_assistant_golden_demo()` TWICE each inside itself specifically to catch any silent idempotency corruption of the two golden demos whose data it builds on top of. All 38 `verify_*` functions were additionally re-run individually (fresh process per function, avoiding a `frappe.set_user()`-related Frappe locale-cache artifact discovered when running many verify functions back-to-back in one long-lived console process — confirmed to be a sweep-script artifact, not a real regression, by re-running the same "failing" functions standalone) on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` — 38/38 pass on both sites.

**Platform Hub updated to show WEB-04 as a 4th Live Demo, same schema WEB-03 already established — no new schema change needed this time.** `public_api.py`'s `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS` gained a new `IP-INGREDIENT-TRADING` pack_code entry (`{"key": "WEB-04", "label": "...", "requires_login": True}`); `live_demo_count` (a simple sum) automatically became 4 with no other backend change. `hub-landing`'s own `lib/api.ts` gained a `WEB04_URL` mapping (`.env.local`'s `HUB_WEB04_URL`, defaulting to port 3005, the next free port in the sequence). **A real, small bug was found and fixed here too**: both `app/page.tsx` and `app/solutions/page.tsx` had hardcoded the login badge text as "Requires dealer login" — accurate when WEB-03 was the only `requires_login` demo, but now false for WEB-04 (a supplier, not a dealer, login). Generalized to "Requires login" in both places, and the home page's own descriptive paragraph ("...one is a login-required dealer portal...") was corrected to "...two require a real login (a dealer portal and a supplier portal)...". Verified live: the Hub's home page stat tile now reads real `4` for "Live Public Sites," and both `/` and `/solutions` render all 4 real demo links with the correct badge on WEB-03 and WEB-04 only. `hub-landing`'s own `npm run build`/`npm run lint` both re-verified clean after this change.
