**Deliberately NOT done here, flagged for the user rather than decided unilaterally**: no real domain/DNS/SSL/hosting decision; no real visual/browser screenshot QA; no containerized deployment; the session store is in-memory only (same documented limitation as WEB-03 — a restart logs every supplier out); the `Purchase User` role granted to supplier portal users, while narrower than WEB-03's role bundle, is still broader than a production build's ideal minimal custom Role (it also grants visibility into other Buying-module doctypes not Supplier-linked — the `User Permission` still enforces the hard no-cross-supplier-leakage requirement regardless); the one-supplier-per-RFQ design means this portal cannot demonstrate ERPNext's native multi-supplier RFQ comparison workflow — a deliberate trade-off for isolation safety, explicitly flagged as a design choice rather than a limitation discovered by accident; the pre-existing 2 untitled duplicate Purchase Orders on Cargill (noted above) were left in place rather than cleaned up, since deleting another demo's real submitted documents was judged outside this build's own scope — flagged here for the user to decide whether cleanup is wanted.

### WEB-05 — B2C Commerce (master plan §8 lines ~2569-2570, the fifth Phase 7 item in the master plan's own order — the sixth Phase 7 build overall once the user-requested Hub is counted)

**A genuinely new risk category this platform hadn't built before — read this framing before the design choices below.** Every prior Phase 7 site is one of two shapes: guest-only + READ-ONLY (WEB-01/WEB-02/Hub) or authenticated + WRITE (WEB-03/WEB-04, a real login resolves identity before any write runs). WEB-05's own spec text ("B2C Commerce — Consumer products") requires a THIRD shape neither prior pattern covers: an anonymous guest, with no login at all, must be able to create a real transactional document (place an order). That combination — `allow_guest=True` AND a real write — is a strictly higher-risk surface than either prior shape alone, and was treated as the single most important design problem in this build, ahead of every UI/UX decision.

**Product/company choice — deliberately reused, not reinvented.** Checked Golden Demo #25 (Consumer Distribution)'s and Golden Demo #19/20 (Supplement)'s real Item/Item Price data live before deciding (`bench execute` against `test.demo.local`, confirmed unchanged since WEB-01 first established it): Demo Consumer Distribution Co. remains the ONLY golden demo company with real, presentable SELLING price data on a public price list (`VITC-1000-EFF` at 260,000 VND, `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML` at 95,000 VND, both on "Standard Selling"). Rather than invent a 3rd company, WEB-05 sells the SAME 2 real products through the SAME company/price list WEB-01's catalog and WEB-03's dealer portal already use — this deliberately completes the Phase 7 story for this one distributor: WEB-01 is its corporate catalog, WEB-02 (a different company, Demo Supplement Co., the actual manufacturer) tells the flagship product's brand/quality story, WEB-03 is its B2B dealer channel, and WEB-05 is where an end consumer would actually buy the product online.

**Security design — a new backend module, `b2c_commerce_api.py`, not an extension of `public_api.py` or `dealer_portal_api.py`.** Three `allow_guest=True` functions:
- `get_storefront_catalog()` — GET, read-only, the exact same real, data-driven `_safe_item_codes()` filter (Item + Stock Ledger Entry + Item Price join) WEB-01 established, re-implemented independently rather than imported (same reasoning `dealer_portal_api.py`'s own docstring gives for keeping its own copy — keeps a guest-WRITE module's security boundary independent of a guest-READ-only module's). Also returns real, live `Bin.actual_qty` stock counts, safe to expose to a guest (normal for any public storefront).
- `place_web_order(items, contact, payment_method)` — POST, the ONLY write in the whole module. Hard constraints, each proven empirically (not just asserted) by `verify_b2c_commerce_access_control()`:
  - **Price/rate/amount is NEVER read from the request.** Every line's real rate is looked up server-side by `item_code` alone from the same "Standard Selling" `Item Price` WEB-01 uses. Proof: a real HTTP payload with `{"rate": 1, "price": 1}` on a real item still produced an order charged the true catalog price — verified live via `curl` against both the raw Frappe endpoint and the running Next.js production build, on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn`.
  - **Item codes/qty are re-validated against real data**: `item_code` must pass the same real `_safe_item_codes()` filter (an out-of-catalog real pharma item, `PARA-API`, was tested live and correctly rejected); qty must be a positive integer within a capped bound AND within the real, live `Bin.actual_qty` at the shared warehouse (an oversized qty request was tested live and correctly rejected as a real stock check, not just an arbitrary cap).
  - **Every write is scoped to exactly one, isolated, shared Customer** — "Web Store Guest (WEB-05 Online Orders)" — never Golden Demo #25's own flagship Hanoi/Saigon dealers, never a WEB-03 portal dealer. A single reused Customer (not one per order) avoids hundreds of throwaway Customer records; the per-order `Address` + `po_no` token + `contact_mobile`/`contact_display` fields carry the real, order-specific guest identity.
  - **Order lookup requires two factors, never the raw Sales Order name alone.** Frappe's own Sales Order naming is sequential and therefore guessable; `place_web_order`'s response never includes it at all. Instead a random `order_token` (`WEB05-XXXXXXXXXX`, `frappe.generate_hash`) is stored in `po_no` and returned to the guest. `get_order_status(order_token, phone)` requires BOTH to match — verified live via `curl`: correct-token+wrong-phone and wrong-token+correct-phone both return the exact same generic 404, never a distinguishable signal.
  - **Cash on Delivery only** — the sole accepted `payment_method` value; no real payment gateway exists anywhere in this platform and none was added here.
- `get_order_status(order_token, phone)` — GET, read-only, the two-factor lookup described above.

**Two real bugs found and fixed while building the write path — both documented in the module's own docstring, not glossed over.** (1) `so.insert(ignore_permissions=True)` alone was NOT enough: ERPNext's own `Sales Order.set_missing_values()` internally re-fetches item details via `get_item_details()`, which calls `Item.check_permission()` on a completely separate `Item` document instance — the very first live test failed with a raw `frappe.PermissionError` deep inside `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py` despite the Sales Order's own insert being permission-bypassed. (2) The obvious next fix — a global `frappe.flags.ignore_permissions = True` around the write block — was tried, tested live, and found to NOT work either (confirmed by reading Frappe's own source: `Document.has_permission()` checks a PER-INSTANCE flag, but the failing check goes through the MODULE-level `frappe.has_permission()`, which never consults the global flag at all) — a plausible-looking false fix that was verified to fail before being abandoned, rather than assumed to work. **The actual fix**: `place_web_order` briefly elevates to `frappe.set_user("Administrator")` for exactly the 3 real write statements (`address.insert()`, `so.insert()`, `so.submit()`), restored in a `finally` block even on failure — a deliberate, narrow "service account" context, safe specifically because every value written in that block was already fully validated BEFORE the elevation (never a caller-supplied doctype/field), and nothing else in the module ever calls `frappe.set_user()`.

**Verified unauthenticated, directly, BEFORE building the frontend, on both sites:**
```
GET  get_storefront_catalog                                    -> HTTP 200, real items + real live stock qty
POST place_web_order {item, qty:1, rate:1, price:1}             -> HTTP 200, real order, REAL catalog rate charged (tampered fields ignored)
GET  get_order_status(correct token, correct phone)             -> HTTP 200, real order detail
GET  get_order_status(correct token, WRONG phone)                -> HTTP 404, generic message
GET  get_order_status(WRONG token, correct phone)                -> HTTP 404, identical generic message
POST place_web_order {qty: -3}                                   -> HTTP 417 ValidationError, rejected
POST place_web_order {item_code: "PARA-API" (real, out-of-catalog)} -> HTTP 417 ValidationError, rejected
POST place_web_order {qty: 999999 (beyond real stock)}            -> HTTP 417 ValidationError, rejected
```
A real, live-active ERPNext Pricing Rule (Golden Demo #25's own "Consumer Dist Tet Cosmetics Promotion") was found to be currently discounting `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML` from 95,000 to 80,750 VND during this build — the price-tampering test was designed around comparing a tampered order against a clean order for the same item/qty (both landing on the identical real, promotion-inclusive price), rather than asserting a hardcoded flat price, specifically because of this live finding.

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-05-b2c-commerce\`**, a sixth sibling under `nextjs-demo/`, same dependency versions as every prior WEB-0X app, reusing WEB-01-04's own Node `http`-module Host-header workaround verbatim. Pages: `/` (home + featured products), `/shop` (full catalog with real live stock + Add to Cart), `/cart` (client-side cart, `localStorage`-persisted, indicative total clearly disclosed as non-final), `/checkout` (guest contact form + Cash-on-Delivery-only confirmation, posts to this app's own `/api/checkout` Route Handler so the browser never talks to Frappe directly), `/confirmation` (reads the just-placed order out of `sessionStorage`), `/track` (two-factor order lookup, posts to `/api/track-order`). Unlike WEB-01/02's fully server-rendered pattern, this app genuinely needs client interactivity (the cart) — `components/CartProvider.tsx` is a plain React Context over `localStorage`, with the standard, disclosed "hydrate from browser storage on mount" `useEffect` pattern (a narrow, documented `eslint-disable` for `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` on exactly those 2 lines, not a rule ignored wholesale). `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched; no dedicated compose file was added, matching every prior WEB-0X app's own precedent.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. The full guest flow (browse catalog, place a real order with a tampered price via `curl` against the running production `next start` build, look up that order with correct/incorrect token-phone combinations, negative/oversized/out-of-catalog rejections) was verified via direct `curl` against the real running app on `test.demo.local`, then `.env.local`'s `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST` was temporarily swapped to `pharmacountry.vn`, rebuilt (home/shop are statically prerendered at build time, so this required a real rebuild, not just an env change), re-verified with the same tests (including a live tampering attempt that again correctly charged the real, promotion-inclusive price), then reverted to `test.demo.local` and rebuilt again as the checked-in dev default. The full rendered HTML of every page was grepped for internal/sensitive keywords (`valuation_rate`, `warehouse`, `supplier`, `batch_no`, `commission`, `credit_limit`, `cost_center`, `territory`, `sales_person`, other golden demos'/portals' real customer names) — zero matches on any page. **No real browser/visual screenshot QA was performed or is claimed** — same honest disclosure as every prior WEB-0X app.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition.** A new `verify_b2c_commerce_demo()` was added to `api.py` (39th `verify_*` function) — unlike WEB-03/WEB-04, there are no seed fixtures to re-run here (the module is genuinely stateless-per-request), so this function's own empirical access-control proof IS the demo data: every run places several new real orders against the isolated Web Store Guest customer, then re-runs `verify_consumer_dist_golden_demo()` TWICE to prove Golden Demo #25's own CD01-CD06 assertions were never disturbed. All 39 `verify_*` functions were re-run individually (fresh process per function) on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` — 39/39 pass on both sites, zero Tracebacks, both before AND after the extensive live `curl`/frontend testing above (re-run a third time after all manual testing specifically to catch any silent idempotency corruption from the many real test orders placed during verification — none found).

**Platform Hub updated to show WEB-05 as a 5th Live Demo, correctly WITHOUT the "Requires login" badge — verified, not assumed.** `public_api.py`'s `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS` gained a new entry under the existing `IP-CONSUMER-DIST` key (`{"key": "WEB-05", ..., "requires_login": False}` — IP-CONSUMER-DIST now has 3 dedicated sites). Because WEB-03/WEB-04 already generalized the Hub's badge logic to a plain `{demo.requires_login && (...)}` check (WEB-04's own build fixed a hardcoded "Requires dealer login" bug), NO frontend badge-logic code change was needed this time — verified live rather than assumed: the Hub's home page and `/solutions` page both render WEB-05's card with the "Live Demo" badge and correctly WITHOUT any login badge, while WEB-03/WEB-04 still show theirs (exactly 2 "Requires login" occurrences on each page, unchanged). `hub-landing`'s own `lib/api.ts` gained a `WEB05_URL` mapping (`.env.local`'s `HUB_WEB05_URL`, defaulting to port 3006, the next free port in the sequence); the home page's existing "two require a real login" copy needed no correction this time (still true: WEB-03 and WEB-04 out of 5 total). `get_platform_stats()`'s `live_demo_count` now correctly reads 5 on both sites. `hub-landing`'s own `npm run build`/`npm run lint` both re-verified clean after this change.

**Deliberately NOT done here, flagged for the user rather than decided unilaterally**: no real domain/DNS/SSL/hosting decision; no real visual/browser screenshot QA; no containerized deployment; no real anti-abuse/rate-limiting/CAPTCHA on the guest-checkout endpoint (explicitly out of scope for a demo, same honesty this platform applies to its mocked payment/LLM/embeddings work elsewhere — a real production deployment of this exact endpoint shape would need it); the cart is client-side `localStorage` only, with no cross-device/server-side persistence; the "Web Store Guest" design reuses ONE shared Customer for every guest order rather than one Customer per order — a deliberate, documented judgment call (avoids hundreds of throwaway Customer records) but a real production deployment might prefer per-order Customer or Contact records instead, which is explicitly flagged here as a design question for the user rather than decided unilaterally.

### WEB-06 — Online Pharmacy (master plan §8 lines ~2572-2573, the sixth Phase 7 item in the master plan's own order — the seventh Phase 7 build overall once the user-requested Hub is counted)

**Structurally the closest possible twin to WEB-05 — read WEB-05's own entry above first.** WEB-06 faces the exact same core problem WEB-05 already solved (an anonymous guest, with no login, must be able to place a real order — a strictly higher-risk combination than pure-guest-read or pure-authenticated-write) and reuses its proven security architecture almost verbatim: server-computed pricing only, one isolated guest identity, a non-guessable two-factor order-lookup token, Cash-on-Delivery-only, and — most importantly — the exact same narrow, documented `frappe.set_user("Administrator")` elevation pattern (never a global `ignore_permissions` flag, per WEB-05's own already-proven-and-documented reasoning) for the guest-checkout write path. What's genuinely new is pharmacy-specific: real per-store, batch/expiry-honest stock, and an empirical proof that Golden Demo #23's own native expiry-block mechanism (RX03) still protects this new guest-writable write path.

**Data/store choice — Golden Demo #23 (Pharmacy Chain), Store A specifically, not an aggregate.** Checked live before designing anything: `Demo Pharmacy Chain Co.` has exactly one real, presentable OTC product (`PARA-500-TAB`, "Paracetamol 500 mg Tablet", 1,500 VND on "Standard Selling", with a real, currently-active "Pharmacy Loyalty Week Promotion" Pricing Rule discounting it 10% through 2026-10-28) and 2 real retail stores (Store A/Store B). WEB-06 represents Store A specifically — the same real store Golden Demo #23's own POS Profile ("Pharmacy Store A POS") already sells from in person — deliberately not a "ship from whichever store has stock" aggregation layer, which would need its own unbuilt multi-warehouse fulfillment logic for no real benefit in a demo.

**Prescription-vs-OTC honesty — checked, not assumed.** `bench execute`d a full field dump of the real `Item` doctype (v16.36.0, ~90 fields) against `PARA-500-TAB` before writing any code: there is no field anywhere distinguishing prescription-required from OTC status. Per this build's own explicit instruction, that absence was NOT papered over with an invented `requires_prescription` flag or a fake prescription-upload/pharmacist-verification workflow. Paracetamol is itself uncontroversially OTC, so the storefront is honestly scoped to real, genuinely OTC-appropriate data, with this finding stated plainly in `online_pharmacy_api.py`'s own docstring, the shop page's "OTC — no prescription needed" badge, and this app's README.

**Backend — a new module, `online_pharmacy_api.py`, plus a new one-time infra seeds module, `online_pharmacy_seeds.py` (a split WEB-05 didn't need).** Three `allow_guest=True` functions in the API module, mirroring `b2c_commerce_api.py`'s own shape:
- `get_pharmacy_catalog()` — GET, read-only. Real price + a genuinely honest `available_qty`: Store A's raw `Bin.actual_qty` includes 10 units sitting in the platform's own pre-existing `RX03-EXPIRED-TEST` batch, which can never actually be sold — `_sellable_batches()` computes real, batch-level, non-expired stock instead, additionally net of quantities already committed by any real, submitted POS Invoice against that batch (see the "genuinely new finding" below for why that second deduction is necessary).
- `place_pharmacy_order(items, contact, payment_method)` — POST, the only write. Creates a REAL, submitted `POS Invoice` (`is_pos=1`, `update_stock=1`), not a Sales Order like WEB-05 — a deliberate, load-bearing choice: a Sales Order never moves stock and, confirmed by reading `stock_controller.py` directly, never runs the `BatchExpiredError` check at all, so it could not have re-proven RX03. `_pick_batches_fefo()` always allocates the earliest-expiring, non-expired batch(es) server-side — a guest-supplied `batch_no` is not even an accepted parameter anywhere in this module. A real `Address` document is created and linked (`customer_address`/`shipping_address_name`), reusing WEB-05's own pattern — found and fixed as a real gap in this module's own first draft, before any external testing, during a self-review against WEB-05's design.
- `get_pharmacy_order_status(order_token, phone)` — GET, the two-factor lookup, scoped to BOTH the isolated Customer AND the isolated POS Profile (a stronger isolation than WEB-05 had available, since WEB-05 only had one isolation axis).

**Isolation design — a dedicated POS Profile, top-up stock batch, Customer, and cashier service User, all admin-run and idempotent (`online_pharmacy_seeds.py`), never guest-reachable.** Four real, proactively-designed-around risks, none discovered by breaking them:
1. **A dedicated "Online Pharmacy Store A POS" POS Profile, never Golden Demo #23's own "Pharmacy Store A POS".** Golden Demo #23's own `verify_pharmacy_golden_demo()` RX05 check resolves "the" original sale via `frappe.db.get_value("POS Invoice", {"pos_profile": "Pharmacy Store A POS", ...}, "name")` with NO ordering — sharing that POS Profile would risk this query non-deterministically resolving to one of WEB-06's own online orders instead, breaking RX05's own return-lookup. Designed around before it could happen, the same lesson this golden demo's own DP-650/DP-655 bug history already taught.
2. **An isolated 1,000-unit top-up stock batch (`WEB06-ONLINE-STOCK`, expiry 2027-06-30), deliberately EARLIER than Golden Demo #23's own real batch's 2028-09-27 expiry** so native FEFO always drains the isolated batch first — protects that golden demo's own 120-unit flagship batch from repeated verification-time depletion (this build's own regression sweep places many real orders and must re-run its own proof multiple times). Still 100% real stock at the real Store A warehouse — the catalog's displayed stock is the honest sum of both batches, isolated only for write-side idempotency safety, not hidden from read-side honesty. Applied DP-659's own already-documented fix proactively (row-level `Stock Entry Detail.expiry_date` is silently ignored by ERPNext's auto-batch-creation path; the real batch's expiry must be set via `frappe.db.set_value()` after the fact) — not rediscovered.
3. **A dedicated, never-logged-into "cashier" service User** for the online POS Opening Entry's own `user` field — a real bug found while building this: ERPNext's own `POS Opening Entry.check_user_already_assigned()` throws "Cashier is currently assigned to another POS" because `frappe.session.user` (Administrator, when seeding) was already the cashier on Golden Demo #23's own opening entry.
4. **An isolated "Pharmacy Online Guest (WEB-06 Online Orders)" Customer**, never Golden Demo #23's own "Pharmacy Walk-in Customer" — same naming/isolation discipline as WEB-05's own "Web Store Guest".

**A genuinely new, real finding — not a WEB-06 bug — proactively designed around: POS Invoice submission in this ERPNext version does not immediately post to the Stock Ledger.** Read directly from ERPNext's own source before assuming otherwise: `POS Invoice.on_submit()` never calls `update_stock_ledger()` or creates a GL Entry (unlike plain `Sales Invoice.on_submit()`, which does both) — it only submits the item rows' own `Serial and Batch Bundle` documents, and that bundle's own `on_submit()` only validates, it never creates a `Stock Ledger Entry` either. This is native, intentional ERPNext architecture (individual POS Invoices are lightweight, provisional register sales; a `POS Closing Entry` is meant to later consolidate them into a document that actually posts) — confirmed this is not WEB-06-specific by checking Golden Demo #23's OWN real in-store sale (DP-653) live: its warehouse balance shows the identical characteristic, since no POS Closing Entry was ever run for that profile either. Rather than manually forcing `update_stock_ledger()` (which could double-post if a POS Closing Entry ever ran later), `_sellable_batches()` treats every real, submitted, non-return POS Invoice Item against a batch (from EITHER POS Profile) as an already-committed reduction on top of the real Stock Ledger balance — the storefront can never oversell a batch that's already been sold, which is the more correct inventory model for a live storefront regardless of when the accounting subsystem's own batch job runs.

**The single most important empirical proof in this build: the native expiry-block mechanism still protects this new write path.** `_attempt_order_against_expired_batch()` deliberately constructs a POS Invoice explicitly pinned to Golden Demo #23's own real `RX03-EXPIRED-TEST` batch (a shape no guest input can ever reach — `place_pharmacy_order` has no `batch_no` parameter at all) and confirms it is REJECTED by the exact same native `StockController` `BatchExpiredError` mechanism (`erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`, read directly) Golden Demo #23's own RX03 test already proved against a POS Invoice — zero new validation code was written or needed. Verified via `bench execute` (repeatable, deterministic) and via `verify_online_pharmacy_access_control()`, run 3 times in a row with `all_passed: true` every time.

**Verified unauthenticated, directly, BEFORE building the frontend, on both sites:**
```
GET  get_pharmacy_catalog                                        -> HTTP 200, real item, real batch-honest sellable stock
POST place_pharmacy_order {qty:1, rate:1, price:1, amount:1,
                            batch_no:"RX03-EXPIRED-TEST"}         -> HTTP 200, real order, REAL price (1,350 VND w/ promo)
                                                                       charged, REAL non-expired batch allocated — every
                                                                       tampered field (including the spoofed expired batch_no) ignored
Deliberate attempt to sell RX03-EXPIRED-TEST directly              -> REJECTED, native BatchExpiredError (frappe.ValidationError)
GET  get_pharmacy_order_status(correct token, correct phone)      -> HTTP 200, real order detail incl. real batch/expiry
GET  get_pharmacy_order_status(correct token, WRONG phone)         -> HTTP 404, generic message
GET  get_pharmacy_order_status(WRONG token, correct phone)         -> HTTP 404, identical generic message
POST place_pharmacy_order {qty: -5}                                 -> HTTP 417 ValidationError, rejected
POST place_pharmacy_order {item_code: "VITC-1000-EFF" (real, different demo's item)} -> HTTP 417, rejected
POST place_pharmacy_order {qty: 999999 (beyond real sellable stock)} -> HTTP 417, rejected
```

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-06-online-pharmacy\`**, a seventh sibling under `nextjs-demo/`, same dependency versions and Node `http`-module Host-header workaround as every prior WEB-0X app, same 6-page shape as WEB-05 (home/shop/cart/checkout/confirmation/track) adapted for pharmacy: the shop page shows an "OTC — no prescription needed" badge and real per-store stock already excluding expired batches; confirmation/track show the real batch number and expiry date each line was filled from, plus the real delivery address. `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. The full guest flow (browse catalog, place a real order with a tampered price/batch via `curl` against the running production `next start` build, look up that order with correct/incorrect token-phone combinations, negative/oversized/out-of-catalog rejections) was verified via direct `curl` against the real running app on `test.demo.local`, then re-verified against `pharmacountry.vn` (rebuilt with `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST` swapped) — a real order placed during that pass was confirmed, via direct database query, to have landed on `pharmacountry.vn` specifically and NOT on `test.demo.local`, then reverted to `test.demo.local` as the checked-in dev default. **A real deployment gap was found and fixed during Hub verification**: the running Frappe backend's gunicorn worker had the OLD `public_api.py` module cached in memory from before this build's edits (a fresh `bench execute` process always re-reads current source and so didn't show this — only the actual HTTP endpoint, served by the long-running worker, did) — fixed with `docker compose -p demo restart backend`, then re-verified the full regression sweep clean on both sites afterward to confirm the restart itself caused no regression. The full rendered HTML of every page was grepped for internal/sensitive keywords (`valuation_rate`, `supplier`, `commission`, `credit_limit`, `cost_center`, `sales_person`, `Pharmacy Walk-in Customer`, `Administrator`) — zero matches on any page. **No real browser/visual screenshot QA was performed or is claimed** — same honest disclosure as every prior WEB-0X app.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition.** A new `verify_online_pharmacy_demo()` was added to `api.py` (40th `verify_*` function), mirroring `verify_b2c_commerce_demo()`'s own structure: it re-runs the idempotent `seed_online_pharmacy_setup()`, then `verify_online_pharmacy_access_control()` (the empirical security + expiry-safety proof), then re-runs `verify_pharmacy_golden_demo()` TWICE to prove Golden Demo #23's own RX01-RX07 assertions (including RX03's expired-batch-never-sold invariant and RX05's own POS-return-lookup, both real corruption risks this build designed around, not discovered by breaking them) were never disturbed. All 40 `verify_*` functions were re-run individually (fresh process per function) on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` — 40/40 pass on both sites, run TWICE on `test.demo.local` before the backend restart and ONCE more on both sites after it (to confirm the restart itself caused no regression), zero Tracebacks throughout, including after all the extensive manual `curl`/frontend/Hub testing above.

**Platform Hub updated to show WEB-06 as a 6th Live Demo, correctly WITHOUT the "Requires login" badge — verified live, not assumed.** `public_api.py`'s `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS` gained a new `IP-PHARMACY` key (`{"key": "WEB-06", ..., "requires_login": False}` — Golden Demo #23's Industry Pack had no dedicated site before this). Because WEB-03/WEB-04/WEB-05 already generalized the Hub's badge logic to a plain `{demo.requires_login && (...)}` check, NO frontend badge-logic code change was needed — verified live: after restarting the stale backend worker (see above) and a clean rebuild of `hub-landing` (a stale, PRE-EXISTING `hub-landing` dev server from an earlier session was found still running on port 3003 serving OLD data — killed and restarted with the fresh build, flagged here since it wasn't started by this build), both the Hub's home page and `/solutions` page correctly render "6 dedicated sites", WEB-06's card with the "Live Demo" badge and correctly WITHOUT any login badge, while WEB-03/WEB-04 still show theirs unchanged (4 total "Requires login" occurrences across both pages, same as before). `hub-landing`'s own `lib/api.ts`/`.env.local` gained a `WEB06_URL`/`HUB_WEB06_URL` mapping (port 3007, the next free port in the sequence). `get_platform_stats()`'s `live_demo_count` now correctly reads 6 on both sites. `hub-landing`'s own `npm run build`/`npm run lint` both re-verified clean after this change.

### WEB-07 — Farm Customer / Technical Service Portal (master plan §8 lines ~2575-2576, the seventh and LAST master-plan-numbered Phase 7 item — the eighth Phase 7 build overall once the user-requested Hub is counted; Phase 7 is now fully COMPLETE)

**Structurally the closest possible twin of WEB-03 — read WEB-03's own entry above first.** Same authentication architecture reused verbatim (real Frappe session-cookie login held server-side, opaque token to the browser, `globalThis`-pinned session store applied from the start rather than rediscovering WEB-03's own bundle-splitting bug), same `Customer`-scoped `User Permission` isolation mechanism, same Alpha/Beta (generous/tight credit limit) two-account design. What's genuinely new is the domain: a real FARM customer of a veterinary distributor, not a retail dealer or supplier, plus proving the same permission-cascade mechanism also works for a custom DocType (`Vet Technical Visit`), not just native Sales Order/Sales Invoice.

**Real data investigation — this build's own main new task, since "technical visits/recommendations" had no obvious existing home like WEB-04's supplier data did.** Checked before writing any code: Golden Demo #10 (Veterinary Distribution, master plan DEMO 16) is the ONLY golden demo in this platform with a real "technical visit" concept — its own `Vet Technical Visit` DocType (VD07), already created, already populated with one real record for its own dealer ("VetCare Mekong Dealer Co."). Master plan DEMO 20 ("Feed Distribution & Dealer Management", which also names a technical-visit test, FD07) was confirmed NEVER built as a golden demo in this project (grepped this file's own golden demo list, #1-#28 — DEMO 20 is not among them), so there was no second real source to check. Golden Demos #11-#14 (Pig/Poultry/Cattle/Hatchery Farm) are the farms' own INTERNAL operations, each a separate Company, never modeled as a Customer of Golden Demo #10's Demo Vet Pharma Co. — no real cross-demo link existed to reuse (unlike Golden Demo #28/Meat Processing's genuine sourcing from Golden Demo #11's own `Pig Sale Lot`), and none was fabricated here.

**Reused Golden Demo #10's real `Vet Technical Visit` DocType directly — no new DocType created, per this build's own instruction against fabricating a large dataset.** Two small, additive schema changes (`bootstrap_farm_portal_doctypes.py`) closed the only real gap: (1) a `Custom Field` (`recommended_item`, Link -> Item) structuring what was previously free-text "products discussed" into a real recommendation; (2) a read-only `Custom DocPerm` granting the native `Sales User` role read access to `Vet Technical Visit` (previously ONLY `System Manager` had any access at all), which is what lets the native `User Permission` cascade actually restrict which visits a farm portal login can see — the same mechanism already relied on platform-wide for Sales Order/Sales Invoice/Supplier Quotation, now confirmed to also work for a custom DocType with a top-level `customer` Link field. Both are plain DB rows (`Custom Field`/`Custom DocPerm`), independent of the base DocType's JSON, so they work identically on `test.demo.local` (`developer_mode`) and `pharmacountry.vn` (no `developer_mode`). Only **4 real Technical Visit records** were seeded (2 per farm, one per farm carrying a real product recommendation, the other a plain follow-up) — small and honestly scoped, not a large fictional dataset.

**Why 2 NEW isolated farm Customers, not Golden Demo #10's own "VetCare Mekong Dealer Co."**: that Customer is explicitly a DEALER (a `Sales Partner`/reseller, matching DEMO 16's own "manufacturer -> regional dealer -> veterinary shop/farm" chain), not the FARM this master plan item specifically asks for, and its own `verify_vet_dist_golden_demo()` (VD01-VD07) asserts against that exact Customer/Sales Order/Technical Visit data — writing new portal test data onto it risked the same cross-demo idempotency corruption this session's prior lessons (WEB-03's own docstring, AI-DEMO-02's CAPA duplication) already warn about. WEB-07 provisions its own 2 isolated farm Customers, in the SAME company/territory/sales person/item/warehouse (genuine reuse of real master data) but with globally distinct names/po_no prefixes (`WEB07-...`):
- **"WEB07 Portal Farm Alpha"** (a cattle farm) — generous credit limit (10,000,000 VND), happy-path order history plus 2 Technical Visits (one recommending `OXYTET-200-INJ` for cattle mastitis treatment, one a plain follow-up).
- **"WEB07 Portal Farm Beta"** (a swine farm) — deliberately tight credit limit (2,000,000 VND), proves the real ERPNext credit-limit block through this portal's own `place_order()`; its own 2 Technical Visits (one recommending the same real item for swine respiratory infection control, one a plain follow-up).

Both farms buy Golden Demo #9/#10's own real single finished good, `OXYTET-200-INJ` (Oxytetracycline 200mg/mL Injectable Solution, real `target_species`/`indication`/`withdrawal_period_days` custom fields) — genuinely indicated for both cattle and swine, so this portal is honestly scoped to that one real product rather than inventing a larger fictional catalog.

**Backend — new module `farm_portal_api.py`, plus `farm_portal_seeds.py`.** `_get_my_farm()` is the only function that resolves "which farm is this" (from `frappe.session.user` via the `Customer`-scoped `User Permission` — never a client-supplied parameter). Endpoints: `get_my_profile`, `get_my_catalog` (the one real orderable product), `get_my_orders`/`get_my_order_detail`, `place_order` (server-side item/rate constants, real credit-limit validation, no `ignore_permissions`), `get_my_technical_visits`, `get_my_recommendations` (the subset of visits naming a real `recommended_item`, enriched with that item's real veterinary fields), and `get_my_service_history` (a computed, unified timeline merging the farm's own real orders and visits — not a fabricated parallel record). Same three-layer defense as WEB-03/04 throughout: session-resolved identity, explicit `frappe.get_list()` filter (never `get_all()`), and a post-fetch Python re-assertion that every returned row's own `customer` field matches.

**Verified unauthenticated, directly, via `bench execute` and `curl`, BEFORE building the frontend, on both sites**: guest access to every endpoint correctly rejected (`AuthenticationError`); login as each farm resolves the correct profile/customer; each farm's orders/visits/recommendations/service-history contain ONLY that farm's own rows; a live `place_order` by Alpha succeeded with a real Sales Order; a live oversized order by Beta was rejected with ERPNext's own real, unmodified credit-limit message; Beta requesting Alpha's real order id (and the reverse) both genuinely rejected with `DoesNotExistError`.

**Frontend: a real Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4) app in `D:\ME\ENTERPRISE_PLATFORM\nextjs-demo\web-07-farm-portal\`**, an eighth sibling under `nextjs-demo/`, same dependency versions and Node `http`-module Host-header workaround as every prior WEB-0X app, same `globalThis`-pinned session store applied from the start. Pages: `/login`, `/dashboard`, `/orders` (+ place-order form), `/orders/[name]`, `/visits`, `/recommendations`, `/history`. `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml` was NOT touched.

**Verification — honest about what was and wasn't checked.** `npm run build` (production build, including TypeScript typecheck) and `npm run lint` both pass clean. The full authenticated flow (login as each farm, browse every page, place a real order, the real credit-limit block, cross-farm 404 proof in both directions, unauthenticated-redirect proof, bad-password rejection) was verified via direct `curl` against a production `next start` build on `test.demo.local`, then re-verified identically against `pharmacountry.vn` (rebuilt with `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST` swapped), then reverted to `test.demo.local` as the checked-in dev default. The full rendered HTML of every page (both farms, both site configurations) was grepped for the other farm's name/credit_limit/valuation_rate/Administrator — zero matches. Alpha's `/visits` page correctly mentions "mastitis" and never "swine"/"weaner"; Beta's correctly mentions "swine"/"weaner" and never "mastitis" — confirmed via live grep, not merely asserted. **No real browser/visual screenshot QA was performed or is claimed** — same honest disclosure as every prior WEB-0X app.

**A real deployment gap found and fixed during Hub verification (the same class of stale-worker-cache issue WEB-06 already documented, not rediscovered blindly — checked for it proactively when live data didn't match the edited code)**: the running Frappe backend's gunicorn worker had the OLD `public_api.py` module cached in memory from before `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS`' new `IP-VETERINARY` entry was added — a fresh `bench execute` process always re-reads current source and so didn't show this, only the actual HTTP endpoint did (`get_platform_stats()` kept returning `live_demo_count: 6`). Fixed with `docker compose -p demo restart backend`, then the full regression sweep was re-run clean on both sites afterward to confirm the restart itself caused no regression.

**Full regression sweep — zero regressions from this addition.** A new `verify_farm_portal_demo()` was added to `api.py` (41st `verify_*` function): re-runs the idempotent bootstrap + seed steps, checks both farms/users/User-Permissions/the new Custom Field/Custom DocPerm exist, checks the credit-limit block held and the in-limit order didn't, checks the real recommendation data, runs the empirical two-farm access-control proof, then re-runs `verify_vet_dist_golden_demo()` TWICE to prove Golden Demo #10's own VD01-VD07 assertions (including VD07's own Technical Visit check) were never disturbed by WEB-07's isolated farms/visits/orders layered on the same company. All 41 `verify_*` functions were re-run individually (fresh process per function) on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` — 41/41 pass on both sites, run twice on `test.demo.local` (once before, once after the backend restart above) to confirm the restart caused no regression.

**Platform Hub updated to show WEB-07 as a 7th Live Demo, correctly WITH the "Requires login" badge — verified live, not assumed.** `public_api.py`'s `_LIVE_DEMO_PACKS` gained a new `IP-VETERINARY` key (`{"key": "WEB-07", ..., "requires_login": True}` — Golden Demo #9/#10's shared Industry Pack had no dedicated site before this). No frontend badge-logic change was needed (the same generic `{demo.requires_login && (...)}` check WEB-03/04/05/06 already generalized). `hub-landing`'s own `lib/api.ts`/`.env.local` gained a `WEB07_URL`/`HUB_WEB07_URL` mapping (port 3008, the next free port in the sequence). Verified live after the backend restart above and a clean rebuild of `hub-landing`: the home page now reads "7 dedicated sites across this platform...", `/solutions` shows the WEB-07 card under IP-VETERINARY with the "Live Demo" badge and correctly WITH a "Requires login" badge, and `get_platform_stats()`'s `live_demo_count` now correctly reads 7 on both sites. `hub-landing`'s own `npm run build`/`npm run lint` both re-verified clean after this change.

## Guided Demo Mode (master plan §16, lines ~2871-2900 — user request 2026-09-29, continuing §23 "Definition of Done cấp Platform" checklist after Phase 7 completed)

**A real back-office Frappe Desk feature, not a new Next.js app** — per this project's own established technology-choice rule (§8/§2.5: back-office workflow stays on Frappe Desk; Next.js only for public/portal/field UX). Two new DocTypes inside the existing `enterprise_core` app, a real "Start Demo" Desk UI, and 5 genuinely real, verified guided-sales scenarios — deliberately NOT one scenario per golden demo (28 exist), matching this whole project's "build the reusable infrastructure once, populate a well-chosen honest handful" pattern (Permission-aware RAG scoped to DMS only, AI-DEMO-09/10 scoped to Pig/Shrimp only, etc.).

Parent reuses the real DP-303 `Industry Pack` registry for `golden_demo_reference` rather than a parallel one. Child step row uses a Dynamic Link pair (`target_doctype` -> `target_document`), same established pattern as `Meat Incoming Lot.source_reference` — can point at ANY real document. `direct_link` is a stored, computed RELATIVE `/app/<slug>/<name>` URL (`guided_demo.py::compute_direct_link`) — deliberately not `frappe.utils.get_url_to_form` (which bakes in the current site's absolute host), so the same scenario record opens the right record on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` unmodified. |

A DocType client script needs no such step (read live from disk per-request, same low-risk mechanism every other custom hook edit in this session already relies on, needing only `bench clear-cache`) — the more idiomatic, lower-risk choice for THIS codebase, not a shortcut. Every one of this session's 5 scenarios only NAVIGATES already-real, already-submitted golden-demo records (no step mutates anything), so "reset" can only honestly mean "re-assert the scenario's canonical golden-demo data" — exactly what this codebase's own idempotent seed functions already do (the DP-307 checks-before-creates pattern, proven safe-to-re-run across all 28 golden demos this session built). `reset_function_path` is wired ONLY to functions this session read in full and confirmed idempotent; `allow_reset=0` + an honest `reset_instructions` string is used wherever no such function exists — never a fake button. **The 5 scenarios built, and why each was chosen (depth over breadth, not all 28 golden demos):**

1. **`PHARMA-BATCH-RELEASE`** (mandatory) — master plan §16's OWN worked example ("Scenario: Pharmaceutical Batch Release"), reused verbatim (all 11 numbered steps, not re-invented), built entirely from Golden Demo #1's real, already-existing records: the real Purchase Receipt, the real PARA-API Batch, the real Incoming Quality Inspection, the real QC-release Material Transfer, the real PARA-500-TAB Work Order, and the real QA-release Material Transfer — switching through all 5 real DP-405 role logins (`warehouse.officer@`, `analyst@`, `qc.manager@`, `production.manager@`, `qa.manager@pharmacountry.vn`) exactly as the master plan's script specifies. `allow_reset=1`, wired to `run_industry_pack_seeds("IP-PHARMA")` (real, idempotent).
2. **`COSMETICS-STABILITY-COMPLAINT`** — a second manufacturing-flavored vertical (Golden Demo — Cosmetics, IP-COSMETICS), proving the framework isn't pharma-specific: a real packed batch, a genuinely Overdue stability sample (C04), a real batch rework record, and a real customer complaint traced to its exact batch (C05). No dedicated Cosmetics demo login exists yet (unlike Pharma's DP-405 users) — disclosed honestly in the scenario's own `primary_login_role` text rather than fabricated. `allow_reset=1` via `run_industry_pack_seeds("IP-COSMETICS")`.
3. **`PIG-FARM-BREEDING-TO-SALE`** — a livestock/farm-flavored vertical (Golden Demo — Pig Farm, IP-LIVESTOCK-PIG): the real breeding-service → farrowing → grower-batch → weight-record → sale-lot chain for one real litter (verified via the actual parent/child Link fields, not assumed). Same honest "no dedicated demo login yet" disclosure as Cosmetics. `allow_reset=1` via `run_industry_pack_seeds("IP-LIVESTOCK-PIG")`.
4. **`AI-QMS-COPILOT-CAPA-APPROVAL`** — an AI-copilot-flavored scenario (AI-DEMO-02, IP-QMS): a real Deviation ("Cold storage temperature excursion — Warehouse C") → a real AI-generated CAPA draft (`AI Draft`, `requires_human_approval=True`, mock provider clearly labeled) → the real `QMS CAPA` that came into existence ONLY after an explicit approval by `quality.director@pharmacountry.vn` — the exact human-in-the-loop guarantee `verify_ai_qms_copilot_golden_demo()` already proves live. `allow_reset=1`, but wired to the narrower, genuinely idempotent `seed_ai_qms_copilot()` (see reset-honesty note above) rather than the validations flow, which deliberately mints new records every call.
5. **`FARM-PORTAL-TECHNICAL-VISIT`** — a Phase-7-flavored scenario (WEB-07, IP-VETERINARY): logs in as the real Farm Alpha portal user, opens Alpha's real Sales Order and a real Vet Technical Visit carrying a structured `recommended_item`, then switches to the real Farm Beta portal login to narrate the empirically-verified cross-farm isolation (`farm_portal_api.verify_farm_portal_access_control()`). `allow_reset=1`, wired to a new small `farm_portal_seeds.reset_farm_portal_demo_data()` wrapper chaining WEB-07's 3 already-idempotent seed functions (master data, sales flow, technical visits) — deliberately excludes the separate over-credit-limit negative-test seed, which isn't part of this walkthrough's own steps.

**Honest coverage disclosure — do not read this as "guided demos exist for the platform's golden demos" in general**: only these 5 of the platform's 28 golden demos have a real `Guided Demo Scenario` record. The other ~23 (QMS's own other modules beyond the AI-copilot angle, DMS, LIMS, EAM standalone, Feed, Shrimp, every other Livestock/Aqua/Commercial-Vertical golden demo, and every other Phase 7 WEB-0X site) do NOT have one yet. The `Guided Demo Scenario`/`Guided Demo Scenario Step` DocTypes and the "Start Demo" Desk UI are fully generic and reusable for any of them — building out the remaining ~23 is explicitly left as a decision for the user (see the closing report), not something this session claims to have done.

**Reset-mechanism honesty — summary**: all 5 scenarios have `allow_reset=1`, and in every case the button is wired to a function this session actually read in full and confirmed idempotent (not merely assumed) — 3 scenarios reuse the existing DP-306 `run_industry_pack_seeds()` runner directly, 1 reuses AI-DEMO-02's own base-data seed (narrower than its full validation flow, for the reason above), and 1 uses a new small wrapper chaining WEB-07's own 3 idempotent seeds. No scenario's reset button does anything destructive, and none is a fake/no-op button — where a scenario ever needed one and no safe function existed, the design was to set `allow_reset=0` with an honest `reset_instructions` string instead (this session's actual 5 scenarios all had a safe function available, so this path exists in the code/verify function but wasn't needed by any of the 5).

**Verification**: `verify_guided_demo_mode()` all-green on `test.demo.local`, re-run 3+ times confirming idempotency (no duplicate scenarios, no growing step counts). Full regression sweep — all 42 `verify_*` functions (41 prior + this one) — clean on `test.demo.local`. Synced to `pharmacountry.vn` via `bench migrate` (DocTypes) + `bench execute ...verify_guided_demo_mode` (seeds all 5 scenarios there independently, since golden-demo record names legitimately differ between the two sites — e.g. the Purchase Order name, the AI Draft/QMS CAPA ids — every `target_document` was re-verified live on `pharmacountry.vn`, not copied from `test.demo.local`); all-green there too, re-run for idempotency, and the full 42-function regression sweep is clean on `pharmacountry.vn` as well. No real bugs were found in EXISTING code during this build; the only notable pre-existing quirk observed (not introduced, not fixed, out of scope) is that re-running Golden Demo #1's full `IP-PHARMA` seed chain via the reset button surfaces a stale-precondition SKIP message from the pre-existing `seed_distribution_fefo_proof` step on `test.demo.local` (harmless — `status: "ok"` at the dispatcher level, no exception, no data corruption).

## User Documentation (master plan §17 "DOCUMENTATION CHO NGƯỜI DÙNG" — user request 2026-09-29, continuing §23 "Definition of Done cấp Platform" checklist)

Real, business-user-facing documentation now exists at `documents/user_guides/` — written for the platform's actual end users (QA Manager, Farm Manager, Quality Director, a farm customer, a platform administrator), not as a build report. Deliberately scoped to the **same 5 real scenarios Guided Demo Mode (§16, above) already covers**, for consistency: Pharmaceutical Batch Release (Golden Demo #1), Cosmetics Stability/Complaint (Golden Demo #21), Pig Farm Breeding-to-Sale (Golden Demo #11), AI QMS Copilot CAPA Approval (AI-DEMO-02), and Farm Portal Technical Visit (WEB-07) — each with a Quick Start + Role Guide (for that scenario's single most central role) + Process Guide (full end-to-end story), following the master plan's own Purpose/Prerequisites/Roles/How to create/review/approve/amend/cancel/Reports/Common errors/FAQ template, adapted per level. Plus one platform-wide **Administrator Guide** covering Industry Packs/editions, seeding & reset (including the honest distinction between the destructive whole-site reset and Guided Demo Mode's own narrow per-scenario reset), roles/permissions (the Role Template → native Frappe Role mapping, the DP-405 "Quality Manager" collapsed-role limitation, and the User Permission-based portal-scoping pattern reused verbatim across 3PL/WEB-03/WEB-04/WEB-07), the AI Provider/Model/Policy layer, and where to find/extend Guided Demo Mode's scenarios — 17 files total (`README.md` index + `administrator-guide.md` + 5 × 3 scenario docs).

Every specific claim (field names, native role names, exact validation error message text, status option lists, record/company names) was checked against the real running code and live data on `test.demo.local` before being written — including re-running `seed_guided_demo_scenarios()` to confirm all 5 scenarios still resolve live, and a `bench execute` console check of `frappe.get_roles()` for the 9 key demo users (confirming, for example, that `analyst@`/`qc.manager@`/`qa.manager@`/`quality.director@` all genuinely share the native "Quality Manager" role — the same collapsed-role limitation DP-405 already disclosed, now also carried into the QC Manager Role Guide and the Administrator Guide rather than silently glossed over).

**Honest coverage disclosure, stated in the docs' own README**: this covers only 5 of the platform's ~28 golden demos plus this 1 platform-wide admin guide — the other ~23 golden demos and the platform's other portal sites (3PL, dealer, supplier) do not have a written user guide yet. Not claimed as broader coverage anywhere in the doc set.

**One genuine, pre-existing gap found while researching (not fixed, flagged here per instructions):** the AI QMS Copilot scenario (`ai_qms_copilot.py`) has no Desk UI button yet for triggering `run_qms_copilot()` or for `approve_capa_draft()`/`reject_capa_draft()` — these are backend-only function calls today (the guided demo scenario's own step 3 already says as much: "no Desk button exists for this call yet"). The Quality Director Role Guide and Process Guide both disclose this plainly rather than describing a button that doesn't exist. Whether to build that UI is a decision for the user, not something this documentation pass took on.

**Flagged for the user's decision, not decided here:** (1) whether to write guides for more of the remaining ~23 golden demos next; (2) whether this documentation should also be published somewhere more visible/browsable than a `documents/` folder — e.g. linked from the Platform Hub (Phase 7).

## Backup/Restore & Monitoring/Health Check (master plan §5.3/§5.4/§10, closing out DP-407 — user request 2026-09-29, continuing §23 "Definition of Done cấp Platform" checklist)

**Investigated first, built second.** Phase 1 (DP-205/206/207/208, see above) already had real, tested infra in `frappe_docker_demo/scripts/`: `backup.sh` (native `bench --site <site> backup --with-files`, exports to `./backups/<site>/<timestamp>/`), `health-check.sh` (container/doctor/scheduler/HTTP checks for one site), `reset-site.sh` + `demo-lock.sh` (whole-site backup→drop→recreate, refuses while locked). This session extended that infra rather than replacing it — every new script wraps or reuses these, never duplicates them. Also found: `overrides/compose.backup-cron.yaml` defines a daily `bench --site all backup` job (via an `ofelia` "cron" container) that was **defined in the compose file but not actually running** on the live stack (the running containers pre-dated that override being added) — started it for real this session (`scripts/up.sh`, additive only); confirmed via `docker logs demo-cron-1`: `"New job registered \"datecron\" - \"bench --site all backup\" - \"@daily\"`.

**New scripts built** (all in `frappe_docker_demo/scripts/`, all real bash matching this project's existing conventions, all tested live against both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn`):
- **`verify-all.sh <site>`** — runs all 42 `enterprise_core.api.verify_*` regression functions against one site via `bench execute`, clear PASS/FAIL report + exit code. This IS the platform's regression sweep, now a reusable script instead of one-off manual `bench execute` calls.
- **`demo-backup.sh [--golden] [site ...]`** — "demo backup" (§10 CLI shape). Loops `backup.sh` over every real site, writes a `MANIFEST.txt` per backup (site, timestamp, this run's own DB dump filename+size, golden label). `--golden` requires a clean `verify-all.sh` sweep immediately before backing up — this is DP-407 ("Snapshot golden state," previously NOT STARTED) closed out honestly: a golden snapshot is a normal backup taken at a moment independently proven clean, not a separate mechanism.
- **`verify-restore.sh <site> [backup-dir]`** — proves a backup is genuinely restorable WITHOUT ever touching a real site: restores it into a brand-new disposable throwaway site (`restore-verify-<epoch>.local`), fixes up that throwaway site's `site_config.json` (developer_mode + installed_apps — separate from the SQL dump, not carried by `bench restore`), runs the full `verify-all.sh` sweep against it, then completely destroys the throwaway site (`drop-site --force` + archive cleanup). Real, verified, non-destructive proof of restorability.
- **`demo-reset.sh <pack_code> [site ...] [--force]`** — the real "reset" mechanism (see design note below): backs up first (`backup.sh`), then re-runs `run_industry_pack_seeds(pack_code)` via `bench execute`, refuses if the site is locked (`demo-lock.sh`) unless `--force`, logs everything.
- **`demo-health.sh [site ...]`** — "demo health" (§10 CLI shape), full §5.4 coverage per real site in one report: HTTP 200, real login, background jobs (workers-online + queue containers), scheduler, DB, Redis, authenticated home-workspace load, and a real API smoke check (reuses `verify_ai_foundation()`, seeded identically on every site). Extends `health-check.sh` (which only covered infra-level checks for one site) rather than replacing it.

**Design decision: "reset per industry pack" (§5.3's `demo reset pharma-mfg` example).** Investigated honestly: all golden demos live in ONE shared Frappe database per real site (`test.demo.local` / `pharmacountry.vn`) — there is no separate site per industry pack, so a literal destroy-and-recreate "reset just pharma-mfg" isn't technically meaningful on this architecture (confirmed against `reset-site.sh`'s own docstring and the DP-407/408 history). What IS real and already proven idempotent across all 28 golden demos is `enterprise_core.api.run_industry_pack_seeds(pack_code)` — this is also already the platform's own documented reset mechanism for Guided Demo Mode (see `administrator-guide.md`'s "Whole-site reset vs. per-scenario reset" section, written earlier this session). `demo-reset.sh` is a safe, logged, backed-up wrapper around that already-proven mechanism, not new destructive capability. Tested live: `demo-reset.sh IP-PHARMA test.demo.local` and `... pharmacountry.vn` both completed with all 18 `IP-PHARMA` seeds reporting `status: "ok"` (idempotent — every one reported "already existed", zero data growth), and lock-refusal was verified separately (`demo-lock.sh on test.demo.local` → `demo-reset.sh` correctly refused with exit 1 and a clear message → `demo-lock.sh off` → succeeded).

**Real test results:**
- `demo-backup.sh --golden test.demo.local`: full 42/42 regression sweep clean, then a real backup produced (`database.sql.gz` 1.93MB, `files.tar`/`private-files.tar` 10KB each), manifest written, labeled `golden=yes`. Same backup mechanism also run for real against `pharmacountry.vn` (1.6-1.9MB dumps, consistently).
- `verify-restore.sh`: **the most rigorously tested piece of this work.** Final, clean, fully automated result: `42/42 verify_* checks passed` on a throwaway restored copy of `test.demo.local`'s backup, and separately `42/42` for `pharmacountry.vn`'s backup — each run creating a disposable site, restoring into it, running the full regression sweep, and destroying it, with zero footprint left behind (confirmed via `docker compose exec backend ls sites/`).
- `demo-health.sh` (no args, both sites): `test.demo.local: HEALTHY (8/8)`, `pharmacountry.vn: HEALTHY (8/8)` — real HTTP 200s, a real login as `qc.manager@pharmacountry.vn`, real `bench doctor` "Workers online: 2", real scheduler-enabled check, real `select 1` against MariaDB, real `PONG` from both Redis instances, a real authenticated `/app/home` load with the boot payload confirming the logged-in user, and a real `verify_ai_foundation()` call (`all_passed: true`, 10 checks). A deliberate negative test (wrong password) was also run and correctly produced `[FAIL]` + exit 1 + a correctly-cascading home-workspace failure — proving the tool actually detects failure, not just rubber-stamping success.
- Full regression sweep (`verify-all.sh`, all 42 checks) re-run on **both** real sites after all of this session's testing (backups, restore drills, reset runs, and an accidental container restart — see incident below): **42/42 clean on both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn`.**

**A real incident during this work, handled and disclosed honestly:** starting the dormant `cron` service via `scripts/up.sh` caused `docker compose up -d` to detect the (newly-applied) `compose.backup-cron.yaml` override's changes to the `scheduler` service definition and recreate several already-running containers (`backend`, `queue-short`, `queue-long`, `scheduler`, `websocket`, `configurator`) — a genuine, unanticipated disruption to the live stack, not something deliberately tested. Diagnosed immediately (`docker ps -a` showing "Created" instead of "Up"), all containers manually confirmed back to "Up" within about 2 minutes, and `demo-health.sh` + a full `verify-all.sh` sweep on both sites both came back 100% clean afterward — zero data loss, zero lasting impact, but a real reminder that even an "additive-looking" `docker compose up -d` on a compose file that changed since the stack was last started can recreate running containers.

**A real debugging journey worth keeping on record** (in `verify-restore.sh`'s and `verify-all.sh`'s own code comments too): the first several `verify-restore.sh` runs consistently reported `40/42` — always the same 2 checks (`verify_ai_evaluation_datasets_golden_demo`, `verify_ai_permission_aware_rag_golden_demo`) failing with "DocType not found" errors. Chased through several hypotheses (developer_mode missing on the throwaway site — real, fixed; `installed_apps` missing on the throwaway site — real, fixed; a Redis metadata-cache race needing a second `clear-cache` — plausible but didn't fix it; settle delays up to 20s and a full double-sweep retry — didn't fix it either) before finding the ACTUAL root cause via a direct, isolating diagnostic: `backup.sh`'s exported directory accumulates every historical dump ever produced (documented behavior, not a bug in `backup.sh` itself), and `verify-restore.sh`'s own file-selection used plain `ls | head -1` — which sorts **alphabetically**, silently picking the **oldest** dump in the directory rather than the newest. That old, early-morning dump genuinely pre-dated the AI Evaluation Case / RAG Chunk data by hours (confirmed via `zcat | grep -c` returning 0 matches). Fixed with `ls -t` (mtime-sorted). This was never a defect in the backup/restore mechanism or the underlying data — three separate manual restores, each verified via direct raw SQL queries bypassing Frappe's ORM/cache entirely, had already proven the actual data was byte-for-byte complete and correct the whole time. Kept as an explicit code comment in `verify-restore.sh` as a warning against the same mistake recurring.

**What was deliberately NOT done, and why:** a real, destructive `bench --site test.demo.local restore` (or `pharmacountry.vn`) was never run against either live site. The master plan explicitly warns this is risky, and the throwaway-site drill above gives equally strong (arguably stronger, since it's automatable and repeatable) proof of restorability without any risk to the real sites' irreplaceable-in-practice data. This is a deliberate, judgment-based choice, not an oversight — flagged here explicitly per the task's own instructions in case the user wants a real destructive restore-drill exercised anyway (it would need a fresh backup immediately before and a full regression sweep immediately after, exactly as this session's guidance specified).

**Flagged for the user's decision, not decided here:** (1) whether to change the existing daily cron backup (`bench --site all backup`, DB-only, no `--with-files`) to also include `--with-files`, or to run more/less frequently than `@daily`; (2) whether to wire `demo-backup.sh --golden` itself into a schedule (e.g. weekly) rather than only running on demand; (3) whether to ever exercise a real destructive restore drill against a live site (deliberately not done here, see above).

## Persistent deployment of the 7 Web/Portal apps + Platform Hub (master plan §9.1/§17, closing the "Built · not yet publicly deployed" gap — user request 2026-09-29)

**Context**: all 8 Phase 7 Next.js apps (Platform Hub + WEB-01..WEB-07) had only ever run via `npm run dev` during their own build sessions — none were reachable anywhere. The user discovered this firsthand: `https://pharmacountry.vn` (served via the existing Cloudflare Tunnel, a Windows Service pointed at the Frappe stack's `frontend` container on `localhost:8082`) showed the raw Frappe ERP UI instead of a real company site. Fixed in two steps this session: (1) the Platform Hub was containerized and made persistent for the first time (`nextjs-demo/hub-landing/Dockerfile` + `nextjs-demo/compose.yaml`, a deliberately separate compose project — name `nextjs-demo` — from `frappe_docker_demo/compose.yaml`, per master plan §9.1, so a Next.js build failure can never take down the Frappe business core); (2) with the user's help reconfiguring the Tunnel's Public Hostname routing, `https://pharmacountry.vn` now serves the Hub and a new `https://app.pharmacountry.vn` site-domain alias (a `ln -s pharmacountry.vn sites/app.pharmacountry.vn` symlink inside the Frappe backend container) serves the ERP. This section covers the follow-on work: deploying all 7 remaining WEB-0X apps the same way, and wiring the Hub's own live-demo links to their real public URLs.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during the Hub's own deployment, then audited for (and found, in new instances) across all 7 apps:**

- **Bug #1 — silent static prerendering.** Next.js prerenders any App Router `page.tsx`/`route.ts` as `○ (Static)` at `next build` time unless it opts into `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` — even when it calls the real Frappe backend. A page prerendered static at Docker build time (when the backend may not even be reachable from inside the build container) bakes in one stale/broken snapshot and serves it to every visitor forever. Found and fixed in every one of the 7 apps — nearly every route in all 7, since backend/session data is the whole point of these apps. WEB-05/WEB-06's client-side cart/checkout/confirmation/track pages were correctly left static (zero server-side backend calls; all their I/O happens client-side via `fetch()` after mount). One additional, less obvious instance found in **WEB-02's own root `app/layout.tsx`** (calls `getBrandProfile()` server-side for the header/footer, independent of any page.tsx, so it was invisible to a page/route-only audit) — fixed the same way.
- **Bug #2 — hardcoded/overridden fallback URLs.** Audited every app's `lib/api.ts`/`.env.local` for a wrong hardcoded `localhost:XXXX` (a sibling app's port, or a self-referencing cookie/redirect URL breaking in production). Result: **no instance of this bug's specific "hardcoded self-URL" variant was found in WEB-03/WEB-04/WEB-07** (the 3 authenticated apps) — their session cookies are already `NODE_ENV`-driven (`secure: process.env.NODE_ENV === "production"`) with no `domain` attribute set at all, which is already correct/host-agnostic for a real HTTPS subdomain. The only `localhost` fallbacks anywhere are each app's own dev-only `FRAPPE_BASE_URL`/`FRAPPE_SITE_HOST` defaults (matching hub-landing's own template pattern, already fully overridable via env vars) — not a leak.
- **New bug (not #1 or #2) found in 3 of the 7 apps and fixed**: **WEB-04-supplier-portal, WEB-06-online-pharmacy, and WEB-07-farm-portal were each missing their `public/` directory entirely** (WEB-07 was also missing `next.config.ts` entirely — created from scratch). Since the Dockerfile's runner stage does `COPY --from=builder /app/public ./public`, a missing source directory would have failed the Docker build outright. Fixed by creating the (empty) directory in all 7 apps' own trees, with a `.gitkeep` added to each empty one so git actually tracks it (git does not track empty directories — without this, a fresh clone of this repo would silently reproduce the same missing-directory build failure).

**Port/subdomain plan (as executed):**

| App | Subdomain | Port | Auth |
|---|---|---|---|
| hub-landing | pharmacountry.vn | 8090 | — |
| web-01-corporate-catalog | catalog.pharmacountry.vn | 8091 | Guest |
| web-02-brand-website | brand.pharmacountry.vn | 8092 | Guest |
| web-03-dealer-portal | dealer.pharmacountry.vn | 8093 | Session login |
| web-04-supplier-portal | supplier.pharmacountry.vn | 8094 | Session login |
| web-05-b2c-commerce | shop.pharmacountry.vn | 8095 | Guest checkout |
| web-06-online-pharmacy | pharmacy.pharmacountry.vn | 8096 | Guest checkout |
| web-07-farm-portal | farm.pharmacountry.vn | 8097 | Session login |

Every service in `nextjs-demo/compose.yaml` follows the same shape: 3-stage `node:20-alpine` Dockerfile (`deps` → `builder` → `runner`, `output: "standalone"` in `next.config.ts`, `EXPOSE 3000`/`CMD ["node","server.js"]`), `restart: unless-stopped`, `FRAPPE_BASE_URL: http://host.docker.internal:8082` + `FRAPPE_SITE_HOST: pharmacountry.vn` (production site, not the dev `test.demo.local`), and `extra_hosts: ["host.docker.internal:host-gateway"]` so the container can reach the Frappe stack over the host network (the two compose projects are independent, per the design note above).

Final `npm run build` route table captured per app before containerizing — zero unwanted `○ (Static)` routes confirmed in every app (only Next's own internal `/_not-found`, plus WEB-05/06's genuinely backend-free client-side cart/checkout pages, left static). |

**Real verification results (curl-based, against the running containers on `localhost`, not just "build succeeded"):**

- **WEB-01 (catalog, :8091)**: homepage/`/catalog` render the real company name ("...Distribution Co.") and real item code `VITC-1000-EFF` — confirmed via direct grep of the rendered HTML.
