- **WEB-05 (shop, :8095)**: `/` and `/shop` both render real catalog items `VITC-1000-EFF`/"Vitamin C 1000mg Effervescent Tablet" and `FACIAL-CLEANSER-150ML`/"Facial Cleanser 150ml Bottle" with live stock counts.
- **WEB-06 (pharmacy, :8096)**: `/` and `/shop` both render the real item `PARA-500-TAB`/"Paracetamol 500 mg Tablet" with real pricing data.
- **WEB-03 (dealer, :8093)** — real login-flow proof, not just HTTP 200: `POST /api/auth/login` with the real test credentials from the app's own README (`dealer.alpha.portal@pharmacountry.vn` / `Demo@1234`) returned `{"ok":true,"customerName":"WEB03 Portal Dealer Alpha"}` and set the `web03_dealer_session` cookie; `GET /dashboard` **without** that cookie correctly 307-redirected to `/login` (proving the gate is real, not decorative); `GET /dashboard` **with** the cookie returned 200 with the real dealer's name/data; a **second**, separate authenticated request (`GET /orders`, same cookie) also returned 200 with real per-account data, proving the session persists across multiple requests (the `globalThis`-pinned session Map fix, carried over from this app's own earlier build, survived containerization).
- **WEB-04 (supplier, :8094)** — same proof shape: login as `supplier.cargill.portal@pharmacountry.vn` / `Demo@1234` returned `{"ok":true,"supplierName":"Cargill Asia Trading Pte Ltd"}`, set `web04_supplier_session`, authenticated `/dashboard` returned 200 with real Cargill data.
- **WEB-07 (farm, :8097)** — same proof shape: login as `farm.alpha.portal@pharmacountry.vn` / `Demo@1234` returned `{"ok":true,"customerName":"WEB07 Portal Farm Alpha"}`, set `web07_farm_session`, authenticated `/dashboard` returned 200 with real Farm Alpha data.
- **Hub (:8090)** — after rebuilding/recreating with the 7 new `HUB_WEB0X_URL` env vars pointing at the real public subdomains (`https://catalog.pharmacountry.vn`, etc., not internal Docker addresses or localhost ports): both `/` and `/solutions` now render all 7 real subdomain URLs (confirmed via grep of the rendered HTML) and the stale "not yet publicly deployed" placeholder text no longer appears anywhere on either page (confirmed 0 matches).

**Deliberately NOT done here, flagged for the user rather than decided unilaterally**: Cloudflare Tunnel Public Hostname routing for the 7 new subdomains was intentionally left untouched — this session has no access to that configuration, and it's the parent session's job to hand the user a single batched list of the 7 entries needed (`catalog`/`brand`/`dealer`/`supplier`/`shop`/`pharmacy`/`farm`.pharmacountry.vn → their respective `http://localhost:809X`). Until those are added at the edge, the 7 subdomains the Hub now links to will not resolve publicly even though the containers themselves are already up, healthy, and verified reachable on their localhost ports. No real-browser/visual QA was performed on any of the 7 apps (same limitation flagged throughout Phase 7) — verification here is curl/HTTP-based empirical proof of real data and real session behavior, not rendered screenshots.

## Platform Hub rebrand — real PharmaCountry branding + bilingual VI/EN i18n + Contact→Lead pipeline (user request 2026-09-30)

**Context**: the user asked that `hub-landing` (the only app touched in this section — WEB-01..07 were explicitly out of scope) stop looking like "a platform" and instead read as a real PharmaCountry software-company website: the real brand (logo/colors from the sibling `Quantri-pharmacountry/fe` project, copied into `hub-landing/brand-assets/` before this session started), rewritten company-voiced copy, a full page set (About/Contact), bilingual Vietnamese/English content defaulting to Vietnamese for Vietnam-based visitors, and real image prompts (no image-generation tool exists in this environment, so prompts were delivered instead of generated files).

**Brand assets reused, not reinvented**: `brand-assets/CustomLogoFooter.jsx` (the real diamond-mark + "PharmaCountry" wordmark lockup, real gradient stops `#3DBB89` → `#158A57` → `#0A4A2D`, accent `#1FA76B`) and `brand-assets/bimi-logo.svg` (the real compact "PMCT" icon mark) were read in full and ported into a new `components/PharmaCountryLogo.tsx` (`PharmaCountryMark` for compact/mobile spaces, `PharmaCountryWordmark` for the main header/footer) — same shapes/colors as the sibling project's own design work, not a new logo invented for this rebrand. The earlier teal/cyan colorway files (`CustomLogo.jsx`/`CustomLogo2.jsx`) were read but deliberately not used (superseded by the footer file's more refined green palette). The real brand green (`#158A57` family) replaced the old amber/slate/emerald mix as the site's accent color throughout `SiteHeader.tsx`, `SiteFooter.tsx`, and both `app/[locale]/page.tsx`/`app/[locale]/solutions/page.tsx` (buttons, links, the "live" badge color, hero accent) — category icon backgrounds in `lib/industryIcons.tsx` were deliberately left with their existing varied colors, per the user's own "can keep varied colors" allowance.

**Positioning**: the Hub is now framed as **"PharmaCountry Enterprise Solutions"** — explicitly PharmaCountry's own enterprise-software/ERP technology division, honestly distinguished from (and non-contradictory with) `PharmaCountry.com`'s real, separate public positioning as a B2B pharmaceutical marketplace (100+ countries, 262K+ products, 33K+ companies — referenced, never claimed to be built by this Hub). All copy was rewritten from "demo platform" framing to real-company framing without fabricating unverifiable business facts (no invented revenue, headcount, founding date, or customer testimonials) — the About page (`app/[locale]/about/page.tsx`) explicitly discloses that the underlying seed data is illustrative/demo data while asserting, honestly, that the software itself is real, working code. **Flagged for the user, not decided unilaterally**: the exact sub-brand phrasing ("PharmaCountry Enterprise Solutions") and homepage headline/tagline wording are this session's own judgment calls, not confirmed brand copy from the user — worth a quick sign-off pass.

**Bilingual i18n (`next-intl`)**: installed and wired per its official Next.js App Router pattern — routes restructured under `app/[locale]/` (`always`-prefixed `/vi/...` and `/en/...`), `messages/vi.json`/`messages/en.json` hold every real static-copy string for home/solutions/about/contact/nav/footer (no inline ternaries). **Locale detection (`middleware.ts`)**: real `CF-IPCountry` header first (confirmed empirically live against `https://pharmacountry.vn` via a temporary diagnostic route — Cloudflare Tunnel DOES forward a real `cf-ipcountry: VN` header to the origin container, alongside `cf-connecting-ip`/`cf-ray`/etc. — not assumed without checking, and the diagnostic route was removed/neutered immediately after confirming), `VN` → `vi`, anything else → `en`; falls back to `Accept-Language` if `CF-IPCountry` is ever absent (e.g. a direct localhost request that never went through Cloudflare); defaults to `vi` if genuinely ambiguous, matching the user's own explicit preference. A manual VI/EN toggle (`components/LocaleSwitcher.tsx`) in the header always overrides the auto-detected locale. **Honest limitation, not silently glossed over**: `industry_category`/`pack_name`/`summary` on the Solutions page come live from the real backend registry (`get_industry_solutions()`) and are authored in English only in the backend seed data — this rebrand translates every piece of static page chrome around them, but does not (and structurally cannot, without a backend content-translation project of its own) translate that backend-sourced text; the Solutions page itself now says so explicitly (`solutions.languageNote`, rendered on the page).

**Old top-level `app/page.tsx`/`app/about/page.tsx`/`app/solutions/page.tsx`/`app/layout.tsx`**: this environment's `nextjs-demo` tree has no git repository, so destructive deletion was avoided; instead these files were overwritten in place — the 3 page files are now thin `redirect()` stubs (dead code in practice, since `middleware.ts`'s matcher already redirects every un-prefixed path to `/vi/...`/`/en/...` before Next.js routing ever reaches them) and `app/layout.tsx` is now a minimal true-root shell (owns `<html>`/`<body>`/fonts only) with the real header/footer/`NextIntlClientProvider` moved one level down into the new `app/[locale]/layout.tsx`, which cannot itself own `<html>` (nested layout). A small client component (`components/HtmlLangSync.tsx`) corrects `document.documentElement.lang` to the real locale on mount, since the true root layout can't know it server-side.

**New Contact → Lead pipeline (real ERPNext CRM write)**: `app/[locale]/contact/page.tsx` + `components/ContactForm.tsx` (client form) submit to a new same-origin Route Handler, `app/api/contact/route.ts` (needed because a browser `<form>` can never itself set the `Host` header this app's Frappe client requires — see `lib/api.ts`'s own long-standing comment on that WHATWG Fetch restriction), which calls a new `lib/api.ts` export, `submitContactLead()`, extended from a GET-only client to also support a real POST + JSON body (same shape as WEB-05's own `b2c_commerce_api.ts` client). The real backend endpoint, `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/public_api.py`'s new `submit_contact_lead()` (`allow_guest=True`, `methods=["POST"]`), creates a real ERPNext `Lead` document (CRM module) — **security pattern deliberately reused, not reinvented**, identical to `b2c_commerce_api.py`'s `place_web_order()`: exactly 4 fields ever read from the request (`full_name`/`email`/`company`/`message`, each length/shape-validated before anything is written), a narrow `frappe.set_user("Administrator")` elevation scoped to only the single `lead.insert()` call and restored in a `finally` block (Guest has no create permission on `Lead`, same real permissions gap WEB-05's own docstring documents for `Sales Order`/`Address`), and no enumerable Lead `name`/id ever returned to the caller (response is just `{"success": true}`). **Lead field choices verified live, not guessed**: `bench execute frappe.get_meta` against `pharmacountry.vn` confirmed `status` is the only DocType-level `reqd=1` field (set to the real, valid option `"Lead"`), and that Lead has no simple free-text message field — the visitor's message is appended as a real `CRM Note` child-table row (`note`/`added_by`/`added_on` fields, confirmed live) rather than stuffed into an unrelated field. A new `verify_hub_contact_lead_access_control()` was added to `public_api.py`, matching every other guest-writable endpoint's own empirical-proof convention (field-injection resistance, validation-rejection checks) — **7/7 checks passed** live against `pharmacountry.vn`.

**End-to-end verification performed, not just unit-level**: (1) `verify_hub_contact_lead_access_control()` run via `bench execute` against `pharmacountry.vn` — 7/7 passed; (2) a raw `curl -X POST` directly against the Frappe HTTP endpoint (bypassing the frontend entirely, same Host-header mechanism the frontend's own `lib/api.ts` uses) — real `Lead` created (`CRM-LEAD-2026-00002`), confirmed via direct DB query to contain exactly the submitted `lead_name`/`company_name`/`email_id`/`status`, nothing extra; (3) a `curl -X POST` against the **live, deployed, public** `https://pharmacountry.vn/api/contact` (the real browser-facing path: Next.js container → `lib/api.ts` → Frappe) — real `Lead` created (`production.e2e.test@example.com`), confirmed via direct DB query. All three levels of the real pipeline (backend function → HTTP endpoint → deployed frontend route) were independently proven, not assumed from one passing level.

**Regression sweep after the `public_api.py` change**: all ~41 `verify_*` functions in `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/api.py` were re-run live against `pharmacountry.vn` via `bench execute`. **40/41 passed clean.** The one failure, `verify_online_pharmacy_demo()`, was investigated with a temporary diagnostic wrapper (added, used once, then fully reverted — `api.py` confirmed back to its exact original line count/content afterward) that surfaced the REAL underlying error bench's own CLI error-handling was silently masking behind an unrelated `NameError`: `frappe.exceptions.ValidationError: POS Opening Entry - POS-OPE-2026-00003 is outdated. Please close the POS and create a new POS Opening Entry.`, raised from deep inside ERPNext's own `sales_invoice.py` POS validation, via `online_pharmacy_api.py`'s `place_pharmacy_order()` — a date/calendar-day-dependent staleness check on a POS Opening Entry created during an earlier session, entirely unrelated to this rebrand's `public_api.py` changes (no import/call relationship between the two modules at all; `verify_pharmacy_golden_demo()`, the underlying Golden Demo #23 check WEB-06 reuses, was independently re-run and passed clean on its own). **Flagged for the user/parent session, not fixed here** (out of scope for this rebrand task): WEB-06's demo setup appears to need its POS Opening Entry re-created periodically (likely daily) for its own regression check to stay green — a pre-existing operational gap, not a defect introduced by this session's work.

**Image prompts (no image-generation tool available in this environment)**: `nextjs-demo/hub-landing/IMAGE_PROMPTS.md` has 3 ready-to-paste prompts (homepage hero banner 16:9, About-page team/technology image 4:3, an optional multi-industry-breadth illustration 3:2), each specifying subject/mood/style/aspect-ratio/negative-prompts, referencing the real brand palette. Every image placement in the actual pages is a CSS-only gradient/icon placeholder (brand-green gradient, no broken `<img>` tags), each marked with a `{/* TODO: replace with real ... image ... */}` comment pointing at the matching prompt — real generated images need a follow-up pass to embed once the user runs these prompts through their own tool.

**Build/deploy verification**: `npm run build` — zero unwanted static routes (`/[locale]`, `/[locale]/solutions`, `/[locale]/contact`, `/api/contact` all correctly `ƒ (Dynamic)`; `/[locale]/about` is `● (SSG)`, correctly static since it has no per-request backend call). `npm run lint` clean. Docker image rebuilt (`docker compose build hub-landing`) and redeployed (`docker compose up -d hub-landing`) from `nextjs-demo/`. Live-verified against `https://pharmacountry.vn`: root redirects to `/vi` (no `CF-IPCountry` override, real Vietnam-based traffic); `CF-IPCountry: VN`/`US` headers redirect to `/vi`/`/en` respectively; `/vi`, `/en`, `/vi/about`, `/en/solutions`, `/vi/contact` all render the correct bilingual `<title>`/copy/real brand green (`#158A57`) throughout the rendered HTML. Not done, deliberately out of scope: migrating `middleware.ts` to Next.js 16's newer `proxy.ts` convention (a deprecation warning only, not a build error; the codemod tool itself refused to run without a git repository to fall back on, which this `nextjs-demo` tree doesn't have) — safe to defer, flagged here for whenever this tree does get git-initialized.

## Platform Hub — per-Industry-Pack detail pages, `/solutions/[packCode]` (user request 2026-09-30)

**Context**: after the rebrand above, the user looked at the live `/solutions` page and said it felt unfinished — of the 27 real Industry Packs, only 7 render as "Live Demo" cards linking to a real dedicated subdomain; the other ~26 rendered as a plain, non-clickable "ERP System Demo" (or "Not Yet Built", for `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL`) card with just a one-line summary. The user explicitly did NOT want 26 more standalone Next.js sub-sites built (disproportionate infrastructure for internal-ERP-accessed verticals) — instead: one reusable "industry detail" page template inside this SAME `hub-landing` app, with genuinely richer REAL per-pack content, and every card (including the 7 "Live Demo" ones) clickable through to it.

**Data-model investigation first (`bench execute` against `pharmacountry.vn`, read-only)**, to decide honestly what's real vs. what would need to be curated:
- `Industry Pack.default_edition` → `Edition.capability_engines` → `Capability Engine`: confirmed this chain is populated for **exactly 1 of 27 packs** (`IP-PHARMA` → `PHARMA_MFG_STARTER`, 9 of the 15 real Capability Engine records). All other 26 packs have a blank `default_edition` — there is only ONE real `Edition` record in the entire system. **Decision: never fabricate a plausible-looking engine list for the other 26 — the new detail page simply omits that section for them**, rather than guessing.
- `Industry Pack.seeds` (`Industry Pack Seed` child rows → `Seed Template`): populated for all 26 packs with a golden demo (confirmed live), giving a real, ordered list of seed step names (e.g. "QMS Deviation -> CAPA -> Closure (DP-529)") — every `Seed Template.description` field is empty in the live data, so only the template's own human name (which usually embeds a real internal ticket reference) is available; no separate prose exists here. `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL` independently reconfirmed to have **zero** `Industry Pack Seed` rows (direct child-table query, not inferred from the boolean the list endpoint already exposed).
- `documents/project_status.md`'s own Phase 3-6 Golden Demo write-ups and the master plan's DEMO 01-32 sections: confirmed to contain real, already-written, per-golden-demo test IDs (Q01-Q08, D01-D07, L01-L08, PF01-PF08, MD01-MD07, RX01-RX07, W01-W06, CD01-CD06, PM01-PM07, FT01-FT07, MP01-MP07, VPM01-07/VD01-07, SF01-10, etc.), short real descriptions of what each proves, real business-flow narratives, and real documented bugs found/fixed during each demo's build — rich enough to need no invention at all for content.

**Mechanism chosen**: a hybrid, not a single approach —
1. **New backend endpoint**, `public_api.py`'s `get_industry_pack_detail(pack_code)` (`allow_guest=True`, GET, same field-allow-list discipline as `get_industry_solutions()`) — returns the pack's existing summary/live-demo fields PLUS two genuinely LIVE, per-request-computed fields: `seed_steps` (the real ordered `Industry Pack Seed` → `Seed Template` list, human label + seed_type only — never `seed_function`/`template_code`/`is_required`) and `capability_engines` (real `Edition.capability_engines` → `Capability Engine` resolution, empty list when `default_edition` isn't set, never guessed). This deliberately revisits `get_industry_solutions()`'s own module docstring, which withheld `Industry Pack.seeds` child-row content from the LIST endpoint as "internal wiring" — that reasoning still holds for a 27-row bulk listing, but not for a single pack's own detail page whose entire purpose is showing a visitor what real engineering work built it (documented explicitly in the new code's own comments).
2. **Curated frontend content file**, `hub-landing/lib/industryPackContent.ts` — a reviewed, checked-into-the-repo TypeScript extract of `project_status.md`/the master plan's real per-golden-demo content (test IDs, real descriptions, business-flow narrative, real bugs fixed, demo company name where documented), covering **all 26 of the 26 packs that have a golden demo** (comprehensive, not a token subset) — deliberately NOT re-parsed live from the markdown file at runtime (static engineering documentation, not transactional data; avoids the Docker build-time-vs-runtime markdown-path fragility this app's own history has already hit twice). A handful of test IDs that the master plan documents as intended scope but which `project_status.md`'s own write-up doesn't separately confirm as built-and-verified for that specific demo (e.g. `IP-PHARMA`'s P02/P03/P04/P07-P10, `IP-VETERINARY`'s VPM05) are marked with an explicit "spec'd, not separately confirmed" flag on the page rather than silently listed as equally proven.

**New page**: `app/[locale]/solutions/[packCode]/page.tsx` — `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` (calls the live backend every request, same reasoning as `page.tsx`/`solutions/page.tsx`; deliberately NOT using `generateStaticParams`, since this is genuinely live per-request data, not a static content page). Renders, per pack: category/name/summary; for "Live Demo" packs, the external site link(s) as a clearly-labeled secondary "Live public site" section (never the page's only content); for the 26 golden-demo packs, "What this demo proves" (real test IDs + descriptions, unconfirmed ones flagged), "Business flow" (real narrative), "Real issues found while building this demo" (real bugs, when documented), "Real seed data steps" (live `seed_steps`), and "Capability engines this pack uses" (only rendered when `capability_engines` is genuinely non-empty — true today only for `IP-PHARMA`); for `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL`, an honest "Not yet built" note (zero seed data, confirmed live) that also surfaces the master plan's own phase-1-scoped, never-built VB01-VB06 intent rather than staying silent about it. A content-language disclosure note (`solutionDetail.contentLanguageNote`, bilingual chrome, English content) mirrors the existing Solutions-page `languageNote` pattern for the same honest reason: this real content is sourced from English-language internal engineering documentation.

**`/solutions` listing page updated**: every card's primary click target is now the new detail page (`Link` wrapping the icon/badge/summary/"View full details" CTA) for all three card types (Live Demo / ERP System Demo / Not Yet Built) — including the 7 Live Demo cards, whose direct external site link(s) are demoted to a secondary action rendered as sibling `<a>` elements below the primary `Link` inside the same card (HTML forbids nesting an `<a>` inside another `<a>`), never removed.

**Bilingual**: all of this page's own static chrome (16 new keys under `solutionDetail` in both `messages/en.json`/`messages/vi.json` — headings, the seed-type labels, the not-found state, the content-language disclosure) is fully translated; the real sourced content itself (test descriptions, business-flow narrative, bug write-ups) stays English-only, honestly disclosed on-page, per the existing precedent.

**Build/deploy verification**: `npm run build` — `/[locale]/solutions/[packCode]` correctly `ƒ (Dynamic)`, same as `/[locale]` and `/[locale]/solutions`; zero accidental static routes. `npm run lint` clean. Backend change deployed by copying the updated `public_api.py` into the running `backend` container and restarting it (this environment's `backend` service bakes the Frappe app into the container's own writable layer rather than bind-mounting the host repo — confirmed via `readlink`/direct inspection — so a plain host-file edit alone would not have gone live). Frontend rebuilt (`docker compose build hub-landing`) and redeployed (`docker compose up -d hub-landing`) from `nextjs-demo/`. **Live-verified against `https://pharmacountry.vn`**: `get_industry_pack_detail` returns real, correct `seed_steps`/`capability_engines` for `IP-PHARMA` (19 seed steps, 9 real capability engines), `IP-QMS` (7 seed steps, empty capability engines — confirmed NOT fabricated), and `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL` (0/0, confirmed); `/vi/solutions/IP-QMS`, `/en/solutions/IP-CONSUMER-DIST` (Live Demo pack — confirmed both the real CD01-CD06 narrative AND all 3 real external WEB-01/03/05 links render), `/en/solutions/IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL` (confirmed the honest not-built note + VB01-VB06/phase-1 reference render), and `/vi/solutions/IP-DOES-NOT-EXIST` (confirmed the inline not-found state renders, HTTP 200, no crash) all verified live via curl/grep of the actual rendered HTML — not just HTTP 200 checks. The `/vi/solutions` listing page confirmed to now emit `href="/vi/solutions/<pack_code>"` for all 27 packs.

**Regression sweep after the `public_api.py` change**: all 42 `verify_*` functions in `enterprise_core/enterprise_core/enterprise_core/api.py` re-run live against `pharmacountry.vn` via `bench execute`. **41/42 passed clean.** The one failure, `verify_online_pharmacy_demo()`, is the SAME pre-existing, already-documented issue from the rebrand entry above (`POS Opening Entry ... is outdated`, a date/calendar-day-dependent staleness check deep inside ERPNext's own POS validation, reached via `online_pharmacy_api.py`, a module this change never touched) — recurring exactly as that earlier entry predicted ("needs its POS Opening Entry re-created periodically"), not a regression introduced by this work. `bench execute`'s own CLI also masked the real exception behind an unrelated `NameError: name 'enterprise_core' is not defined` on repeat invocations (its `except Exception: eval(method)` fallback path) — the real root cause was confirmed from the first invocation's own full traceback before it got masked.

**Not done / flagged for the user**: no real product photography/screenshots exist anywhere in this platform's seed data (same limitation `lib/industryIcons.tsx` already documents) — the detail page's only visual identity per pack is its category icon, same as the listing cards. The Capability-Engine section only ever has real content for 1 of 27 packs today; if the user wants this to be a richer feature platform-wide, the underlying `Industry Pack.default_edition`/`Edition` records would need to actually be populated for the other 26 packs first (a data-entry task, not a frontend one) — this session did not fabricate that data to make the section look more complete.

## P0 follow-up: public-facing UOM display + a last contradictory wording instance (user request 2026-09-30, closing out an external reviewer's finding)

After the previous P0 agent fixed `VITC-1000-EFF`'s actual price/UOM data (Item's real `stock_uom` stays `Kg` — blocked from a direct change by real Stock Ledger Entry history across 2 companies, per ERPNext's own `check_stock_uom_with_bin()` — instead added a real `sales_uom = "Tube"` + `UOM Conversion Detail` + re-priced `Item Price` rows), the public-facing API layer was still reading `item.stock_uom` directly for its `"uom"` display field in 4 places — so a visitor saw the now-correct price (55,000 VND) rendered as **"55,000 ₫ / Kg"**, arguably more confusing than the original bug. Fixed `_serialize_item()`/`_serialize_catalog_item()` in `public_api.py` (WEB-01), `b2c_commerce_api.py` (WEB-05), `dealer_portal_api.py` (WEB-03), and `online_pharmacy_api.py` (WEB-06) to read `item.sales_uom or item.stock_uom` instead — WEB-05's `_available_qty()` was also updated to convert `Bin.actual_qty` (always in `stock_uom` terms) into the display UOM using the Item's own real `UOM Conversion Detail.conversion_factor` when the two differ, rather than showing a raw Kg-equivalent number next to a per-Tube price. Live-verified on `pharmacountry.vn`: the shop page now reads "55,000 ₫ / Tube" with no remaining "/ Kg".

Also fixed the one wording-standardization instance the previous P0 agent found but correctly flagged as out of its own scope: `online_pharmacy_api.py`'s `_STOREFRONT_COPY["PARA-500-TAB"]["blurb"]` (presentation-only copy, not `Item.description` — the previous agent's guess about where this text lived was corrected during this fix) said "the same **real** store this chain's own point-of-sale sells from in person," contradicting the platform's own disclosed demo-data framing. Reworded to "the same store this chain's own point-of-sale sells from in person, live demo data generated and managed by the connected ERP system," consistent with the wording standardization already applied to WEB-01/02/05/06.

**Deployment note**: `enterprise_core`'s backend code is baked into the `demo-backend-1` container's own filesystem layer (via `bench get-app` at image-build/site-install time), NOT bind-mounted from the host — editing files on the host alone does not take effect. Applied via `docker cp` into the running container (both `test.demo.local` and `pharmacountry.vn` share the SAME `backend` container/bench installation, so one copy+restart updates both sites at once) followed by `docker compose -p demo restart backend`. Re-verified via `verify_b2c_commerce_demo()` (which itself re-runs `verify_consumer_dist_golden_demo()` twice for idempotency) on both sites: `all_passed: true` on both, zero regressions.

## Platform Hub — P1 positioning/UX pass (user-approved external reviewer feedback, 2026-09-30)

**Context**: after P0 (data/UOM bug + wording standardization + portal login access, see the section above), the same external reviewer's remaining feedback was a positioning/content-hierarchy pass on `hub-landing`, user-approved in full. Frontend-only — no backend/Python changes were needed (client-side filtering over the already-fetched 27-pack list).

**Issue #1 — pharma-centric homepage headline fixed**: `messages/{vi,en}.json`'s `home.title`/`home.lead` (and `footer.tagline`, for on-page consistency) rewritten to lead with the platform's real multi-industry breadth instead of over-indexing on "pharma". New EN headline: *"Enterprise software for regulated manufacturing, distribution & farm operations."* New EN subheadline: *"ERP, manufacturing, quality, laboratory, supply chain, and farm management — built as one connected, working system for pharmaceutical, healthcare, veterinary, animal feed, livestock, and aquaculture businesses. Every solution below is a real backend running today, not a mockup."* Real Vietnamese versions authored (not machine-translated verbatim): *"Phần mềm doanh nghiệp cho sản xuất, phân phối và vận hành trang trại trong các ngành quản lý chặt chẽ."* / *"ERP, sản xuất, chất lượng, phòng thí nghiệm, chuỗi cung ứng và quản lý trang trại — được xây dựng thành một hệ thống liền mạch, thực tế, phục vụ các doanh nghiệp dược phẩm, y tế, thú y, thức ăn chăn nuôi, chăn nuôi và thủy sản."* **Flagged for the user, same as the earlier rebrand's sub-brand phrasing**: exact headline wording is this session's own judgment call, worth a quick sign-off.

**Issue #2 — homepage CTA structure**: `app/[locale]/page.tsx`'s hero now leads with **"Explore Live Demos"** (primary, `href="#live-demos"` — a same-page anchor scrolling straight to the existing "Live Product Sites" section, `scroll-mt-16` added for sticky-header offset) rather than a generic "Explore Industries & Solutions" catalog link. **"Request a Demo"** (secondary) links to the EXISTING `/contact` page (real Contact → ERPNext CRM Lead pipeline, built in the earlier rebrand — no new/duplicate contact mechanism built) with `?intent=demo-request` appended for attribution. "About This Platform" kept as a tertiary, visually de-emphasized text link (plain underline, no button chrome) — also already in `SiteHeader.tsx`'s nav.

**Issue #4 — Operation/Software filtering added to `/solutions`**: new `lib/industryPackTags.ts` curates two tag dimensions per pack — `operations` (11 keys: manufacturing, quality, laboratory, documentControl, assetMaintenance, farm, processing, distribution, retail, warehousing, trading) and `software` (9 keys: erp, qms, dms, lims, eamCmms, wms, farmManagement, crm, pos) — for all 27 real Industry Packs (26 with a golden demo + `IP-VETERINARY-BIOLOGICAL`). **Grounded, not guessed**: every tag is derived directly from `industryPackContent.ts`'s own real `businessFlowSummary`/`testDescriptions` for that pack (e.g. `IP-QMS` → operation "quality" + software "qms", the standalone quality system it actually is; `IP-PHARMA` → operations "manufacturing"+"quality"+"distribution" + software "erp"+"qms"+"wms", reflecting Golden Demo #1's manufacturing/QC flow AND Golden Demo #2's real FEFO/credit-limit distribution extension; `IP-PIG-FARM`-family packs → operation "farm" + software "farmManagement", since their real flows never reach manufacturing or a customer). New client component `components/SolutionsExplorer.tsx` ("use client") renders 3 independent pill-based filter rows (Industry/Operation/Software, each defaulting to "All", combinable — a visitor can narrow by all three simultaneously) plus a live "Showing N solutions" count and a "Clear filters" action, filtering client-side over the already-fetched 27-pack list the server component (`app/[locale]/solutions/page.tsx`) passes down as plain serializable props (deliberately no import of `lib/api.ts` into the client bundle — that file uses Node's `http` module for the Frappe Host-header workaround and must never ship to the browser). All filter/tag labels bilingual (`solutions.filters.*`/`solutions.operations.*`/`solutions.software.*` in both `messages/*.json`).

**Issue #3 — Solutions cards redesigned business-first**: each card now leads with the category eyebrow + plain-English `pack_name` + one-line `summary`, followed by the new Operation/Software tag pills, a status indicator (kept the existing 3-tier Live Demo/ERP System Demo/Not Yet Built badge — more informative than a single generic "Verified" tag, reviewer's example was illustrative), and two clear actions ("Explore Solution" → the detail page, "Request Demo" → `/contact?intent=demo-request&pack=<code>`). The `pack_code` (e.g. "IP-QMS") is demoted from its own inline paragraph to a small `font-mono text-[10px] text-slate-300` footer line at the bottom of the card — present for reference, but no longer competing visually with the business content above it.

**Issue #3 (detail page) — technical identifiers de-emphasized on `/solutions/[packCode]`**: `pack_code` removed from directly under the `<h1>` entirely; combined with the golden-demo label (e.g. "Golden Demo #1 & #2") into a single small monospace footer line at the very bottom of the page. The "What this demo proves" section (business-facing, primary) now shows only the plain-English test descriptions — the raw test-ID codes (P01, Q01, etc.) and the "unconfirmed/spec'd" flag were removed from this level. A new closed-by-default `<details>` section, **"Engineering Evidence"** (`FlaskConical` icon, chevron that rotates on open via `group-open:rotate-180`), holds everything a technical reviewer (CTO/QA/IT) actually wants: the golden-demo label + demo company name, the full test-ID-to-description mapping (with the "spec'd, not separately confirmed" flag restored here), the real seed-data steps, and the capability-engine list — never shown at the same priority as the business content above it.

**Issue #5 — "issues found" content collapsed, not deleted**: the existing `notableBugsFixed` content (real, documented bugs — e.g. IP-PHARMA's packaging-quantity misconfiguration, IP-SHRIMP's unrealistic FCR, IP-MEDICAL-DEVICE's partial-component-transfer bug) is now inside a closed-by-default `<details>` using the existing `solutionDetail.bugsHeading` string ("Real issues found while building this demo") as its `<summary>`, positioned after the business-facing "What this demo proves"/"Business flow" sections — nothing deleted, only collapsed and repositioned per the reviewer's explicit instruction.

**Build/deploy verification**: `npm run build` route table unchanged and correct — `/[locale]`, `/[locale]/solutions`, `/[locale]/solutions/[packCode]`, `/[locale]/contact` all `ƒ (Dynamic)`; `/[locale]/about` still `● (SSG)`; zero accidentally-static routes. `npm run lint` clean. Docker image rebuilt (`docker compose build hub-landing`) and redeployed (`docker compose up -d hub-landing`) from `nextjs-demo/`. **Live-verified against `https://pharmacountry.vn`** via curl/grep of the actual rendered HTML (not just HTTP 200 checks): `/en` and `/vi` homepages render the new headline/subheadline and all 3 CTAs with correct `href`s (`#live-demos`, `/en/contact?intent=demo-request`, `/about`); `/vi/solutions` and `/en/solutions` render the 3 filter rows with real bilingual Operation/Software pill labels (`Sản xuất`/`Manufacturing`, `Chất lượng`/`Quality`, `Quản lý trang trại`/`Farm Management`, `QMS`, `ERP`, etc.) and a correct "Showing 27 solutions" count; `IP-PHARMA`/`IP-QMS`/`IP-SHRIMP` detail pages confirmed via curl: no `pack_code` immediately under the `<h1>`, an `Engineering Evidence` `<details>` present with no `open` attribute (closed by default), the bugs `<details>` also closed by default, the footer monospace line combining `pack_code` + golden-demo label present, and — checked programmatically — zero occurrences of the `P01` test-ID badge between the "What this demo proves" and "Business flow" headings (confirmed moved entirely into the Engineering Evidence section).

**Not done here (explicitly out of scope for this P1 pass)**: P2 items — richer product-catalog seeding across the 7 sub-apps, About-page enterprise-trust content, an AI & Automation homepage section, and full Vietnamese translation of the real sourced `industryPackContent.ts`/test-ID content itself (still honestly disclosed as English-only via the existing `solutionDetail.contentLanguageNote`/`solutions.languageNote` strings) — are separate, later tasks per the user's own scoping.

## Platform Hub — P2 content pass: Contact company info, About vendor profile, AI & Automation section (user-approved external reviewer feedback, final round, 2026-09-30)

**Context**: the last 3 items of the same external reviewer's feedback that produced the P0 (bug/wording fixes) and P1 (positioning/UX redesign) passes above. Frontend-only, `hub-landing` — no backend/Python changes. Scope was deliberately narrow: exactly the 3 items below, not the other P2 items (product-catalog seeding across the 7 sub-apps, full Vietnamese translation of `industryPackContent.ts`) which are separate, parallel tasks.

**Item #1 — Contact page Company Info**: reviewer's complaint was "Contact chỉ có form" (Contact is just a form). Added a real Company Info sidebar next to the existing `ContactForm` (`app/[locale]/contact/page.tsx`, now a `grid md:grid-cols-[1fr_320px]` layout): company name + one-line description, a real contact email (`hello@pharmacountry.vn` — this session's own judgment call, matching the `pharmacountry.vn` domain this Hub is deployed on; not tied to a fabricated individual's name), a location line ("Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam" — a general-region claim, deliberately NOT a fabricated street address), and a LinkedIn row shown as a plain label with no `href` and an honest "Company profile coming soon" note rather than a fabricated URL (no capability exists in this environment to look up or verify a real LinkedIn URL). Bilingual (`contact.companyInfo.*` in both `messages/*.json`).

**Item #2 — About page enterprise-vendor-profile sections**: reviewer's complaint was that About read like a project explainer, not an enterprise vendor profile. Added 6 new sections after the existing rebrand-era `p1`–`p4` paragraphs (left untouched — they already carry the PharmaCountry.com-vs-Enterprise-Solutions distinction, just given more visual structure via new subsection headings around them): **Company & Location** (Vietnam-based, same honest general-region framing as Contact); **Technology** (honestly: Frappe/ERPNext, Next.js for customer-facing apps, a real MariaDB/PostgreSQL-backed database — Frappe genuinely supports both, confirmed by reading `frappe_docker_demo/overrides/compose.mariadb*.yaml` and `compose.postgres.yaml` both existing as real supported deployment options — and Docker containers throughout); **Deployment Options** (Cloud/Dedicated/On-Premise, explicitly framed as options available to a real customer engagement — a standard, honest Frappe/ERPNext-vendor claim — not as something this one demo instance itself proves, which is called out explicitly in `deploymentNote`); **Implementation Approach** (5 generic, standard-practice steps — Discovery & Scoping → Configuration → Data Migration → Training → Go-Live — no invented timelines or client names); **Support** (support is part of every engagement, no fabricated SLA numbers or phone lines); **Security & Data Isolation** (grounded in real, verified platform behavior: Frappe's own role-based permission system, plus the empirically-verified per-account data isolation across the Dealer/Supplier/Farm portals — WEB-03/WEB-04/WEB-07 above — via Frappe's `User Permission` mechanism, checked by confirming two different portal logins see genuinely non-overlapping records rather than trusting a status flag; multi-tenant per-customer instance isolation noted as the real-deployment extension of the same principle). All 6 sections bilingual (`about.vendorProfile.*`).

**Item #3 — AI & Automation homepage section**: the platform's real Phase 6A AI work (see the Phase 6A section above — AI-DEMO-01 through Evaluation Datasets, all COMPLETE) was listed as 1 of 15 capability engines on the homepage but never explained. New section in `app/[locale]/page.tsx` (after "How our platform is built") lists 7 real capabilities, each with an honest one-line description grounded in what was actually built: **Executive Assistant** (`ask_enterprise()`'s 5 vetted, permission-scoped, cited real-data questions), **QMS Copilot** (deviation investigation/CAPA drafting, mandatory human approval via the shared `AI Draft` DocType, no-self-approval enforced), **DMS Copilot** (document revision comparison + knowledge search), **Manufacturing Insight** (production delay/yield anomaly analysis), **Procurement Assistant** (supplier quotation comparison/history, never auto-approves — human-gated, same `AI Draft` mechanism), **Livestock & Aquaculture Assistant** (AI-DEMO-09/10's FCR/mortality/pond-anomaly analysis), and **Permission-Aware Enterprise Search** (the real RAG chunk/index/retrieval pipeline, permission-scoped). Deliberately did NOT invent a separate "Inventory/Supply Chain" card — the near-expiry-inventory/batches-on-hold tools that would justify one are real, but they belong to the Executive Assistant's own tool set (AI-DEMO-01), not a distinct dedicated copilot, so folding them into that card was more accurate than fabricating a standalone capability.

**The central honesty decision (per the task's explicit hard constraint)**: `ai_core.py`'s own module docstring states `_call_provider_adapter()` is "a clearly-labeled MOCK: no live API keys exist in this demo environment" — confirmed by reading the function itself, which literally returns `f"[MOCK — no live provider call made] ..."`. What IS real and genuinely verified (per Phase 2A's own build notes and every Phase 6A item above): capability-based routing, tenant policy enforcement, fallback-on-failure between models, a governed tool registry, permission-scoped data access enforced via `frappe.get_list(user=...)` (never raw SQL), human-approval gates on anything that could become a real record, and full audit logging (`AI Job Log`) of every request. The homepage copy (`home.aiAutomation.honesty.*`) states this directly and once, in a single callout box above the capability cards, rather than a disclaimer repeated on every card: the architecture (routing/policy/tools/permissions/audit) is real and working; the language-model provider is genuinely provider-agnostic and designed to call a real provider (OpenAI/Anthropic Claude/Groq/a private self-hosted model) in a production deployment; but in this public demo specifically, the model's own response is a simulated one, so visitors can see exactly how permissions, approvals, and audit logging behave without live model calls or API keys in a public environment. No claim anywhere of a live/real language-model call happening in this demo. Bilingual (`home.aiAutomation.*`).

**One nesting bug found and fixed during this build**: the first edit of both `messages/en.json` and `messages/vi.json` placed the new `aiAutomation` object as a sibling of `home` at the JSON top level instead of nested inside it (an extra closing brace carried over from the existing `howBuilt` block's own closing). `JSON.parse()` succeeded either way (both are structurally valid JSON), so this wasn't caught by JSON validation or `npm run build`/`npm run lint` — it only surfaced when curl-verifying the actual live HTML, which rendered the literal fallback string `home.aiAutomation.heading` instead of translated text (next-intl's `getTranslations("home")` couldn't find `aiAutomation.*` inside the `home` namespace). Fixed by moving the closing/opening braces so `aiAutomation` sits inside `home` alongside `howBuilt`, re-verified by both a `node -e` structural check (`m.home.aiAutomation` truthy) and a second live curl pass confirming real English/Vietnamese text renders instead of key names.

**Build/deploy verification**: `npm run build` route table unchanged and correct — `/[locale]` and `/[locale]/contact` still `ƒ (Dynamic)` (no live-data dependency added to either, kept dynamic per this app's own established discipline anyway); `/[locale]/about` still `● (SSG)` (pure static content, no `force-dynamic` needed). `npm run lint` clean. One lucide-react import error caught by the build (`Linkedin` icon doesn't exist in the installed lucide-react version — brand icons were dropped; verified via `node -e` against the actual installed package before picking a fix), fixed by using the generic `Link2` icon instead. Docker image rebuilt (`docker compose build hub-landing`) and redeployed (`docker compose up -d hub-landing`) from `nextjs-demo/` — twice, once before and once after the JSON-nesting fix above. **Live-verified against `https://pharmacountry.vn`** via curl/grep of actual rendered HTML on both `/vi` and `/en` for all 3 pages: Contact's company email/location/LinkedIn-label render correctly bilingually; About's 6 new vendor-profile section headings (Company & Location, Technology, Deployment Options, Implementation Approach, Support, Security & Data Isolation) render bilingually with the real MariaDB/PostgreSQL and Dealer/Supplier/Farm-portal text present; the homepage AI & Automation section renders its heading, all 7 capability cards' titles, the 2 "Human approval required" badges (QMS Copilot, Procurement Assistant), the honesty-callout text (including the literal phrase "model response itself is simulated" / "mô phỏng (simulated)"), and the closing CTA link, on both locales. All other 7 `nextjs-demo` sibling services (`web-01`..`web-07`) confirmed still running unmodified (`docker compose ps`) throughout — only the `hub-landing` service was rebuilt/recreated.

**Not done here (explicitly out of scope, per the task's own instruction)**: the other 2 P2 items — richer product-catalog seeding across the 7 sub-apps, and full Vietnamese translation of `industryPackContent.ts`'s real sourced golden-demo content — are separate, parallel tasks handled elsewhere, not attempted here.

## Legend
Status values: `PLANNED`, `IN PROGRESS`, `CODE COMPLETE`, `MIGRATED`, `TESTED`, `UAT READY`, `DEMO READY`, `BLOCKED`. Never `DONE` for code-only completion.
